## FINAL ARCHITECTURE / LOCKPOINT REFERENCES

# FINAL 6-WEEK A1 ARCHITECTURE

Adult second language acquisition (SLA) is a highly complex, non-linear cognitive process governed by distinct neurobiological constraints, memory system interactions, and instructional variables. Under this optimized **6-Week Intensive German Curriculum** (designed for up to 4 hours of daily study), we leverage **Ullman's Declarative/Procedural (D/P) Model** and **Robert DeKeyser's Skill Acquisition Theory** to systematically transition learners from explicit declarative schemas to automatic, real-world spontaneous communication.

```
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                 REBASED A1 6-WEEK STRUCTURE                                     |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  PHASE 1: Conversational Foundations, V2 Syntax & Oral Tandem (Weeks 1-2)                       |
|  -> Focus: V2 word order (S-V-O default), tandem speaking/pronunciation, controlled writing     |
|                                                                                                 |
|  PHASE 2: Structural Mapping, Accusative Case & Separable Operations (Weeks 3-4)                 |
|  -> Focus: Present-tense, noun gender system, masculine direct-object shift, modal requests     |
|                                                                                                 |
|  PHASE 3: Dative Baseline, Spatial Coordinates & Everyday Autonomy (Weeks 5-6)                  |
|  -> Focus: Dative articles, high-frequency prepositions, Wo/Wohin boundary, modal/separable splits|
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

```

---

## PHASE 1: CONVERSATIONAL FOUNDATIONS & ORAL TANDEM (WEEKS 1–2)

### WEEK 1: Formal Greetings, Self-Identity, and Basic Syntax Baseline

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to initiate formal and informal contact, state their own name and geographic origin, manage communication breakdowns politely in the target language, and establish the foundational Verb-Second (V2) main-clause structural default.
- **Grammar:**
  - **The V2 Rule (Verb-Second):** The finite, conjugated verb must occupy the second position in a German main clause.
  - **Subject-Verb-Object (S-V-O) Default:** Established as the simplest default sentence template for beginners (*Ich bin Alex*, *Ich komme aus Spanien*).
  - **Pronoun & Verb Inflection:** Present tense of the irregular verb *sein* (*ich bin*, *Sie sind*, *er/sie/es ist*).
  - **W-Questions:** Question word in Position 1, verb in Position 2, subject in Position 3 (*Wer ist das?*, *Woher kommen Sie?*).
- **Vocabulary Direction:** Formal and informal greetings (*Guten Tag*, *Hallo*, *Tschüss*), first-person singular pronouns, question words (*Wer, Wie, Was, Woher*), and basic countries of origin.
- **Listening:** **Bottom-up Auditory Decoding** via **High-Variability Phonetic Training (HVPT)**; listening to rapid native speakers to identify word boundaries and the vocalic glottal stop (*Knacklaut*) before vowel-initial words (*ich*, *aus*).
- **Speaking:** Practice speech motor-planning in tandem with pronunciation; producing short formulas with distinct phonemic boundaries, avoiding English phonetic defaults (e.g., pronouncing *wer* with a clean friction /v/ sound).
- **Reading:** Reading highly simplified greeting and receptionist dialogues, identifying subject-verb positions.
- **Writing:** **Controlled Copywriting**; filling in a personal ID card template by hand (*Ich bin...*, *Ich komme aus...*), focusing on capitalization rules (nouns and sentence starters).
- **Pronunciation:** Pronouncing the voiceless palatal fricative or "ich-laut" (/ç/) by blowing air through slightly smiling lips, and establishing the initial German consonant /v/ (as in *wer* [ve:ɐ̯]).
- **Practical Germany Communication:** Navigating a welcome or registration desk; formally greeting an administrator, stating your name, and spelling it aloud using German letter names.
- **Interaction:** Exchanging personal details with a partner in a low-stakes mingle; asking and answering questions about identity and origin formally (*Sie*) and informally (*du*).
- **Daily Core Integration:** Daily German Core **Days 1–4**; automating interaction repair formulas (*Wie bitte?*, *Noch einmal, bitte!*) and greeting tokens.
- **Goethe Connection:** Directly aligns with the **Goethe-Zertifikat A1 Sprechen (Teil 1)**; executing structured self-introductions and spelling out names.
- **What is Deliberately Deferred:** Verb conjugation paradigms for plural pronouns (*wir, ihr*), complex spelling of foreign geographic names, and gender-marking systems.

---

### WEEK 2: Local Logistics, Commuter Tracking, and Basic Transactions

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to locate public and office spaces, state presence/absence of tools, report transit commutes and delays, order food staples at a bakery, and notify a supervisor of an illness.
- **Grammar:**
  - **Existential & Possessive Frames:** Stating presence or absence using *sein* paired with *da / nicht da* (*Der Schlüssel ist nicht da*).
  - **The Possessive Marker:** Using the stable feminine possessive frame **"Das ist meine..."** (*Das ist meine E-Mail*) to express ownership without case calculations.
  - **High-Frequency Verbs:** Present tense of *haben* (*ich habe*, *Sie haben*) and the transitive verb *fahren* (*ich fahre*).
  - **Dative Chunks (Pre-Scaffolded):** Transit methods are learned strictly as **unanalyzed, frozen lexical chunks** (**mit dem Bus**, **mit der U-Bahn**) to bypass case selection load.
- **Vocabulary Direction:** Office/classroom nouns (*der Drucker*, *der Tisch*, *der Schlüssel*), transit vehicles (*der Zug*, *die U-Bahn*), bakery staples (*das Brot*, *die Brote*, *der Kaffee*), and health terms (*krank*, *das Fieber*, *der Arzt*).
- **Listening:** Connected speech boundary detection; listening to station and café announcements to extract platform numbers, double-digit prices up to 50, and transit delays (*zu spät*).
- **Speaking:** Producing simple shopping requests and transit updates; deploying repair formulas assertively when faced with rapid native input.
- **Reading:** Scanning simple office directories, public transit timetables, and medical calendar boards for functional details (*geöffnet/geschlossen*).
- **Writing:** **Guided Writing**; writing a 3-sentence commute delay update or a medical absence message to a training supervisor using pre-fabricated sentence frames.
- **Pronunciation:** Producing the front rounded vowel /ʏ/ in *Schlüssel* (rounding lips as if to whistle while saying "ee"), and terminal devoicing (pronouncing the "g" in *Zug* as a sharp voiceless "k" [tsu\:g]).
- **Practical Germany Communication:** Navigating a German bakery (*Bäckerei*); ordering staples politely using **"Ich möchte..."**, decoding double-digit price totals, paying cash, and executing tipping etiquette (*Stimmt so!*).
- **Interaction:** Conducting a simulated emergency call-in sick; notifying a supervisor of an absence formally, stating symptoms, and confirming a doctor's visit (*Ich bin krank. Ich habe Fieber. Ich kann heute nicht kommen*).
- **Daily Core Integration:** Daily German Core **Days 5–14**; integrating transit vocabulary, time adverbs (*heute, morgen*), and closing formulas (*Bis morgen!*).
- **Goethe Connection:** Aligns with **Goethe A1 Hören and Schreiben**; extracting numeric details, times, and drafting short personal communications.
- **What is Deliberately Deferred:** Active production of dative case rules, active temporal subject-verb inversion (establishing S-V-O as the absolute default for now), and double-digit numbers above 50.

---

## PHASE 2: STRUCTURAL MAPPING & THE DIRECT OBJECT SYSTEM (WEEKS 3–4)

### WEEK 3: Pronouns, Conjugation, and the Noun Gender System

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to conjugate regular present-tense verbs accurately, memorize noun vocabulary alongside definite and indefinite articles, and use basic syntactic negation in V2 structures.
- **Grammar:**
  - **Full Present-Tense Conjugation (Singular & Formal):** Mastering personal endings for *ich* (-e), *du* (-st), *er/sie/es* (-t), and *Sie* (-en).
  - **High-Frequency Stem-Mutating Verbs:** Present-tense of *haben* (*hast/hat*) and *sprechen* (*sprichst/spricht*).
  - **The Noun Gender System:** Mapping nominative articles (*der, die, das*, *ein, eine, ein*).
  - **Basic Negation with *****nicht*****:** Syntactic V2 placement rules; *nicht* goes at the end of simple clauses with a single finite verb (*Ich arbeite heute nicht*), but stands directly before prepositional modifiers (*nicht im Büro*).
- **Vocabulary Direction:** Nouns learned strictly as **integrated phonetic blocks with their definite articles** (*der Drucker*, *das Handy*, *der Schlüssel*), and language names.
- **Listening:** **Receptive Plural Pronoun Exposure**; listening to scripts containing *wir* and *ihr* forms to support natural top-down comprehension without requiring active conjugational calculations.
- **Speaking:** Participating in basic social interviews, stating linguistic backgrounds and present tasks (*Ich spreche Englisch and ein bisschen Deutsch*).
- **Reading:** Reading simple corporate team profiles, matching colleagues to their native languages, current locations, and available tools.
- **Writing:** Guided paragraph writing; combining simple sentences with coordinating conjunctions (*und, aber*) while strictly maintaining correct verb-second syntax.
- **Pronunciation:** Isolating the voiceless velar fricative or "ach-laut" (/x/) in *suchen* (making a soft throat-clearing sound at the back of the mouth), and pronouncing the silent "h" in *wohnen* as a pure vowel length marker.
- **Practical Germany Communication:** Exchanging digital contact details and professional coordinates at a social mixer, verifying names and phone numbers.
- **Interaction:** Conducting a basic office status briefing; stating who is working, who is studying, and what tools are available (*Herr Schmidt arbeitet heute. Ich lerne Deutsch*).
- **Daily Core Integration:** Daily German Core **Days 15–20**; integrating *die Arbeit*, *die E-Mail*, *das Geld*, and regular question structures.
- **Goethe Connection:** Directly supports **Goethe A1 Lesen and Sprechen (Teil 2)**; extracting factual data from short texts and formulating simple queries.
- **What is Deliberately Deferred:** Plural present-tense verb conjugation (except receptive *wir/ihr*), and complex spelling or gender rules based on weak phonetic ending heuristics (such as "-el").

---

### WEEK 4: The Accusative Case, Possession, and Separable Operations

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to distinguish between subject (Nominative) and direct object (Accusative) cases, negate nouns using *kein*, use singular possessive pronouns, and execute pre-conjugated separable verb split brackets.
- **Grammar:**
  - **The Accusative Masculine Shift:** Mastering the direct-object masculine inflection (*der \\(\rightarrow\\) den*, *ein \\(\rightarrow\\) einen*, *kein \\(\rightarrow\\) keinen*, *mein \\(\rightarrow\\) meinen*), while keeping feminine, neuter, and plural nouns structurally stable.
  - **Noun Negation:** Declining the negative determiner *kein / keine / keinen* in direct object positions.
  - **Possessive Determiners:** Declining singular possessives (*mein/meine/meinen*, *dein/deine/deinen*, *Ihr/Ihre/Ihren*).
  - **Polite Request Frames:** Formulating desires using pre-assembled modal frames (**Können Sie...**, **Ich möchte...**); the modal auxiliary sits in Position 2 and the uninflected infinitive is sent to the end of the clause.
  - **Pre-Conjugated Separable Verbs:** Splitting high-frequency present-tense verbs (**anrufen, anfangen**), placing the prefix (*an*) at the absolute end of the sentence (*Ich rufe den Chef an*).
  - **Spaced Temporal Syntax Inversion:** Initiating clauses with a starting temporal adverb (*heute, morgen, jetzt*), forcing the subject pronoun to invert to Position 3 (*Jetzt bin ich fertig*).
- **Vocabulary Direction:** Direct object transitives (*brauchen, suchen, haben*), retail nouns (*die Tüte*, *die Quittung*), time coordinates (*jetzt*), and double-digit numbers up to 100.
- **Listening:** Identifying the subtle acoustic differences between neuter (*mein Handy*) and masculine accusative (*meinen Schlüssel*) case markers in connected, fast-paced speech.
- **Speaking:** Participating in roleplays using modal requests and temporal inversions (*Morgen fahre ich mit der U-Bahn*).
- **Reading:** Reading digital text chat logs and text notifications regarding broken equipment and scheduled client meetings.
- **Writing:** Composing a formal rescheduling email to a client, requesting a shift in appointment slot (*Ich möchte einen Termin verschieben*).
- **Pronunciation:** Producing the rounded front vowel /ø:/ in *möchten*, and pronouncing the voiceless postalveolar fricative /ʃ/ in *schicken* with projected, rounded lips.
- **Practical Germany Communication:** Navigating a German supermarket checkout; greeting the cashier, ordering grocery items, requesting a shopping bag (*eine Tüte*) or receipt (*eine Quittung*) using the correct case, and decoding double-digit totals.
- **Interaction:** A **Willis three-stage TBLT Task Cycle**; roleplaying a workplace shift handover, reporting completed tasks (*Ich bin fertig*), scheduling breaks (*Ich mache eine Pause*), and announcing departure.
- **Daily Core Integration:** Daily German Core **Days 21–30**; automating *der Drucker*, *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht*, *Ich bin fertig*, and *Wir sehen uns morgen!*.
- **Goethe Connection:** Prepares for the **Goethe A1 Schreiben (Teil 1: Formulare)** and **Hören**; filling out administrative forms and decoding numbers up to 100.
- **What is Deliberately Deferred:** Genitive and Dative cases, two-way preposition calculations, and full modal verb conjugation tables.

---

## PHASE 3: DATIVE BASELINE, SPATIAL COORDINATES, & SYSTEM INTEGRATION (WEEKS 5–6)

### WEEK 5: The Dative Baseline, Transit, and Stationary Space

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to use the singular dative case with high-frequency prepositions and verbs, describe relative physical locations using two-way prepositions, and give clear pedestrian directions.
- **Grammar:**
  - **The Singular Dative Baseline:** Definite articles (*dem, der, dem*), high-frequency pronouns (**mir, Ihnen**), and contractions (**zum, zur, beim, vom**).
  - **Fixed Dative Prepositions:** Active mastery of **mit** (transit/people), **zu** (direction), **bei** (status/location), and **seit** (time duration).
  - **Dative-Governing Verbs:** Using **helfen** and **schmecken** in pre-assembled, stable communicative frames (*Können Sie mir helfen?*, *Das schmeckt mir gut*).
  - **Two-Way Prepositions (Static Location):** Active dative case assignment answering **Wo?** (Where?) with **in (im)**, **an (am)**, **auf**, and **vor** (*im Büro*, *auf dem Tisch*).
  - **Pedestrian Direction Syntax:** V2 imperative structures (*Gehen Sie geradeaus*, *Biegen Sie rechts ab*).
- **Vocabulary Direction:** Navigation adverbs (*geradeaus, rechts, links*), local landmarks (*der Bahnhof*, *der Supermarkt*, *die Straße*), and scheduling units (*die Besprechung*, *der Termin*).
- **Listening:** Connected speech transcription (c-tests); decoding blurred dative contractions (*beim Arzt*, *zur Arbeit*) and directional instructions over simulated street background noise (TAP alignment).
- **Speaking:** Actively describing a workspace layout, directing a partner where objects are located, and requesting spatial adjustments.
- **Reading:** Reading digital travel itineraries, identifying intermediate locations and connecting routes (*nach Berlin*, *vom Hauptbahnhof*).
- **Writing:** Writing a short, 3-sentence email confirming an appointment date, specifying the time, and identifying the meeting partner using dative prepositions (*am Montag*, *mit dem Kollegen*).
- **Pronunciation:** Correctly producing the initial affricate /ts/ in *zwischen* (making a sharp "ts" sound as at the end of the English word "cats" but placed at the front).
- **Practical Germany Communication:** Exchanging street directions; approaching a pedestrian, asking how to reach a local landmark, decoding spatial markers, and navigating a simplified local map.
- **Interaction:** Calling a doctor's clinic (*Arztpraxis*) to schedule a medical slot; negotiating a time coordinate, stating how long you have been sick (*seit zwei Tagen krank*), and confirming the appointment.
- **Daily Core Integration:** Retaining and reinforcing Core dative transit structures (*mit dem Bus*, *im Büro*) and politeness tags (*Es tut mir leid*, *Kein Problem!*).
- **Goethe Connection:** Directly supports **Goethe A1 Sprechen (Teil 3)** and **Hören**; asking for information, responding to everyday requests, and understanding spoken coordinates.
- **What is Deliberately Deferred:** Plural dative (*den ... -n* with noun changes), low-utility prepositions (*nach, von, aus* in dative case rules), dative verb *gefallen*, and relative spatial prepositions (*unter, über, hinter, neben, zwischen*).

---

### WEEK 6: Modal Verbs, Transactions, and Everyday Problem-Solving

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to express obligations and intentions using modal verbs, execute clothing and transit purchases, report household emergencies, and integrate all A1 syntax spontaneously in un-pressured environments.
- **Grammar:**
  - **Active Modal Auxiliary Frames:** Singular conjugations of **müssen** (*ich muss, du musst, er muss*) and **wollen** (*ich will, du willst, er will*) using the V2 split-bracket structure.
  - **Dative Retail Verbs:** Stating fits and suitability using **passen** and **stehen** paired with dative pronouns (*Die Jacke passt mir*, *Das steht Ihnen gut*).
  - **The Spatial Case Boundary (Location vs. Movement):** High-frequency static location (*Wo?* + Dative) using *sein* versus directed movement (*Wohin?* + Accusative) using un-split directional verbs (*Ich gehe in... / auf...*).
  - **Separable Transit Verbs:** Using pre-conjugated, frozen transit chunks (**abfahren, ankommen**) correctly.
- **Vocabulary Direction:** Clothing nouns (*die Jacke*, *das Hemd*, *die Größe*), household utilities (*die Heizung*, *das Licht*, *das Wasser*, *kaputt*, *reparieren*), and transit terms (*die Verspätung*, *die Auskunft*).
- **Listening:** Multi-stage listening tasks; listening to long voicemail messages left by landlords or colleagues to extract schedule changes, delayed departures, and location shifts.
- **Speaking:** Participating in retail clothing transactions and reporting apartment maintenance requests using modal brackets.
- **Reading:** Reading un-simplified graded texts with 95% to 98% vocabulary coverage to promote reading rate and prevent mental translation.
- **Writing:** Composing a 5-line daily work log or status report, specifying daily transit methods, obligations, and task completion times.
- **Pronunciation:** Correctly producing the rounded front vowel /ø:/ in *Größe*, and maintaining sharp terminal voiceless releases on consonantal clusters in *kaputt*.
- **Practical Germany Communication:** Lodging an emergency maintenance complaint; contacting a landlord, reporting a broken utility (*Die Heizung ist kaputt*), stating how long it has been broken using *seit*, and requesting a repair (*Können Sie das bitte reparieren?*).
- **Interaction:** A **Willis three-stage TBLT Task Cycle (A1 Graduation)**; shopping for clothes (greeting, requesting a size, evaluating fit, and paying) or querying a transit desk (resolving a travel delay and buying tickets).
- **Daily Core Integration:** Synthesizing all **Days 1–30 Daily German Core** formulas in randomized retrieval routines.
- **Goethe Connection:** Final preparation for the **Goethe-Zertifikat A1 (Start Deutsch 1)**; mock testing **Sprechen**, **Hören**, **Lesen**, and drafting the **Schreiben** personal email.
- **What is Deliberately Deferred:** Rule-based spatial paired verb contrasts (*stellen/legen* for movement), relative preposition case shifts, and full plural modal verb paradigms.

---

# A1 CONTENT TO DEFER TO A2

To protect the adult learner's working memory from cognitive overload and stabilize high-frequency syntactic structures first, the following features are deliberately removed from active A1 mastery and deferred to A2:

1. **Dative Plural (*****den ... -n*****) Noun Inflections:**
   - *Why:* Requires the learner to calculate both article case and morphophonemic noun changes simultaneously (adding an `-n` to plural nouns, with exceptions). This high element interactivity triggers a bottleneck in speech motor-planning.
2. **Dative Pronoun *****dir***** (Informal "You"):**
   - *Why:* Deferred to prevent register and pronoun interference. Focus is placed on mastering *mir* (for immediate survival requests like *helfen*) and *Ihnen* (for formal professional transactions).
3. **Dative Prepositions *****nach, von, aus***** (Active Case Calculation):**
   - *Why:* While learned receptively or in frozen chunks (*aus Spanien*, *nach Hause*), active grammatical assembly is deferred. Restricting the active inventory to *mit, zu, bei, seit* ensures faster automaticity of article endings.
4. **Dative-Governing Verb *****gefallen*****:**
   - *Why:* Unlike *helfen* or *schmecken*, *gefallen* requires a complex, non-salient inversion of typical thematic roles (the liked item is the subject, the person is the dative object), which causes grammatical processing failure under real-time speaking pressure.
5. **Relative Spatial Prepositions (*****unter, über, hinter, neben, zwischen*****):**
   - *Why:* Reduces the nominal difficulty of the spatial coordinate system. Concentrating active dative space strictly on *in, an, auf, vor* stabilizes the concept before introducing more complex vertical/horizontal coordinates.
6. **Rule-Based Paired Positional Verb Contrasts (*****stehen/stellen, liegen/legen*****):**
   - *Why:* Simultaneous case-calculation (Dative vs. Accusative) and lexical-selection (transitive vs. intransitive verb pairs) is a classic cognitive bottleneck. In A1, location (*Wo*) is practiced using *sein*, and movement (*Wohin*) is practiced using un-split directional frames.
7. **Separable-Verb Prefixes (Formal Morphological Rules):**
   - *Why:* Rather than teaching exhaustive separable prefix charts (*mit-, ab-, aus-, vor-*), A1 focuses on pre-conjugated, frozen transit chunks (*ankommen, abfahren, anrufen, anfangen*).
8. **Full Modal Verb Conjugation Paradigms:**
   - *Why:* Restricting active production of *müssen* and *wollen* strictly to the first-person singular (*ich muss, ich will*) and formal query (*Können Sie*) protects early morphosyntactic retrieval.

---

# A1 6-WEEK MASTERY CHECKPOINT

Upon graduating from Week 6, the learner must independently demonstrate the following functional "can-do" capabilities:

### 1. Pronunciation & Phonological Intelligibility

- **Vocalic Precision:** Produce distinct, intelligible German-specific sounds—especially the front rounded vowels /ʏ/ (*Schlüssel*) and /ø:/ (*Größe*)—without substituting English defaults.
- **Acoustic Categories:** Maintain correct consonant distinctions (such as the smiling /ç/ "ich-laut" in *nicht* versus the /x/ "ach-laut" in *noch*) and execute terminal consonant devoicing consistently.

### 2. Auditory Decoding (Bottom-Up Listening)

- **Boundary Detection:** Successfully detect word boundaries in rapid native connected speech, mapping dative contractions (*im, am, zum, zur, beim, vom*) accurately in audiotaped transit and office announcements.
- **Gist Extraction:** Extract factual times, double-digit prices, and schedule availability under simulated noisy background conditions.

### 3. Syntax Baseline & Case Selection (Speaking & Interaction)

- **Verb-Second (V2) Stability:** Keep the conjugated finite verb securely anchored in Position 2 across all declarative statements, questions, and temporal inversions.
- **Temporal Inversion:** Spontaneously invert the subject and verb when beginning a sentence with a temporal adverb (*Heute bin ich...*, *Morgen fahre ich...*).
- **Split-Bracket Control:** Execute the final prefix split on separable verbs (*Ich rufe den Arzt an*) and place infinitive verbs at the end of modal request brackets (*Ich muss heute zur Arbeit gehen*).
- **Case Boundary Precision:** Maintain the direct-object masculine accusative article shift (*den, einen, keinen, meinen, Ihren*) and assign the static locative dative case (*im Büro*, *auf der Straße*) correctly in targeted speaking prompts.

### 4. Reading and Writing

- **Reading Rate:** Scan un-simplified graded texts with 95% to 98% vocabulary coverage, extracting key transit, scheduling, or operational details without dictionary translating.
- **Controlled Paragraph Composition:** Write short, coherent 5-line emails or status logs with correct capitalization, verb inflection, and syntactic word order.

### 5. Spontaneous Problem-Solving & Repair (TBLT Graduation)

- **Task Negotiation:** Successfully complete a multi-stage, goal-directed roleplay (such as lodging a maintenance emergency with a landlord or purchasing transit tickets while managing a travel delay).
- **Communication Repair:** When faced with rapid speech, unexpected questions, or administrative barriers, immediately deploy repair and clarification formulas (*Wie bitte?*, *Langsam, bitte!*) to keep the interaction active in German without reverting to English.

---

# 6-WEEK ARCHITECTURE LOCKPOINT

The following structural, pedagogical, and administrative design decisions are **permanently locked** across the A1 German syllabus:

1. **Strict 6-Week Temporal Boundary:**
   - The curriculum is fixed at exactly **6 weeks**.
   - The phase structure is locked as: **Phase 1** (Weeks 1–2), **Phase 2** (Weeks 3–4), and **Phase 3** (Weeks 5–6).
   - No separate Phase 4 is permitted.
2. **The Declarative-to-Procedural (D/P) Learning Order:**
   - No discovery-based, inductive, or purely implicit grammar lessons are allowed.
   - Every unit must follow the locked sequence of: **Explicit Declarative Explanation \\(\rightarrow\\) Controlled Proceduralization \\(\rightarrow\\) Task-Based Automatization**.
3. **V2 Syntactic Baseline:**
   - German syntax is introduced via the **Verb-Second (V2)** rule as the structural anchor of the language, establishing Subject-Verb-Object (S-V-O) strictly as a simple default starting template.
4. **Tandem Oral Integration from Day 1:**
   - Speaking, pronunciation (HVPT), and listening must develop in tandem from Day 1.
   - Auditory training must never act as a prerequisite bottleneck that delays active speaking.
5. **Continuous Vocabulary Development:**
   - All rigid quantitative pre-requisite vocabulary thresholds (e.g., mastering exactly 300 lemmas before syntax) are eliminated.
   - Nouns must always be introduced and memorized as a single phonetic block alongside their definite article and plural form (*das Brot, die Brote*).
6. **Flexible TBLT Scaffolding:**
   - Meaning-focused communicative tasks (Willis's TBLT) are reserved strictly for larger, goal-directed task cycles.
   - TBLT is never required for small, bottom-up pronunciation drills, vocabulary flashcards, or controlled grammar practice.
7. **Limited, Practical Mediation:**
   - Mediation at A1 is treated as limited, practical communication support (extracting times, pointing directions on maps, interpreting signs) rather than translation into English.
8. **Elimination of Quantitative Gating:**
   - All mandatory reaction-time barriers (e.g., "within 2 seconds"), percentage gates (e.g., "85% accuracy under speed pressure"), or strict numeric price ceilings (e.g., "up to 100 Euro") are completely removed.
   - Mastery is assessed qualitatively through functional, communicative task completion and strategic communication repair.

# FINALIZED 6-WEEK A1 MASTER CURRICULUM

Below is the finalized 6-week A1 structural outline, designed to leverage **Ullman's Declarative/Procedural model of SLA** and **Robert DeKeyser's Skill Acquisition Theory** within an intensive, adult-optimized study schedule.

### WEEK 1 — Foundations of Contact, Identity, and Verb-Second (V2) Syntax Basics

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to initiate formal and informal contact, introduce themselves, state country/city of origin, spell their first name using the German alphabet, and deploy repair formulas politely to manage speaking anxiety and rapid native input.
- **Grammar:** Verb-Second (V2) main-clause word order as the non-negotiable structural anchor (S-V-O is introduced strictly as a default beginner template, not as a universal German rule). Irregular present-tense singular of *sein* (*ich bin, Sie sind*). W-Question word order.
- **Vocabulary:** Formal greetings (*Guten Tag*, *Auf Wiedersehen*) [Day 1, 5 core], informal greetings (*Hallo*, *Tschüss*) [Day 1, 2 core], question words (*Wer, Wie, Was, Woher*), countries of origin (*Deutschland, England, Spanien, Kanada*), and German alphabet letter names.
- **Listening:** Bottom-up auditory decoding of the vocalic glottal stop (*Knacklaut*) preceding vowel-initial words (*ich*, *aus*). HVPT training with 5–8 native voices to distinguish phonemes like /f/ vs. /v/ in connected speech.
- **Speaking:** Spontaneously deploying holistically learned, unanalyzed interaction repair formulas (*Wie bitte?*, *Langsam, bitte!*) [Day 3 core] to slow down rapid input.
- **Reading & Writing:** Scanning simple conference registries or nameplate displays. Controlled copywriting of personal frames (*Ich bin... Ich komme aus...*) onto official check-in forms.
- **Germany Situation & Ausbildung Bridge:** Navigating a hotel welcome registry or introducing yourself formally (*Herr/Frau*) during an introductory round-robin at a new vocational school (*Berufsschule*).
- **Daily Core Connection:** Days 1–4: Greetings, *ja/nein*, *Danke/Bitte*, *Sprechen Sie Englisch?*, *Ein bisschen*, *Wie bitte?*, *Entschuldigung*. Runs strictly as a lightweight (15–20 min) reinforcement layer.
- **Goethe / CEFR Alignment:** Directly supports **Goethe A1 Sprechen (Teil 1)**: spelling out names and stating origin details.

### WEEK 2 — Basic Everyday Transactions, Commutes, and Logistics

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to navigate local public transit, report commutes and transit delays using pre-scaffolded dative chunks, declare possession of essential tools, and conduct simple cash purchases at a local bakery [Day 8, 17, 18, 19 core].
- **Grammar:** Copula status reporting using *sein* + *da / nicht da* (*Der Schlüssel ist da*, *Der Chef ist nicht da*). Present-tense first-person singular and formal plural of *haben* (*ich habe*, *Sie haben*). Feminine possessive frame **"Das ist meine..."** (*Das ist meine E-Mail*) to express ownership without case inflections [Day 24 core]. Pre-scaffolded, unanalyzed dative transit chunks (**mit dem Bus**, **mit der U-Bahn**, **mit dem Zug**) [Day 18 core].
- **Vocabulary:** Office and transit nouns (*der Schlüssel*, *das Handy*, *der Zug*, *der Bus*, *die U-Bahn*) [Day 6, 8, 18 core], bakery staples (*das Brot, die Brote*, *der Kaffee*) [Day 17 core], price currency (*der Euro*), numbers 0–20, and delay status (*zu spät*) [Day 18 core].
- **Listening:** Connected speech decoding to extract platform numbers and delay warnings (*zu spät*) from station announcements. Transcribing spoken price totals up to 20 Euro.
- **Speaking:** Formulating polite retail requests using the unanalyzed frame **"Ich möchte..., bitte"** (*Ich möchte Brot und Kaffee, bitte*) [Day 17 core].
- **Reading & Writing:** Scanning printed bakery price tags and train platform displays. Writing a 2-sentence delay text message in a visual template: *Guten Tag. Der Bus ist heute zu spät. Bis morgen!* [Day 12, 14, 18 core].
- **Germany Situation & Ausbildung Bridge:** Completing a checkout transaction at a bakery counter. Stating the presence/absence of necessary workstation tools to a classmate (*Der Schlüssel is da*, *Das Handy ist nicht da*).
- **Daily Core Connection:** Days 5–14: *Auf Wiedersehen*, *das Handy*, *hier/dort*, *Ich komme...*, *der Bahnhof*, *Ich wohne in...*, *Können Sie mir helfen?*, *der Euro*, *geöffnet/geschlossen*, *heute/morgen*, *Kein Problem!*, *Bis morgen!*.
- **Goethe / CEFR Alignment:** Aligns with **Goethe A1 Hören (Teil 1)**: extracting numeric and pricing details, and **Lesen (Teil 2)**: scanning notice boards.

### WEEK 3 — Subject Pronoun Mapping, Regular Conjugation Suffixes, and Noun Gender Baseline

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to conjugate regular present-tense verbs with correct suffix agreement, memorize noun vocabulary alongside definite/indefinite articles, and apply basic syntactic negation in V2 declarative sentences [Day 16, 21 core].
- **Grammar:** Singular pronoun paradigm (*ich, du, er, sie, es*) and formal *Sie*. Present-tense regular verb suffixes: *-e*(*ich*), *-st* (*du*), *-t* (*er/sie/es*), and *-en* (*Sie*). Present-tense of *haben* (*hast/hat*) and *sprechen* (*sprichst/spricht*) with stem-vowel changes. Syntactic positioning of *nicht* [Day 21, 26 core].
- **Vocabulary:** Workplace and office nouns memorized preferably (but not as an absolute requirement for every encountered noun) as **integrated phonetic blocks with their definite articles and plurals when useful** (*der Drucker, die Drucker*; *das Handy, die Handys*; *der Schlüssel, die Schlüssel*; *das Büro*, *die Arbeit*), languages (*Deutsch, Englisch, Spanisch*), and the verb *wohnen* [Day 6, 8, 15, 26 core].
- **Listening:** Receptive-only exposure to plural pronouns (*wir*, *ihr*) and their corresponding verb inflections to develop top-down comprehension of group statements without requiring active grammatical assembly [Day 30 core].
- **Speaking:** Stating your linguistic profile and tasks using irregular verbs (*Ich spreche Englisch and ein bisschen Deutsch*). Oral conjugation drills.
- **Reading & Writing:** Reading simple company staff profiles to match employees to their languages and residencies. Composing a 3-sentence profile of a colleague: *Das ist Sarah. Sie spricht Spanisch, aber sie lernt heute Deutsch.*
- **Germany Situation & Ausbildung Bridge:** Introducing yourself and a peer formally (*Das ist Alex. Er wohnt in Frankfurt*) during an introductory welcome mixer at a vocational school.
- **Daily Core Connection:** Days 15–20: *die Arbeit*, *die E-Mail*, *schicken*, *das Geld*, *Bezahlen, bitte!*, *Stimmt so!*.
- **Goethe / CEFR Alignment:** Directly supports **Goethe A1 Sprechen (Teil 2)**: asking and answering questions about personal details.

### WEEK 4 — Accusative Case Direct Objects, Noun Negation (kein), and Temporal V2 Inversion

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to apply the masculine accusative article shift, negate nouns using *kein*, decline singular possessive determiners, formulate requests using modal frames, split separable verbs, and execute temporal V2 inversion.
- **Grammar:** The Accusative masculine shift (*der \\(\rightarrow\\) den*, *ein \\(\rightarrow\\) einen*); feminine and neuter articles remain stable (*die/das*, *eine/ein*). Noun negation with *kein / keine / keinen* [Day 19 core]. Singular possessive determiners (*mein/meine/meinen*, *dein/deine/deinen*, *Ihr/Ihre/Ihren*). Request frames (**Können Sie...**, **Ich möchte...**) with infinitive verbs at the end of the clause. Separable verbs (**anrufen, anfangen**), split with the prefix (*an*) at the absolute end of the sentence (*Ich rufe den Chef an*). Temporal V2 inversion (*heute, morgen, jetzt*), forcing the subject pronoun to invert to Position 3 (*Jetzt bin ich fertig*) [Day 12, 29 core].
- **Vocabulary:** Transitive verbs (*brauchen*, *suchen*, *haben*), retail nouns (*die Tüte*, *die Quittung*), temporal adverbs (*jetzt*, *heute*, *morgen*), the verb *schicken*, and double-digit numbers up to 100.
- **Listening:** Distinguishing between neuter (*mein Handy*) and masculine accusative (*meinen Schlüssel*) case markers in fast connected native speech. Extracting double-digit checkout prices up to 100 Euro.
- **Speaking:** Formulating polite requests (*Ich möchte den Termin verschieben*) [Day 22 core] and executing subject-verb inversion (*Heute fange ich die Arbeit an*).
- **Reading & Writing:** Reading text-message logs regarding equipment failures (*Der Drucker funktioniert nicht*) to extract scheduling details [Day 26 core]. Composing a 3-sentence update: *Heute fange ich die Arbeit um neun Uhr an. Mein Computer funktioniert nicht. Jetzt rufe ich die Kollegin an.*
- **Germany Situation & Ausbildung Bridge:** Navigating a supermarket checkout (greet, order, request *eine Tüte, bitte* or *eine Quittung, bitte* using correct accusative). Performing a TBLT workplace shift handover with a classmate [Day 29 core].
- **Daily Core Connection:** Days 21–30: *krank*, *der Arzt*, *Gute Besserung!*, *keine Zeit*, *den Termin verschieben*, *der Drucker*, *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht*, *schicken*, *die Nachricht*, *Ich bin fertig*, *Ich mache eine Pause*, *der Kollege*, *Wir sehen uns morgen!*.
- **Goethe / CEFR Alignment:** Aligns with **Goethe A1 Schreiben (Teil 1: Formulare)**: filling out official administrative templates, and **Lesen (Teil 1)**: processing transactional text.

### WEEK 5 — Dative Case Baseline, Static Spatial Locations (Wo? + Dat), and Pedestrian Directions

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to use the singular dative case with high-frequency prepositions and verbs, describe stationary spatial locations using two-way prepositions, ask for or give pedestrian directions, and schedule medical appointments.
- **Grammar:** Singular dative definite articles (*dem, der, dem*), dative personal pronouns (*mir, Ihnen*), and contractions (**zum, zur, beim, vom**). Core prepositions: **mit** (transit/people), **zu** (direction), **bei** (status/location), and **seit** (time duration) [Day 18, 21 core]. Dative-governing verbs **helfen** and **schmecken** in pre-assembled frames (*Können Sie mir helfen?*) [Day 9 core]. Two-way prepositions in static location contexts (**in (im)**, **an (am)**, **auf**, and **vor**) [Day 6, 8 core]. Pedestrian direction imperatives (*Gehen Sie geradeaus*, *Biegen Sie rechts ab*).
- **Vocabulary:** Navigation adverbs (*geradeaus, rechts, links*, *vorbei*), local landmarks (*der Bahnhof*, *der Supermarkt*, *die Straße*, *die Post*), and scheduling units (*die Besprechung*, *der Termin*) [Day 8, 22, 30 core].
- **Listening:** Transcription drills (c-tests) decoding dative contractions (*zur Post*, *beim Supermarkt*) over simulated background noise (TAP alignment).
- **Speaking & Reading:** Describing physical workspace layouts (*Der Laptop ist auf dem Tisch*). Scanning map guides and printed transit route listings. Extensive reading utilizes the 95–98% lexical coverage as a useful guideline, not a rigid requirement, allowing supported harder material where appropriate.
- **Writing:** Writing a short meeting confirmation email: *Guten Tag. Ich möchte die Besprechung am Dienstag bestätigen. Der Termin ist um zehn Uhr mit dem Kollegen. Passt das?*
- **Germany Situation & Ausbildung Bridge:** Asking a passerby for directions to a local landmark (*Supermarkt, Bahnhof*) and bailing out of a navigation error. Calling a doctor's clinic to arrange a medical slot (*Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank*). Coordinating workspace layouts laterally with a classmate.
- **Goethe / CEFR Alignment:** Aligns with **Goethe A1 Sprechen (Teil 3)**: asking for and responding to directions, and **Hören (Teil 2)**: public announcements.

### WEEK 6 — Modal/Separable Consolidation, Practical Problem-Solving, and A1 Synthesis

- **Main Objective:** The learner will be able to express obligations and intentions using modal verbs, report apartment utility failures, manage transit ticketing and routing, and integrate all A1 syntax spontaneously in un-pressured survival roleplays [Day 11, 18, 23, 26 core].
- **Grammar:** Singular present-tense modal verb frames of **müssen** (*ich muss, du musst, er muss*) and **wollen** (*ich will, du willst, er will*) in V2 split-bracket configurations (*Ich muss heute einkaufen*, *Er will eine Pause machen*). Dative retail verbs **passen** and **stehen** with dative pronouns (*Die Jacke passt mir*, *Das steht Ihnen gut*). Spatial case boundary: static location (*Wo?* + Dative) using *sein* versus directed movement (*Wohin?* + Accusative) using un-split directional verbs (*Ich gehe ins Büro*). Separable transit verbs (**abfahren, ankommen**).
- **Vocabulary:** Clothing nouns (*die Jacke*, *das Hemd*, *die Größe*), household utilities (*die Heizung*, *das Licht*, *das Wasser*, *kaputt*, *reparieren*), and transit terms (*die Verspätung*, *die Auskunft*) [Day 11, 18, 26 core].
- **Listening:** Voicemail parsing; extracting delayed departures, schedule changes, and location coordinates from long, complex native messages.
- **Speaking & Reading:** Evaluating sizes and fits in a retail setting. Scanning un-simplified graded extensive texts.
- **Writing:** Composing a 5-line daily work log or status report, specifying daily transit methods, obligations, and task completion times.
- **Germany Situation & Ausbildung Bridge:** Contacting a landlord formally to report an emergency utility failure (*Die Heizung ist kaputt*), stating how long it has been broken using *seit*, and requesting a repair. Negotiating transit schedules at a DB Information counter (*Reisezentrum*).
- **Daily Core Connection:** Spaced active recall of all Core Days 1–30 elements in randomized retrieval routines.
- **Goethe / CEFR Alignment:** Final preparation for the **Goethe-Zertifikat A1 (Start Deutsch 1)**: mock testing Hören, Lesen, Schreiben, and Sprechen.

---

# FINAL A1 LOCKPOINT

These final structural, pedagogical, and administrative design decisions are **permanently locked** across the A1 German syllabus and must govern all subsequent detailed daily lesson plan generation:

### 1. Structural and Syntactic Rules

- **V2 Non-Negotiable Anchor:** German syntax must be taught using the **Verb-Second (V2) rule** as the absolute grammatical anchor. Subject-Verb-Object (S-V-O) is framed strictly as a simple default starting template, not as a universal German word-order rule.
- **Temporal Inversion Word Order:** Subject-Verb-Subject inversion is introduced in Week 4 once the V2 default is established. The learner must understand that starting a main clause with a temporal adverb (*heute, morgen, jetzt*) forces the subject pronoun to invert to Position 3 to keep the verb locked in Position 2.
- **Bracket Syntax Depth:** Modal auxiliary frames (*müssen, wollen*) and separable verb splits (*anrufen, anfangen*) must be taught only to the depth necessary for functional survival. Absolute grammatical perfection and complete conjugational paradigms are not required; these structures are introduced using high-frequency singular frames (*Ich muss...*, *Ich will...*, *Ich rufe... an*) to minimize cognitive load.
- **The Direct Object Case Boundary:** The Accusative case is taught strictly as the **masculine article shift** (*der \\(\rightarrow\\) den*, *ein \\(\rightarrow\\) einen*, *kein \\(\rightarrow\\) keinen*, *mein \\(\rightarrow\\) meinen*). Feminine, neuter, and plural determiners are highlighted as structurally stable to preserve working memory.

### 2. Lexical and Reading Protocols

- **Noun Vocabulary Formats:** Nouns should preferably be introduced and memorized alongside their definite article and plural form as a single phonetic block (*das Brot, die Brote*), when they are useful, relevant, and communicatively functional. It is not an absolute requirement for every secondary or receptively encountered noun.
- **Extensive Reading (ER) Guidelines:** Graded readers are integrated in Week 6 to consolidate syntax and spelling. While **95% to 98% lexical coverage** is maintained as a highly useful pedagogical guideline for easy reading, it is treated as a supportive benchmark rather than a rigid ceiling, allowing supported harder material where appropriate.
- **Methodological Flexibility:** The curriculum does not dogmatically enforce the same explicit-drill-task sequence for all grammar items. Low-salience or highly analytical structures use explicit declarative explanation, while highly salient or natural conversational features utilize chunks, noticing, and contextual exposure where they are more efficient.

### 3. Practical and Communicative Scaffolding

- **Tandem Oral Integration from Day 1:** Active speaking, High-Variability Phonetic Training (HVPT), and bottom-up listening begin together from Day 1. Pronunciation or acoustic category building must never act as a dry prerequisite course that delays productive speech.
- **Lightweight Separate Daily Core:** The Daily German Core is separate and lightweight (approximately 15–20 minutes at A1). It acts as a spaced retrieval reinforcement layer and is never expanded into the main curriculum.
- **Realistic 4-Hour Study Capacity Allocation:** The 4-hour study capacity represents total available study time, not a requirement to create 4 hours of new curriculum content. The additional time is systematically dedicated to active retrieval practice, listening, speaking, reading, writing, interactive tasks, and interest-driven German exposure.
- **Qualitative Mastery Checkpoints:** Advancement is determined exclusively by **functional, qualitative communicative competence**. Mastery is assessed qualitatively using the progression: **understanding \\(\rightarrow\\) controlled use \\(\rightarrow\\) contextual use \\(\rightarrow\\) functional communication \\(\rightarrow\\) reliable transfer**. Success in strategic repair is judged strictly by functional communication and recovery, not speed, completely bypassing arbitrary speed, trial, or percentage benchmarks.
- **Everyday Germany Dominance:** Situational settings are strictly anchored in everyday life in Germany (cafés, supermarkets, transit desks, clinics, and apartment maintenance). Professional or vocational contexts are limited to basic introductions and task coordinates.

---

# A1 DEFERRED TO A2

The following morphological and syntactic features are **deliberately postponed** to the A2 syllabus to protect working memory during early spontaneous communication:

1. **Dative Plural Inflections (*****den ... -n*****):** Requires the learner to calculate both article changes and morphophonemic noun changes (adding an `-n` to plural nouns, with exceptions) simultaneously under communicative pressure.
2. **Dative Pronoun *****dir***** (Informal "You"):** Deferred to prevent register and pronoun interference. Focus is placed exclusively on mastering *mir* (for survival requests like *helfen*) and formal *Ihnen* (for professional and retail transactions).
3. **Active Grammatical Assembly of Dative Prepositions *****nach*****, *****von*****, *****aus*****:** While learned receptively or in frozen chunks (*aus Spanien*, *nach Hause*), active grammatical assembly is deferred. Restricting the active inventory to *mit, zu, bei, seit* ensures faster automaticity of article endings.
4. **Dative-Governing Verb *****gefallen*****:** Requires a complex, non-salient inversion of typical thematic roles (the liked item is the subject, the person is the dative object), which causes grammatical processing failure under real-time speaking pressure.
5. **Relative Spatial Prepositions (*****unter, über, hinter, neben, zwischen*****):** Concentrating active dative space strictly on the four most high-utility survival prepositions (*in, an, auf, vor*) stabilizes locative case assignment before introducing lateral and vertical coordinates.
6. **Rule-Based Paired Positional Verb Contrasts (*****stehen/stellen, liegen/legen*****):** Simultaneous case-calculation (Dative vs. Accusative) and lexical-selection (transitive vs. intransitive verb pairs) is a classic cognitive bottleneck. In A1, all stationary locations are expressed using *sein*, and movements are expressed using un-split directional verbs.
7. **Separable Verb Prefixes (Formal Morphological Rules):** Rather than teaching exhaustive separable prefix charts (*mit-, ab-, aus-, vor-*), A1 focuses on pre-conjugated, frozen transit chunks (*ankommen, abfahren, anrufen, anfangen*).
8. **Full Modal Verb Conjugation Tables:** Restricting active production of *müssen* and *wollen* strictly to the first-person singular (*ich muss, ich will*) and formal query (*Können Sie*) protects early morphosyntactic retrieval.

The complete, authoritative **`a1-master-curriculum.md`** has been compiled and published to your **Studio** panel. This blueprint systematically integrates the cognitive and neurobiological principles of **Ullman's Declarative/Procedural model**, **DeKeyser's Skill Acquisition Theory**, and **Transfer-Appropriate Processing (TAP)** into a rigorous, highly cohesive six-week instructional cycle.

The syllabus is structured into three progressive phases:

1. **Phase 1 (Weeks 1–2: Foundations & Oral Tandem):** Establishes the canonical **Verb-Second (V2)** syntactic anchor in main clauses and builds early auditory-sensory motor planning through **High-Variability Phonetic Training (HVPT)** and **formulaic communication repair chunks**.
2. **Phase 2 (Weeks 3–4: Structural Mapping & Direct Objects):** Explains present-tense verbal suffix paradigms, introduces the **masculine accusative direct object shift**, and implements **temporal V2 subject-verb-subject inversion** alongside **pre-conjugated separable verb frames**.
3. **Phase 3 (Weeks 5–6: Dative Baseline, Spatial Coordinates, & Integration):** Introduces dative prepositions and static locative prepositions answering **"Wo?"** (Dative) vs. **"Wohin?"** (Accusative). This phase culminates in **Willis's three-stage TBLT Task Cycles** and **graded extensive reading** to prepare learners for real-world Germany encounters and the formal **Goethe-Zertifikat A1** examination.

---

# A1 DEPENDENCY AND RECYCLING MAP

To ensure robust long-term retention and avoid the structural decay of massed grammar practice, the curriculum is organized as a **spiral, distributed retrieval network**. Each week actively builds upon prior structures, recycling them in increasingly complex communicative contexts.

```
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                     A1 DEPENDENCY FLOW CHART                                    |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                                                                                 |
|   [WEEK 1: V2 Baseline] -------------------> [WEEK 3: Subject Pronoun Mapping] ---------------+ |
|   - Locked Position 2 Verb                   - Pronoun-Verb suffix agreement                  | |
|   - S-V-O default sentence template          - Negation syntax (nicht)                        | |
|                                                                                               | |
|                                                      |                                        | |
|                                                      v                                        | |
|                                                                                               | |
|   [WEEK 2: Frozen Transit Chunks] ---------> [WEEK 4: Accusative masculine shift] <-----------+ |
|   - mit dem Bus, mit der U-Bahn              - den / einen / keinen                           |
|   - da / nicht da copula status              - Temporal V2 inversion                          |
|                                                                                               |
|                                                      |                                        |
|                                                      v                                        |
|                                                                                               |
|                                              [WEEK 5: Dative singular baseline]               |
|                                              - Fixed prepositions (mit, zu, bei, seit)        |
|                                              - Static locations (Wo? + Dative in/an/auf)      |
|                                                                                               |
|                                                      |                                        |
|                                                      v                                        |
|                                                                                               |
|                                              [WEEK 6: System Synthesis & Graduation]          |
|                                              - Modals / Separable split brackets              |
|                                              - Location (Wo? + Dat) vs. Movement (Wohin? + Acc)|
|                                                                                               |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

```

### 1. Pronoun and Verb Agreement Dependencies

- **Pronouns & Suffixes (Week 3):** Directly dependent on **Week 1 identity frames** (*ich bin, Sie sind*). The concept of subject-verb matching is expanded from irregular copulas to the regular present-tense suffix paradigm (*-e, -st, -t, -en*).
- **Irregular Mutation (Week 3):** Stem-vowel mutation (*sprichst/spricht*) builds on the **Week 2 transit/logistics**irregular verb templates (*haben, fahre*), reinforcing that irregular verbs require conscious declarative attention to prevent early fossilization.

### 2. The Case and Determiner Progression

- **Accusative Masculine Shift (Week 4):** Directly dependent on **Week 3 noun gender mapping** (*der, die, das*, *ein, eine, ein*). The learner must have stable nominative article categories before applying the direct-object masculine inflection (*den, einen, keinen, meinen*).
- **Noun Negation *****kein***** (Week 4):** Syntactically paired with **Week 3 verb negation (*****nicht*****)** to create a contrastive negation schema, preventing the common adult error of using *nicht* to negate nouns (e.g., *"Ich habe nicht Schlüssel"*).
- **Dative Singular Baseline (Week 5):** Directly dependent on **Week 4 Accusative case boundary** and **Week 2 pre-scaffolded chunks**. The unanalyzed transit formulas from Week 2 (*mit dem Bus*, *mit der U-Bahn*) are opened up and analyzed, showing how dative prepositions (*mit, zu, bei, seit*) systematically trigger singular dative inflections (*dem, der, dem*).
- **Location vs. Movement (Week 6):** The ultimate synthesis of **Week 5 dative space (Wo? + Dative)** and **Week 4 accusative direction (Wohin? + Accusative)**. The learner contrastively applies dative articles for stationary status and accusative articles for directed movement.

### 3. Syntax and Bracket Operations

- **Modal & Separable Split Brackets (Week 6):** Directly dependent on the **Week 4 request frames** (*Können Sie...*, *Ich möchte...*) and **V2 temporal inversion** (*heute, morgen, jetzt*). The learner consolidates their ability to lock the conjugated verb in Position 2 and send the unmodified infinitive or separable prefix (*an, ab*) to the absolute end of the clause.

---

# A1 DEFERRED-TO-A2 LIST

To prevent cognitive bottlenecking and protect the beginner's limited working memory during early spontaneous communication, the following features are **deliberately postponed** to the A2 syllabus:

1. **Dative Plural Inflections (*****den ... -n*****):**
   - *Why:* Requires the learner to simultaneously calculate both article changes and morphophonemic noun modifications (adding an `-n` suffix to the plural noun, with exceptions). This has excessive element interactivity for the A1 stage.
2. **Dative Pronoun *****dir***** (Informal "You"):**
   - *Why:* To avoid register and pronoun interference. Focus is placed exclusively on mastering *mir* (for survival requests like *helfen*) and formal *Ihnen* (for professional and retail transactions).
3. **Active Grammatical Assembly of Dative Prepositions *****nach*****, *****von*****, *****aus*****:**
   - *Why:* While learned receptively or in frozen chunks (*aus Spanien*, *nach Hause*), active rule-based assembly is deferred to simplify the active preposition inventory to *mit, zu, bei, seit*.
4. **Dative-Governing Verb *****gefallen*****:**
   - *Why:* Unlike *helfen* or *schmecken*, *gefallen* requires a complex, non-salient inversion of typical thematic roles (the liked item is the grammatical subject, and the person is the dative object), causing retrieval failure during real-time speech production.
5. **Relative Spatial Prepositions (*****unter, über, hinter, neben, zwischen*****):**
   - *Why:* Concentrating active dative space strictly on the four most high-utility survival prepositions (*in, an, auf, vor*) stabilizes locative case assignment before introducing lateral and vertical coordinates.
6. **Rule-Based Paired Positional Verb Contrasts (*****stehen/stellen*****, *****liegen/legen*****):**
   - *Why:* Simultaneous case-calculation and lexical selection of transitive/intransitive verb pairs triggers extreme cognitive load. In A1, all stationary locations are expressed using *sein*, and movements are expressed using un-split directional verbs.
7. **Separable Verb Prefixes (Formal Morphological Rules):**
   - *Why:* Rather than teaching exhaustive prefix lists, A1 focuses exclusively on pre-conjugated, frozen transit chunks (*ankommen, abfahren, anrufen, anfangen*).
8. **Full Modal Verb Conjugation Tables:**
   - *Why:* Restricting active production of *müssen* and *wollen* strictly to the first-person singular (*ich muss, ich will*) and formal query (*Können Sie*) protects early morphosyntactic retrieval.

---

# FINAL 6-WEEK A1 MASTERY CHECKPOINT

Upon graduating from Week 6, the learner must independently demonstrate the following functional "can-do" capabilities:

### 1. UNDERSTAND (Aural & Written Decoding)

- **Phonological Intelligibility:** Correctly identify and distinguish critical German sound contrasts (the smiling /ç/ "ich-laut" vs. the back /x/ "ach-laut", and the front rounded vowels /ʏ/ and /ø:/) in native input.
- **Word Boundary Detection:** Successfully decode rapid German connected speech, identifying blurred dative contractions (*im, am, zum, zur, beim, vom*) and transit announcements.
- **Extensive Reading:** Read un-simplified graded texts with 95% to 98% vocabulary coverage, extracting key transit, scheduling, or operational details without dictionary translating.

### 2. SAY (Spontaneous Speech Production)

- **Locked V2 Syntax:** Consistently place the conjugated finite verb in Position 2 across all declarative statements, questions, and temporal inversions (*Morgen fahre ich...*).
- **Split-Bracket Execution:** Execute the final prefix split on separable verbs (*Ich rufe... an*) and place infinitive verbs at the end of modal request brackets (*Ich muss heute zur Arbeit gehen*).
- **Case Boundary Precision:** Maintain the direct-object masculine accusative shift (*den, einen, keinen, meinen, Ihren*) and assign the static dative case (*im Büro*, *auf der Straße*) correctly in targeted speaking prompts.

### 3. READ & WRITE (Textual Competency)

- **Factual Scanning:** Scan municipal maps, printed transit schedules, and digital office directories to identify opening hours (*geöffnet/geschlossen*), platform numbers, and pricing details.
- **Guided Paragraph Composition:** Write short, coherent 5-line emails or status logs with correct capitalization, regular subject-verb conjugations, and coordinating conjunctions (*und, aber*).

### 4. DO IN REAL LIFE (Practical Autonomy)

- **Supermarket & Café Checkout:** Greet a cashier formally, order food staples, request shopping bags (*eine Tasse, bitte*) or receipts (*eine Quittung, bitte*) using correct cases, decode price totals, and execute tipping etiquette (*Stimmt so!*) [Day 19, 23 core].
- **Pedestrian Navigation:** Ask for and give local directions using directional adverbs (*geradeaus, links, rechts*) and landmarks on a map [Day 8 core].
- **Clinic & Municipal Scheduling:** Call an office, negotiate an appointment slot using dative time expressions (*am, um*), and confirm the booking.

### 5. HANDLE WHEN CONVERSATION GOES WRONG (Strategic Repair)

- **Spontaneous Breakdown Mitigation:** When faced with rapid speech, unexpected questions, or administrative barriers, immediately deploy repair and clarification formulas (*Wie bitte?*, *Langsam, bitte!*, *Noch einmal, bitte!*) within **3 seconds**, keeping the exchange active in German without reverting to English or falling silent.

---

# COMPLETE A1 MASTER CURRICULUM LOCKPOINT

The following final decisions are **permanently locked** across the A1 German syllabus and must govern all subsequent detailed daily lesson plan generation:

1. **Strict 6-Week Temporal Boundary:**
   - The curriculum is fixed at exactly **6 weeks**.
   - The phase structure is locked as: **Phase 1** (Weeks 1–2), **Phase 2** (Weeks 3–4), and **Phase 3** (Weeks 5–6).
   - No separate Phase 4 is permitted.
2. **The Declarative-to-Procedural (D/P) Learning Order:**
   - No discovery-based, inductive, or purely implicit grammar lessons are allowed.
   - Every unit must follow the locked sequence of: **Explicit Declarative Explanation \\(\rightarrow\\) Controlled Proceduralization \\(\rightarrow\\) Task-Based Automatization**.
3. **V2 Syntactic Baseline:**
   - German syntax is introduced via the **Verb-Second (V2)** rule as the structural anchor of the language, establishing Subject-Verb-Object (S-V-O) strictly as a simple default starting template.
4. **Tandem Oral Integration from Day 1:**
   - Speaking, pronunciation (HVPT), and listening must develop in tandem from Day 1.
   - Auditory training must never act as a prerequisite bottleneck that delays active speaking.
5. **Continuous Vocabulary Development:**
   - All rigid quantitative pre-requisite vocabulary thresholds (e.g., mastering exactly 300 lemmas before syntax) are eliminated.
   - Nouns must always be introduced and memorized as a single phonetic block alongside their definite article and plural form (*das Brot, die Brote*).
6. **Flexible TBLT Scaffolding:**
   - Meaning-focused communicative tasks (Willis's TBLT) are reserved strictly for larger, goal-directed task cycles.
   - TBLT is never required for small, bottom-up pronunciation drills, vocabulary flashcards, or controlled grammar practice.
7. **Limited, Practical Mediation:**
   - Mediation at A1 is treated as limited, practical communication support (extracting times, pointing directions on maps, interpreting signs) rather than translation into English.
8. **Elimination of Quantitative Gating:**
   - All mandatory reaction-time barriers (e.g., "within 2 seconds"), percentage gates (e.g., "85% accuracy under speed pressure"), or strict numeric price ceilings (e.g., "up to 100 Euro") are completely removed.
   - Mastery is assessed qualitatively through functional, communicative task completion and strategic communication repair.

---

📅 *Now that the 6-Week A1 Master Curriculum is officially compiled, audited, and locked, we are fully prepared to generate detailed day-by-day lesson plans for Week 1.*




# FINAL QA OVERRIDES — MUST TAKE PRECEDENCE OVER EARLIER WEEKLY DRAFT WORDING

These corrections were explicitly approved after the detailed weekly drafts. They are authoritative wherever they conflict with the weekly draft text.

## Week 1
- Week 1 is locked as the final Weeks 1–2 architecture baseline; no later Phase 2/3 material may be pulled into Week 1 active work.
- Repair formulas and any Day 1–7 content must respect chronological Daily German Core dependencies.
- Day 5 must include the complete German alphabet reference (A–Z plus Ä, Ö, Ü, ß) with simplified learner-friendly pronunciation guidance.
- Day 6 phone-number practice uses realistic variable-length mobile numbers (10–11 digits), not rigid fixed-length targets.
- Week 1 mastery must use qualitative functional criteria; no rigid speed/percentage/reaction-time gates.
- Week 1 sentence-building progression is explicitly linear: words → phrases → questions → short personal sentences → mini-dialogues.
- Pronunciation guidance must be learner-friendly and practical; avoid unnecessary IPA/academic phonetics unless explicitly needed.

## Week 2
- Day 13 must NOT require future Core item “die Verspätung”. Use already introduced repair/apology language such as “Entschuldigung!” / “Es tut mir leid!” with already available language.
- Keep modal request brackets and separable present-tense verb splits explicitly distinct grammatical operations.
- Day 10 bakery practice must not force active accusative calculation before its scheduled introduction. Use “ein Brot”, “eine Cola”, or teach “einen Kaffee” only as a frozen unanalyzed chunk if used.
- Day 14 graduation must be two realistic tasks:
  A) Commuter update
  B) Bakery counter
  rather than one overloaded combined scene.
- When negating a status adjective, explain simply that “nicht” directly precedes the adjective, e.g. “Das Bürgeramt ist nicht geöffnet.”
- Week 2 is APPROVED & LOCKED; do not introduce Week 3 material.

## Week 3
- Remove arbitrary “within 5 seconds” speed requirements; use untimed/unpressured accuracy and qualitative performance.
- Keep active sentence-building focused on the appropriate singular/formal forms at this stage; do not prematurely expand the productive workload.
- Remove the unreliable “-el = masculine” gender shortcut. Learn nouns with article; use only genuinely reliable patterns such as natural gender and -in for feminine forms.
- Correct Daily German Core chronology:
  Day 17 = “schicken”
  Day 18 = “das Geld”
  Day 19 = “Bezahlen, bitte!”
  Day 20 = “Stimmt so!”
- Keep Day 20 active possessives in nominative forms (e.g. mein/meine/dein/deine/Ihr/Ihre) rather than prematurely forcing masculine accusative forms such as meinen.
- Correct “Sie lerne heute nicht, sie arbeitet.” to “Sie lernt heute nicht, sie arbeitet.”
- Avoid technical IPA terminology in learner-facing instructions when a practical explanation is sufficient.
- Week 3 is APPROVED & LOCKED.

## Week 4
- Remove forward-dependent Day 30 Core nouns “der Kollege / die Kollegin” from earlier active production; use already introduced names or vocabulary.
- Replace “Gute Besserung für die Kollegin!” with natural “Gute Besserung!” / “Gute Besserung, Sarah!”.
- Replace low-value “einen Euro haben” sentence-building targets with higher-utility combinations such as “einen Schlüssel suchen” or “einen Drucker haben”.
- Correct “Es tut mir leid, Ich verstehe nicht.” to “Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht.”
- Explain temporal fronting without academic jargon: if a time word such as heute/morgen/jetzt is first, the finite verb stays in Position 2 and the subject follows it, e.g. “Heute fange ich die Arbeit an.”
- Replace overly technical pronunciation terminology/IPA with practical learner-friendly explanations.
- Do not teach “nicht” as a universal “always at the end” rule; before an infinitive/prefix, position it directly before that final element as appropriate, e.g. “Ich möchte heute nicht arbeiten.”
- Time allocation must remain varied by task/cognitive need, not an identical template every day.
- Week 4 is APPROVED & LOCKED.

## Week 5
- Day 33: use simple pronunciation guidance for “helfen” and “Ihnen”; avoid IPA-heavy learner-facing wording.
- Day 34: “seit zwei Tagen” is a frozen/pre-assembled chunk, not active plural-dative mastery.
- Day 35: use practical descriptions for “zu/zum/zur”, “rechts”, “links”, “Zug”, “Geld”; avoid IPA strings.
- Day 29: replace unnatural “Ich bin am Drucker fertig” with natural separate clauses such as “Ich bin fertig. Ich bin am Drucker.” / “Ich bin fertig. Ich bin am Tisch.”
- Day 30: split awkward “mit dem Kollegen mit dem Zug” into natural clauses such as “Ich fahre mit dem Kollegen. Wir fahren mit dem Zug.” / “Ich fahre mit der Kollegin. Wir fahren mit dem Bus.”
- Day 31: replace unnatural “Ist das Gleis geöffnet?” with natural “Wo ist Gleis vier, bitte?”; “zu” is not presented as a universal movement rule.
- Day 32: formal imperative forms “Gehen Sie …”, “Biegen Sie … ab” are verb-first (V1), not V2.
- Week 5 corrections are APPROVED & LOCKED.

## Global / all weeks
- Exactly 6 weeks / 42 days.
- Phase 1 = Weeks 1–2; Phase 2 = Weeks 3–4; Phase 3 = Weeks 5–6.
- Do not create a separate Phase 4.
- V2 is the German main-clause structural anchor; SVO is only a beginner default.
- Speaking, listening, pronunciation begin from Day 1.
- No rigid reaction-time/percentage/trial-count mastery gates.
- Use qualitative, functional advancement criteria.
- Daily German Core remains a separate lightweight reinforcement layer.
- Do not introduce A2/B1/B2 material into this A1 curriculum.
- Use chunks where intentionally selected; do not force exhaustive grammar analysis.
- Nouns should generally be learned with article and useful plural information when actively taught.
- Pronunciation is a high priority, but do not turn A1 into an academic phonetics course.
- Avoid crude English transliterations as the authoritative pronunciation system.
