## DAY 11: Assessing Operational Hours (Open vs. Closed) and Facility Navigation

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to identify and declare whether public facilities and offices are open (*geöffnet*) or closed (*geschlossen*) using the copula *sein*, and navigate office coordinate displays.

### 2. Main Curriculum Lesson Content

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the status adjectives *geöffnet* (open) and *geschlossen* (closed) [Day 11 core].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit explanation of how these status adjectives function as predicate modifiers after the verb *sein*. They observe that *nicht* stands directly before adjectives to negate them (*ist nicht geöffnet*) [Day 11 core].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner scans printed notice boards and office listings to identify operational status [Day 11 core].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves local landmarks (*der Bahnhof*) and workplace coordinates (*hier/dort*) [Day 6, 8 core].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Card-sorting drills matching landmarks with their status indicators (*geöffnet* vs. *geschlossen*).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work where students ask each other if specific office rooms are currently accessible [Day 11 core].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Verifying office hours outside a German municipal registry (*Bürgeramt*) [Unit 3].
- **REVISIT:** Focused pronunciation of the vocalic rounded Umlaut in *geöffnet* [Day 11 core].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **geöffnet** (Open / Adjective) [Day 11 core]
- **geschlossen** (Closed / Adjective) [Day 11 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The front rounded vowel **"ö" (/œ/)** in *geöffnet* and the sibilant **"sch" (/ʃ/)** in *geschlossen* [Day 11 core].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /œ/ (Round lips tightly as if to say "o", but try to pronounce the English vowel "e"—similar to the vowel in the English word "hurt" but shorter and more rounded).
  2. *Syllable:* /gəˈœf.nət/
  3. *Word:* *geöffnet* [gəˈœfnət] [Day 11 core].
  4. *Phrase:* *ist geöffnet* [ɪst gəˈœfnət].
  5. *Sentence:* *Der Bahnhof ist geöffnet.* [Day 8, 11 core].
  6. *Connected Speech:* *Guten Tag. Der Bahnhof ist heute geöffnet.* [Day 11, 12 core].

### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **Predicate Adjective Modifier Syntax:** In German, adjectives showing the status of a facility (*geöffnet / geschlossen*) follow the copula *ist/sind* [Day 11 core].
- **Adjective Negation:** To negate an adjective, the negative adverb **nicht** is placed directly *before* the adjective modifier [Day 11 core]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|c} \text{Subject (Pos 1)} & \text{Verb (Pos 2: Locked)} & \text{Negation (Pos 3)} & \text{Adjective Modifier (Pos 4)} \ \hline \text{Das Büro} & \text{ist} & \text{nicht} & \text{geöffnet.} \ \text{Der Bahnhof} & \text{ist} & & \text{geschlossen.} \end{array}$$

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Status Extraction:** Listen to three different telephone voicemail messages from local offices. Write down whether each office is declared open (*geöffnet*) or closed (*geschlossen*) on a worksheet [Day 11 core].
- **Acoustic Sound Contrast:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *geschlossen* vs. *schließen*. Focus on identifying the voiceless alveolar fricative sequence.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Transformation Drills (Positive to Negated):** Convert positive status statements into negated adjective structures:
  - *Das Büro ist geöffnet.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das Büro ist nicht geöffnet.* [Day 11 core]
  - *Der Bahnhof ist geöffnet.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Der Bahnhof ist nicht geöffnet.* [Day 8, 11 core]
- **Landmark Reporting:** Point to visual cards of a library, registry, or station with "Open" signs and state: *Das Bürgeramt ist geöffnet.* [Unit 3].

### 8. Reading Practice

- The student scans a simple printed status display outside a language school [Day 11 core]:
  - *Büro 101: geöffnet. Büro 102: geschlossen. Der Bahnhof: geöffnet.*
- The student translates this layout into complete spoken German V2 statements.

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Status Composition:** Write down three sentences describing the status of different public spaces today:
  1. `Der Bahnhof ist geöffnet.` [Day 8, 11 core]
  2. `Das Büro ist geschlossen.` [Day 11 core]
  3. `Das Handy ist hier.` [Day 6 core]

### 10. Mandatory Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Sentence Transformation (Positive to Negated Status) [Day 11 core].
- **Execution:** Negate positive facility status reports using *nicht* in the correct syntactic position:
  - *Das Büro ist geöffnet.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das Büro ist nicht geöffnet.* [Day 11 core]
  - *Der Bahnhof ist geöffnet.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Der Bahnhof ist nicht geöffnet.* [Day 8, 11 core]
  - *Der Schlüssel ist hier.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Der Schlüssel ist nicht hier.* [Day 6 core, Unit 11]

### 11. Retrieval from Week 1

- The learner retrieves the noun *das Büro* and the spatial adverbs *hier/dort* [Day 6 core, Unit 11] to locate open or closed administrative facilities.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Municipal Desk Check:** The learner roleplays arriving at a local German registry office (*Bürgeramt*) [Unit 3]. They approach the entrance, verify whether the registration desk is open or closed, and politely inquire if the coordinator is present [Day 11 core, Unit 11]:
  - *Learner:* "Entschuldigung. Ist das Büro geöffnet? — Nein, das Büro ist heute geschlossen. — Ah, das Büro ist nicht geöffnet. Danke. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Days 1, 4, 5, 11 core, Unit 11]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the entry guard points to a different door and speaks rapidly, the learner deploys: **"Wie bitte?"** [Day 3 core] to prompt a simplified direction in German.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Guard):* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie das Bürgeramt?" [Unit 3]
- *Learner:* "Ja. Ist das Büro geöffnet?" [Day 11 core]
- *Native (Guard):* "Nein, das Büro ist geschlossen." [Day 11 core]
- *Learner:* "Oh, geschlossen. Danke. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Days 5, 11 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Graded Reader Preview:** The learner spends 15 minutes scanning a highly simplified A1 German text about city life. They do not translate; they simply scan and locate the status adjectives *geöffnet* and *geschlossen* in the text.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can identify and declare the operational status of local offices using *geöffnet* and *geschlossen* [Day 11 core].
  - Can accurately negate predicate adjectives using *nicht* in the correct V2 position [Day 11 core].

---

### WEEK 2, DAY 11 STUDY TIME ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *geöffnet*, *geschlossen*) [Day 11 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Predicate adjective modifiers, V2 negation structure, office directories) [Day 11 core].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing the front rounded /œ/ in *geöffnet*; 60 mins: scanning notice boards; 45 mins: positive-to-negative status conversion drills).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Walking past local shops and silently classifying them as *geöffnet* or *geschlossen* in German).

---

## DAY 12: Temporal Coordination, Calendar Scheduling, and the Appointment Inquiry

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to coordinate simple dates and times using the temporal adverbs *heute* (today) and *morgen*(tomorrow), ask *Wann...?* questions with V2 syntax, and schedule appointments [Day 12 core].

### 2. Main Curriculum Lesson Content

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the temporal adverbs *heute* and *morgen* [Day 12 core], alongside the time-inquiry question word *Wann* (When) [Day 12 core].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit grammatical guide on W-Question structures starting with *Wann*. They observe that the verb remains locked in Position 2, while the subject pronoun moves to Position 3.
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to calendar voice memos and schedules corresponding dates on a grid sheet [Day 12 core].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves the formal appointment noun *der Termin* and number coordinates from previous days [Day 10 core, Unit 21].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Match-up exercises connecting temporal prompts with calendar boxes (*heute* \\(\rightarrow\\) Monday, *morgen* \\(\rightarrow\\) Tuesday).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work where students roleplay setting up study sessions on different days [Day 12 core].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Calling a registration clerk and verifying the day of their appointment.
- **REVISIT:** Rehearsing the vowel length in *heute* [Day 12 core].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Wann geht es?** (When is it possible? / Sentence Frame) [Day 12 core]
- **heute / morgen** (Today / Tomorrow / Temporal Adverbs - Reinforced) [Day 12 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The German diphthong **"eu" (/ɔʏ/)** in *heute* and the soft vocalic **"r"** in *morgen* [Day 12 core].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ɔʏ/ (Open mouth as if to say "o", and glide quickly into "ee"—similar to the vowel in the English word "boy" but more rounded at the start).
  2. *Syllable:* /hɔʏ.tə/
  3. *Word:* *heute* [ˈhɔʏtə] [Day 12 core].
  4. *Phrase:* *ist heute* [... ɪst ˈhɔʏtə].
  5. *Sentence:* *Der Termin ist heute.* [Day 12 core, Unit 21].
  6. *Connected Speech:* *Wann geht es? — Morgen passt gut.* [Day 12 core, Unit 5].

### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **W-Question Time Inquiry Syntax:** In German main clauses starting with the question word **Wann** (When), the verb remains locked in the V2 Position 2 anchor, while the subject pronoun moves to Position 3 [Day 12 core]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c} \text{Position 1 (W-Word)} & \text{Position 2 (Verb: Locked)} & \text{Position 3 (Subject)} \ \hline \text{Wann} & \text{ist} & \text{der Termin?} \ \text{Wann} & \text{kommen} & \text{Sie?} \end{array}$$

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Schedule Tracking:** Listen to three different telephone appointments. Write down whether each appointment is scheduled for *heute* (today) or *morgen* (tomorrow) by checking the correct box [Day 12 core].
- **Connected Speech Recognition:** Identify how the final consonant of *ist* links with the starting diphthong of *heute*in connected native speech.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Question-Answer Drills:** Prompt: *"When is the appointment?"* $\rightarrow$ Learner: *Wann ist der Termin?*[Day 12 core, Unit 21]. Prompt: *"The appointment is today."* $\rightarrow$ Learner: *Der Termin ist heute.* [Day 12 core].
- **Interactive Scheduling:** Alternate with your partner, asking *Wann geht es?* and proposing days [Day 12 core].

### 8. Reading Practice

- The student reads a basic printed calendar itinerary [Day 12 core]:
  - *Montag: Termin beim Arzt (heute). Dienstag: Termin im Büro (morgen).*
- The student summarizes aloud: *Der Termin ist heute. Der Termin ist morgen.* [Day 12 core].

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Guided Schedule Composition:** Complete the short calendar confirmation email:
  - `Guten Tag. Ich habe einen Termin.` [Unit 21]
  - `Der Termin ist morgen.` [Day 12 core]
  - `Danke.`

### 10. Mandatory Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Question and Answer Pairings [Day 12 core].
- **Execution:** Formulate time-inquiry questions and answer them using temporal adverbs:
  - *Termin / ist / Wann / der* $\rightarrow$ *Wann ist der Termin?* [Day 12 core, Unit 21]
  - *Termin / heute / ist / Der* $\rightarrow$ *Der Termin ist heute.* [Day 12 core]
  - *geht / Wann / es* $\rightarrow$ *Wann geht es? — Morgen geht es.* [Day 12 core]

### 11. Retrieval from Week 1

- The learner retrieves the formal appointment noun *der Termin* [Unit 21] and greetings from Week 1 to frame their written confirmations.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Clinic Appointment Check:** The learner roleplays calling a German doctor's clinic (*Arztpraxis*) to verify their registration [Day 12 core, Unit 11]. They must greet the receptionist, verify their appointment date using *Wann ist...*, and confirm their slot:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich habe einen Termin. Wann ist der Termin? — Der Termin ist morgen. — Ah, morgen. Danke. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Days 1, 5, 12 core, Unit 11, 21]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the receptionist states that the slot is already taken, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Wann geht es?"** [Days 3, 12 core] to request an alternative day.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Receptionist):* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie einen Termin?" [Unit 21]
- *Learner:* "Ja. Wann geht es?" [Day 12 core]
- *Native (Receptionist):* "Morgen ist frei. Passt das?" [Day 12 core, Unit 5]
- *Learner:* "Ja, morgen passt gut. Danke!" [Day 12 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Interactive Calendar Review:** The learner navigates a simplified German online calendar or booking portal. They practice selecting appointment slots and reading time coordinates in German without dictionary translation.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can ask "Wann ist...?" and "Wann geht es?" using correct V2 question word order [Day 12 core].
  - Can confirm dates using temporal adverbs *heute* and *morgen* appropriately [Day 12 core].

---

### WEEK 2, DAY 12 STUDY TIME ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *Wann geht es?*, *heute/morgen*) [Day 12 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (W-Question time syntax, temporal adverbs, scheduling frames) [Day 12 core].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing diphthong /ɔʏ/ transitions; 60 mins: transcribing spoken schedules; 45 mins: mock telephone booking rehearsals).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing saying "heute / morgen" aloud while planning their real daily schedule).

## DAY 13: Commuting Logistics, Unanalyzed Transit Chunks, and the Delay Report

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to state transit methods using unanalyzed dative chunks (**mit dem Bus**, **mit der U-Bahn**), report travel delays using the adjective *zu spät* with the copula *sein*, and express reassurance (*Kein Problem!*) [Day 13 core, Unit 6].

### 2. Main Curriculum Lesson Content

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the transit chunks **mit dem Bus** (by bus), **mit dem Zug** (by train), and **mit der U-Bahn** (by subway) [Unit 6], and the delay modifier *zu spät* [Unit 6].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit guideline explaining that transit prepositions govern the dative case. **Crucially, dative rules are bypassed at this level!** Transit expressions are memorized strictly as **unanalyzed, frozen lexical chunks** to protect working memory.
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to station updates and identifies which vehicle is reported as delayed (*zu spät*) [Unit 6].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves transit nouns (*der Zug*, *der Bus*) and greetings from previous days [Day 8 core].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Gap-fill exercises matching travel plans with correct transit chunks (*Ich fahre mit dem Zug*).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work where students roleplay commuters updating each other on travel schedules [Unit 6].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Sending a text update to a classmate explaining a transit delay.
- **REVISIT:** Focused pronunciation of the terminal devoiced "g" in *Zug* [Unit 4].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Kein Problem!** (No problem! / Interaction Formula) [Day 13 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Liaison and vowel transitions in **"mit dem"** and **"mit der"** [Unit 6].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /d/ (Voiced dental consonant—pronounced smoothly without heavy aspiration, flowing directly from the preceding "t" in *mit*).
  2. *Word:* *mit dem* [mɪt de\:m] $\rightarrow$ *mit der* [mɪt de:ɐ̯] [Unit 6].
  3. *Phrase:* *mit dem Bus* [mɪt de\:m bʊs] [Unit 6].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich fahre mit dem Zug.* [Unit 6].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Entschuldigung, der Zug ist heute zu spät. — Kein Problem!* [Day 13 core, Unit 6].

### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **Strict Lexical Chunking for Transit:** In German, expressing *how* you travel uses the preposition *mit* (with) [Unit 6]. To bypass dative paradigm tables at this beginner stage, memorize these expressions as **single, frozen lexical chunks** [268, Unit 6]:
  - **mit dem Bus** (by bus) [Unit 6]
  - **mit dem Zug** (by train) [Unit 6]
  - **mit der U-Bahn** (by subway) [Unit 6]
- **The V2 Baseline:** The verb *fahren* (to travel/drive) is locked in Position 2 [Unit 1].

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Delay Tracking:** Listen to train station voice announcements. Circle whether the transit methods are reported on time or delayed (*zu spät*) on a sheet [Unit 6].
- **Chunck Identification:** Listen to spoken commuter updates. Identify the exact transit chunk used by writing it down.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Substitution Drills:** The instructor prompts: *"train"* $\rightarrow$ Learner: *Ich fahre mit dem Zug.* [Unit 6]. *"subway"* $\rightarrow$ Learner: *Ich fahre mit der U-Bahn.* [Unit 6].
- **Delay Reporting:** Practice saying *Der Zug ist zu spät* [Unit 6] with a natural, apologetic falling intonation.

### 8. Reading Practice

- The student reads an office chat update [Unit 6]:
  - *Alex: Guten Tag. Ich komme heute mit dem Bus. Der Bus ist zu spät.*
  - *Chef: Kein Problem! Bis morgen!* [Day 13, 14 core].
- The student underlines the frozen transit chunks and highlights the delay status *zu spät*.

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Guided Transit Composition:** Write a short commuter update message to your teacher:
  - `Guten Tag. Ich fahre mit der U-Bahn.` [Unit 6]
  - `Die U-Bahn ist heute zu spät. Entschuldigung!` [Day 4 core, Unit 6]

### 10. Mandatory Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Transit Chunk Substitutions [Unit 6].
- **Execution:** Create parallel transit statements by substituting different unvoiced chunks:
  - *Ich fahre \_\_\_\_\_\_ (by train).* $\rightarrow$ *Ich fahre ****mit dem Zug****.* [Unit 6]
  - *Ich fahre \_\_\_\_\_\_ (by bus).* $\rightarrow$ *Ich fahre ****mit dem Bus****.* [Unit 6]
  - *Ich fahre \_\_\_\_\_\_ (by subway).* $\rightarrow$ *Ich fahre ****mit der U-Bahn****.* [Unit 6]

### 11. Retrieval from Week 1

- The learner retrieves the formal apology *Entschuldigung für die Verspätung* [Day 18 core] from Week 1 to open their delay report politely.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Late Commuter Update:** The learner roleplays calling their vocational classmate to report a transit delay on their way to school [Unit 6]. They must apologize, state their current transit method, explain the delay using *zu spät*, and coordinate:
  - *Learner:* "Entschuldigung für die Verspätung! Ich fahre mit dem Zug. Der Zug ist heute zu spät. — Kein Problem! — Danke. Bis morgen!" [Days 2, 13 core, Unit 6]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the partner expresses confusion, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte?"** [Day 3 core] to prompt a repetition, ensuring they negotiate meaning successfully.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Classmate):* "Hallo. Wo bist du?" [Day 2 core]
- *Learner:* "Ich fahre mit dem Bus. Der Bus ist zu spät." [Unit 6]
- *Native (Classmate):* "Ah, kein Problem! Der Unterricht ist morgen." [Day 13 core, Unit 12]
- *Learner:* "Super, danke. Tschüss!" [Day 2 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Transit Vlogging Experience:** The learner watches a short 5-minute video of a German train commute or transit guide. They focus on identifying how the host pronounces *Bahnhof*, *Zug*, and *U-Bahn* in connected speech, noting the visual cues of transit displays.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can state their transit method using unanalyzed chunks (*mit dem Bus/Zug/U-Bahn*) with correct V2 syntax [Unit 6].
  - Can report travel delays using *zu spät* and respond reassuringly using *Kein Problem!* [Day 13 core, Unit 6].

---

### WEEK 2, DAY 13 STUDY TIME ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *Kein Problem!*) [Day 13 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Unanalyzed transit chunking, copula status with *zu spät*, commuter updates) [Unit 6].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing liaison in *mit dem / mit der*; 60 mins: transcribing train station announcements; 45 mins: writing and sending delay updates).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Self-talk: narrating their commute or travel plans using the target transit chunks).

---

## DAY 14: Week 2 Comprehensive Synthesis and Formal Checkout Simulation

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to synthesize all Week 1 and Week 2 grammatical, lexical, and phonetic coordinates to execute a multi-stage, interactive checkout transaction and transit delay report entirely in German.

### 2. Main Curriculum Lesson Content

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor reviews all Week 2 structural and pragmatic coordinates, focusing on the *sein/haben*expansion, numbers up to 20, transit chunks, and spatial adverbs [Unit 40].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit synthesis flowchart showing how greetings, appointments, transactions, and transit methods link together in a cohesive communicative loop.
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner scans a complete written transaction dialogue and highlights any V2 word order errors or register mismatches.
- **RETRIEVE:** Systematic spaced recall of all Core and main curriculum vocabulary from Weeks 1–2.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Reordering scrambled dialogues containing scheduling, purchase, and commuting updates.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work executing the complete Week 2 checkout simulation from start to finish.
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching the formal checkout examiner to complete the Week 2 Milestone Task.
- **REVISIT:** Final pronunciation audit of terminal devoicings and Umlauts before the test.

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Bis morgen!** (See you tomorrow! / Formal parting / Interaction Formula) [Day 14 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Consolidating **terminal devoicing**, **Umlauts**, and **connected speech liaison** across all target Week 2 items [Unit 40].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ʏ/ $\rightarrow$ /œ/ $\rightarrow$ /ts/ $\rightarrow$ /ʃ/ (Reviewing rounded vowels, affricates, and shushing sounds).
  2. *Word:* *Zug* [tsu\:k] $\rightarrow$ *Geld* [gɛlt] $\rightarrow$ *Bahnhof* [ˈba\:nho\:f] (Crisp devoiced terminal releases).
  3. *Phrase:* *mit dem Bus* $\rightarrow$ *ist geöffnet* (Smooth transitions without choppy gaps).
  4. *Sentence:* *Der Bahnhof ist heute geöffnet.* [Day 8, 11, 12 core].
  5. *Connected Speech:* Deliver the entire travel and checkout presentation as a fluid, connected breathing unit with natural intonation.

### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **The V2 Syntactic Anchor:** Regardless of how many elements are added (times, places, greetings), the conjugated verb is permanently locked in Car 2 (Position 2) of a German main clause [Unit 1]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c} \text{Car 1: Element} & \text{Car 2: Verb (Locked)} & \text{Car 3: Subject/Object} \ \hline \text{Heute} & \text{ist} & \text{der Bahnhof geöffnet.} \ \text{Morgen} & \text{habe} & \text{ich einen Termin.} \ \text{Wann} & \text{geht} & \text{es?} \end{array}$$

### 6. Listening Practice

- **C-Test Transcription:** Listen to a multi-stage native dialogue recorded at a train station. Fill in the missing letters and numbers on your worksheet, focusing on transcribing double-digit prices, times, and transit status.
- **Register Validation:** Audit three audio clips and confirm if the speakers maintain consistent formal register (*Sie/sind/haben*).

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **The Roleplay rehearsal:** Practice reciting the complete, multi-stage checkout and transit dialogue with natural sentence stress.
- **Rapid Retrieval Round:** Immediately answer prompt cards showed by the instructor:
  - *"20 €"* $\rightarrow$ *Das macht zwanzig Euro.* [Day 10 core]
  - *"Open"* $\rightarrow$ *Das Büro ist geöffnet.* [Day 11 core]

### 8. Reading Practice

- The student reads an un-simplified graded extensive text containing 98% familiar vocabulary:
  - *Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich wohne in Berlin. Heute fahre ich mit dem Zug zur Arbeit. Der Bahnhof ist geöffnet. Ich habe heute einen Termin um zehn Uhr. Morgen ist das Büro geschlossen. Bis morgen!*
- The student identifies and verifies all V2 positions in the text [Unit 1].

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Essay Composition:** The learner writes their own daily commuting and scheduling log (5 sentences) in their notebook, ensuring correct capitalization, spelling, and perfect verb-second syntax.

### 10. Mandatory Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** The Mini-Dialogue Creation (Week 2 Synthesis) [Unit 40].
- **Execution:** Construct a complete, coherent conversation between a commuter and a ticket agent, combining Week 1 and Week 2 language coordinates:
  - *Commuter:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John."
  - *Agent:* "Guten Tag. Haben Sie einen Termin?"
  - *Commuter:* "Ja. Wann ist der Termin? — Der Termin ist heute. — Ist das Büro geöffnet? — Ja, es ist geöffnet. — Danke!"

### 11. Retrieval from Week 1

- The learner systematically retrieves greetings, name spelling, country of origin, and repair formulas from Week 1 to populate the milestone interaction.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Travel and Bakery Purchase (Milestone Task):** The learner roleplays navigating a complete commuting and purchase cycle in Germany [Unit 40]. They approach an agent at the station counter, verify the station status, order food at the bakery, decode a price total up to 20 Euro, pay cash, and politely bid goodbye:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich fahre mit der U-Bahn. Ist der Bahnhof geöffnet? — Ja, der Bahnhof ist geöffnet. — Ich möchte ein Brot und einen Kaffee, bitte. — Das macht sieben Euro. — Hier sind zehn Euro. Stimmt so! Danke. Bis morgen!" [Days 1, 2, 10, 11, 14, 17, 19, 23 core, Unit 6, 24]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the agent speaks too rapidly, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 3 core] to manage the input velocity without breaking character.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Agent):* "Guten Tag. Bitte schön?"
- *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich habe einen Termin heute. Ist das Büro geöffnet?" [Day 11 core, Unit 21]
- *Native (Agent):* "Nein, das Büro ist geschlossen." [Day 11 core]
- *Learner:* "Oh, geschlossen. Wann geht es?" [Days 11, 12 core]
- *Native (Agent):* "Morgen um zehn Uhr." [Day 12 core]
- *Learner:* "Morgen um zehn. Kein Problem! Bis morgen!" [Days 13, 14 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Extensive Reading Sprint:** The learner reads a short graded story about a traveler's commute through Munich. They focus on enjoying the narrative flow, noting how the structural boundaries of *sein/haben* and transit chunks are naturally embedded in the text.

### 16. Mastery Check

- The learner successfully completes the Day 14 Milestone Task under un-speeded roleplay conditions, demonstrating V2 syntax stability, clear phonetic boundaries, and spontaneous repair deployment [Unit 40].

---

### WEEK 2, DAY 14 STUDY TIME ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 20 minutes (Reviewing all Days 8–14 items) [Day 14 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 40 minutes (Verb-Second syntactic synthesis, transit chunks, cashier dialogues) [Unit 40].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (Mirror presentation, question-answer conversion drills, spelling and phone number dictations, mock testing).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Completing a final self-assessment checklist and reviewing incorrect pronunciation entries).

---

# WEEK 2 REVIEW

### 1. What should now be recognized (Receptive Mastery)

- The difference between the irregular present-tense singular and formal forms of *sein* (*ist/sind*) and *haben*(*habe/haben*) [Unit 11, 17].
- Tricky German pricing announcements and double-digit numbers up to 20 in connected speech [Unit 24].
- The status adjectives *geöffnet* (open) and *geschlossen* (closed) indicating facility availability [Day 11 core].
- The unanalyzed dative transit chunks (**mit dem Bus**, **mit dem Zug**, **mit der U-Bahn**) as single vocabulary elements [Unit 6].
- The temporal coordination adverbs *heute* (today) and *morgen* (tomorrow) [Day 12 core].

### 2. What should now be actively produced (Active Mastery)

- Formulating presence and absence using *sein* + *da / nicht da* [Unit 11].
- Declaring your city of residence using *Ich wohne in...* [Day 8 core].
- Asserting possession using *ich habe* and asking *haben Sie* questions [Unit 17].
- Formulating polite requests using the modal split-bracket structures *Können Sie mir helfen?* and *Schreiben Sie das bitte auf?* with main verbs sent to the end [Day 9 core].
- Counting from 0 to 20 in German with accurate pronunciation of the affricate /ts/ [Unit 4].
- Executing simple retail bakery purchases, decoding price totals, and executing tipping etiquette (*Stimmt so!*) [Day 23 core, Unit 24].
- Asking *Wann...?* questions with perfect V2 syntax (*Wann ist der Termin?*) [Day 12 core, Unit 21].
- Reporting transit methods using unanalyzed chunks (*mit dem Zug*) and travel delays using *zu spät* [Unit 6].

### 3. What should remain mainly receptive

- Full dative or accusative case paradigms beyond fixed, frozen chunks [Unit 13, 26].
- Regular verb conjugations for other pronouns (*du*, *wir*, *ihr*) [Unit 12].
- Advanced numerical coordinates above 20 [Unit 4].

### 4. Practical Abilities Gained

- Can navigate local public transit, buying tickets, resolving delays, and tracking timetables [Unit 6].
- Can conduct cash purchases at bakery counters and supermarkets, managing double-digit price totals [Unit 24].
- Can call offices or clinics to coordinate and verify appointments [Day 12 core].
- Can report workstation tool presence/absence and request administrative write-downs [Day 9 core].

### 5. Weaknesses to Check (Diagnostic Guide)

- **V2 Violations on Temporal Inversion:** Ensure the learner does not place the verb at the end of clauses when starting with time adverbs (e.g., *Heute ich fahre mit dem Bus* instead of *Heute fahre ich mit dem Bus*) [Unit 1].
- **Chunk Breakdown:** Check if the learner is trying to inflect the transit chunks (e.g., saying *mit den Bus* instead of the frozen *mit dem Bus*) [Unit 6].
- **Pronunciation defaults:** Watch for English "z" defaults in *zwanzig* or over-aspiration in *Woher* [Unit 3, 4].

---

# WEEK 2 MASTERY CHECK

This formal evaluation uses **qualitative, functional competence criteria** to determine readiness for Week 3, completely bypassing arbitrary speed, percentage, or trial-count gates [Newton & Nation, 2020].

```
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                 WEEK 2 MASTERY CHECKLIST                                        |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  1. PHONOLOGICAL INTELLIGIBILITY (Receptive & Productive)                                       |
|     -> Can produce distinct rounded Umlauts /œ/ (geöffnet) and sibilant clusters /ts/ (zwanzig).|
|                                                                                                 |
|  2. CANONICAL V2 STABILITY                                                                      |
|     -> Keeps the conjugated verb locked in Position 2 across statements and Wann-questions.     |
|                                                                                                 |
|  3. BRACKET SYNTAX RECOVERY                                                                     |
|     -> Consistently sends the main verb infinitive to the end in Können Sie... request frames.   |
|                                                                                                 |
|  4. LOGISTICAL AND TRANSACTIONAL AUTONOMY (TBLT Task)                                           |
|     -> Purchase tickets, manage transit delays, coordinate appointments, and checkout cash.     |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

```

### 1. Pronunciation Check

- **Target Sound Isolation:** The learner reads aloud the following three sentences, which contain the critical phonetic contrasts of Week 2 [Vaughan-Rees, 2002]:
  1. *Der Bahn-hof ist heu-te ge-öff-net.* (Evaluates German initial /b/ in *Bahnhof*, the /ɔʏ/ diphthong in *heute*, and the /œ/ Umlaut in *geöffnet*).
  2. *Ich fahr-e mit dem Zug nach Ber-lin.* (Evaluates vocalic "r" in *fahre*, the liaison in *mit dem*, and the terminal devoiced "g" in *Zug*).
  3. *Das macht zwan-zig Eu-ro, bit-te.* (Evaluates the affricate /ts/ and "-zig" soft hushing [ç] in *zwanzig*, and uninflected *Euro*).
- **Standard of Success:** Intelligible pronunciation. The learner must show a clear articulatory effort to produce German-specific sibilants and avoid English "w" slurs or flat vowels.

### 2. Functional Communication Check (The Commuter and Bakery Run)

- **The Scenario:** The learner roleplays navigating a complete commuting and purchase cycle in Germany. The examiner acts as the transit agent and cashier [Unit 40].
- **The Task:** The learner must complete a 6-step interactive cycle under natural, un-speeded conditions:
  1. Initiate contact formally, state residence, and inquire about station availability (*Is das Büro geöffnet?*) [Day 8, 11 core].
  2. Decode a spoken time or platform coordinate, and verify an appointment date (*Wann ist der Termin?*) [Day 12 core, Unit 21].
  3. Report a transit delay politely using *Entschuldigung für die Verspätung* and the copula structure *Der Zug ist zu spät* [Unit 6].
  4. State transit method using unanalyzed chunks (**mit dem Zug**, **mit dem Bus**) [Unit 6].
  5. Order bakery staples (*Brot*, *Kaffee*), decode the price total up to 20 Euro, pay cash, and execute tipping etiquette (*Stimmt so!*) [Day 10, 17, 19, 23 core, Unit 24].
  6. Politely close the interaction using *Bis morgen!* or *Auf Wiedersehen* [Day 5, 14 core].
- **Standard of Success:** Conversational success. Minor grammatical slips (such as irregular verb endings) are recast implicitly [Long, 2015]. The learner meets the criteria if meaning is successfully negotiated, the verb remains consistently in Position 2, request frames lock verbs at the end, and any rapid input is slowed down using German repair formulas (*Wie bitte?*) without reverting to English.

---

*The complete Week 2 (Days 8–14) detailed study plan is finalized, audited, and officially locked under the designated design parameters. Stop. Do not proceed to Week 3.*

# WEEK 3 — DAYS 15–21


# WEEK 3 STUDY PLAN: PRONOUNS, PRESENT-TENSE CONJUGATION, NOUN GENDER, ARTICLE AWARENESS, AND PERSONAL COMMUNICATION

Adult second language acquisition (SLA) is optimized when structural instruction aligns with the brain's dual memory systems. In Week 3, we transition from frozen conversational chunks to **systematic grammatical mapping**. By explicitly introducing personal pronouns, regular conjugations, and the noun gender system, we establish stable **declarative models**. We then proceduralize these models through targeted, speed-restricted sentence-building drills and contextualized interaction, guiding learners toward spontaneous communicative automaticity.

---

## WEEK 3 DAILY SCHEDULE (DAYS 15–21)

---

### DAY 15: Singular Personal Pronouns and the Copula *sein* (Unit 11)

#### 1. Objective

The learner will be able to identify themselves and others using the full set of singular personal pronouns (*ich, du, er, sie, es*) and the formal address (*Sie*), pairing them with the correct present-tense conjugated forms of *sein* (*bin, bist, ist, sind*) in V2 statements.

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the singular pronoun system (*ich, du, er, sie, es*) and the formal address (*Sie*), pairing each with its corresponding form of the irregular verb *sein* [Unit 11].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit declarative breakdown of singular pronoun-verb mapping [Unit 11]. They observe that the formal *Sie* (capitalized) is used with strangers and supervisors, while the informal *du* is reserved for peers [Unit 11].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short office greetings and identifies whether the speakers use a formal (*Sie*) or informal (*du*) register based on the conjugated form of *sein* heard [Unit 11].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves the existential presence frames (*da / nicht da*) from Week 2 to report status.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** A quick matching drill where singular pronouns are paired with correct *sein* copulas within 5 seconds [Unit 11].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Seating Chart Navigation. Partners point to a visual seating chart of a German vocational classroom and describe who is present: *Das ist Herr Schmidt. Er ist im Büro. Und du bist John? Du bist auch hier.*[Unit 11].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Conducting an administrative verification round at a school registration office, confirming identities formally [Unit 11].
- **REVISIT:** Rehearsing the phonetic distinction between formal *Sie* [zi:] and informal *du* [du:] before concluding.

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **die Arbeit** (The work / Feminine Noun) [Day 15 core]
- **die E-Mail** (The email / Feminine Noun) [Day 15 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation

- **Focus:** The vocalic **"r"** in *er* [e: Er] and the tense **long vowel /i:/** in *sie/Sie* [zi:] [Unit 11].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /e:ɐ̯/ (Drop the terminal "r" and pronounce it as a soft, relaxed, breathy schwa).
  2. *Word:* *er* [e:ɐ̯] (He) [Unit 11].
  3. *Phrase:* *er ist* [e:ɐ̯ ɪst].
  4. *Sentence:* *Er ist heute da.* [Unit 11].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Das ist Herr Schmidt. Er ist im Büro.* (Ensure a smooth transition without a rolling English "r" sound [Unit 11]).

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **The Copula Paradigm:** The irregular verb *sein* (to be) changes its stem form completely depending on the subject pronoun [Unit 11]. Singular pronouns act as subjects in the Nominative case [Unit 11]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c} \text{Pronoun (Subject)} & \text{sein Copula} & \text{Register / Meaning} \ \hline \text{ich} & \text{bin} & \text{I am} \ \text{du} & \text{bist} & \text{you are (informal)} \ \text{er / sie / es} & \text{ist} & \text{he / she / it is} \ \text{Sie} & \text{sind} & \text{you are (formal)} \end{array}$$
- **V2 Constraint:** Even when changing subjects and verbs, the copula remains firmly anchored in **Position 2** [Unit 11].

#### 6. Listening

- **Bottom-Up Boundary Parsing:** Listen to audio of native speakers uttering *"Du bist da."* and *"Sie sind da."* [Unit 11]. Identify how the terminal consonant of the verb links with the following word in connected speech: /du: bɪstda:/ [Unit 11].
- **Register Detection:** Listen to three office corridor interactions. Identify the social relationship (formal vs. informal) based on the copula used (*bist* vs. *sind*) [Unit 11].

#### 7. Speaking

- **Prompted Oral Drills:** The teacher displays a photo of a female colleague (e.g., *Sarah*) and says: *"Das ist Sarah."*The learner must immediately respond: *"Sie ist hier."* [Unit 11].
- **Workspace Mapping:** Point to empty chairs in a room diagram and declare who is absent: *Herr Wagner ist nicht da. Er ist bei der Arbeit.* [Unit 11].

#### 8. Reading

- Read a brief reception log:
  - *Receptionist: Guten Tag. Ich bin Frau Müller. Sind Sie Herr Wagner?*
  - *Visitor: Guten Tag. Ja, ich bin Herr Wagner. Ist Herr Schmidt da?*
  - *Receptionist: Nein, er ist heute nicht da.* [Unit 11]
- Draw connecting lines between pronouns and their corresponding conjugated verbs.

#### 9. Writing

- **Guided Composition:** Write a 3-sentence description matching photos of three training classmates:
  1. `Das ist Alex. Er ist bei der Arbeit.`
  2. `Das ist Sarah. Sie ist krank.`
  3. `Sie sind Herr Schmidt. Sie sind der Chef.` [Unit 11]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Pronoun Substitution.
- **Execution:** Swap the subject pronouns and adapt the conjugated verb forms of *sein*, maintaining proper V2 syntax:
  - *Ich bin hier.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Sie (formal) \_\_\_\_\_\_ hier.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Sie ****sind**** hier.*
  - *Er ist da.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Du \_\_\_\_\_\_ da.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Du ****bist**** da.*
  - *Sie (she) ist krank.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich \_\_\_\_\_\_ krank.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich ****bin**** krank.*

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–2

- Retrieve the location status markers *da / nicht da* [Unit 11] and greetings (*Guten Tag*, *Hallo*, *Tschüss*) [Day 1, 2 core] to initiate social introductions.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Roster Verification:** The learner roleplays approaching their vocational school supervisor (*Personalchef*) on their first day of training (*Ausbildung*) [Unit 11]. They must greet the supervisor formally, confirm their own identity, verify the supervisor's formal identity, and introduce a classmate:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Sie sind Herr Schmidt? — Ja. — Das ist Alex. Er ist auch hier." [Unit 11]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the supervisor asks for a document spelling, the learner uses the Week 1 non-verbal and echoic repair strategy (rising name intonation: *"John?"*) to prompt confirmation while handing over their passport.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Supervisor):* "Guten Tag. Sind Sie Herr John?" [Unit 11]
- *Learner:* "Ja, ich bin John. Und Sie sind Herr Schmidt?" [Unit 11]
- *Native (Supervisor):* "Richtig. Willkommen bei der Arbeit!" [Unit 11]
- *Learner:* "Danke!" [Day 1 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- Spend 15 minutes listening to a German interview with a professional trainer. Focus entirely on tracking how many times the formal pronoun *Sie* and its matching copula *sind* are used in conversation [Unit 11].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can conjugate *sein* for all singular subject pronouns and formal *Sie* without morphological errors [Unit 11].
  - Can navigate a formal introduction block maintaining consistent registers [Unit 11].

---

### DAY 15 TIME BUDGET (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, adding *die Arbeit*, *die E-Mail*) [Day 15 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Singular personal pronouns, *sein* conjugation, register split theory) [Unit 11].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: mouth-muscle workouts for vocalic "er" [e:ɐ̯]; 60 mins: pronoun-copula physical sorting cards; 45 mins: register recognition exercises).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Mirror self-talk: describing who is present or absent in your household using *er ist*, *sie ist*, *ich bin*).

---

### DAY 16: Present Tense of Regular Verbs and Endings (Unit 12)

#### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to conjugate regular present-tense verbs (*kommen, wohnen, arbeiten, lernen*) for all singular subject pronouns and formal *Sie*, appending the correct morphological suffixes in declarative V2 main clauses.

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the infinitive base verb and regular conjugation suffixes (*-e, -st, -t, -en*) for high-frequency verbs *kommen, wohnen, arbeiten, lernen* [Unit 12].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit declarative rule explaining how to strip the infinitive suffix **-en** to find the verb stem, and append subject-specific suffixes [Unit 12].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short recordings of three introductory dialogues and checks whether the speakers are currently working (*arbeiten*) or studying (*lernen*) based on the verb endings heard [Unit 12].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves transit adverbs (*heute, morgen*) and city coordinates from previous weeks.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical gap-fills matching verb stems with correct suffixes based on pronoun prompts [Unit 12].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Language exchange roleplay. Partners take turns asking and answering: *Wo wohnen Sie, Herr Schmidt? — Ich wohne in Hamburg. Und wo wohnst du, Sarah? — Ich wohne in Berlin.* [Unit 12].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Declaring study and work plans to an administrative coordinator on campus.
- **REVISIT:** Focused articulation of the dental stem expansion in *du arbeitest* at the end of the session [Unit 12].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **der Chef** (The boss / Masculine Noun) [Day 16 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation

- **Focus:** Suffix consolidation and the consonant cluster **"st"** (/st/) in *wohnst* and *lernst* [Unit 12].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /st/ (Crisp alveolar stop /t/ immediately following the voiceless sibilant /s/).
  2. *Word:* *wohnst* [vo\:nst] [Unit 12].
  3. *Phrase:* *wo wohnst du* [vo: vo\:nst du:].
  4. *Sentence:* *Wo wohnst du, Alex?*
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Hallo Alex, wo wohnst du? — Ich wohne in Berlin.* [Unit 12].

#### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **Regular Verb Suffix Paradigm:** To conjugate regular German verbs, strip the infinitive ending **-en** to find the verbal stem, and append subject-specific suffixes [Unit 12]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|l} \text{Subject} & \text{Stem} & \text{Suffix} & \text{Example} \ \hline \text{ich} & \text{wohn-} & \mathbf{-e} & \text{ich wohn}\mathbf{e} \ \text{du} & \text{wohn-} & \mathbf{-st} & \text{du wohn}\mathbf{st} \ \text{er / sie / es} & \text{wohn-} & \mathbf{-t} & \text{er wohn}\mathbf{t} \ \text{Sie} & \text{wohn-} & \mathbf{-en} & \text{Sie wohn}\mathbf{en} \end{array}$$
- **Dental Stem Exception:** If a verb stem ends in a dental consonant **-d-** or **-t-** (like *arbeit-*), insert an **-e-** before the suffix for *du* and *er/sie/es* to make it pronounceable: *du arbeit****e****st, er arbeit****e****t* [Unit 12].

#### 6. Listening

- **Bottom-Up Transcription:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *"Ich lerne heute"* vs. *"Er lernt heute"* [Unit 12]. Transcribe the verb endings to verify your phonetic parsing accuracy.
- **Acoustic Suffix Sorting:** Listen to five spoken sentences and circle the pronoun that matches the verb ending heard (*ich* vs. *er*).

#### 7. Speaking

- **Oral Suffix Drills:** The teacher prompts: *"ich / kommen / aus Spanien"*. The learner says: *"Ich komme aus Spanien."* Teacher prompts: *"er / arbeiten / heute"*. Learner says: *"Er arbeitet heute."* [Unit 12].
- **Class Survey:** Survey classmates' schedules: *Arbeitest du heute, Alex? — Nein, ich lerne heute. Und arbeitet Herr Schmidt? — Ja, er arbeitet heute.* [Unit 12].

#### 8. Reading

- Read a brief personal database profile of three training apprentices [Unit 12]:
  - *Alex kommt aus Kanada. Er wohnt in Berlin. Er lernt heute.*
  - *Sarah kommt aus Spanien. Sie wohnt in Frankfurt. Sie arbeitet heute.*
- Answer written questions based on the profiles: *Wo wohnt Alex?* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Er wohnt in Berlin.* [Unit 12].

#### 9. Writing

- **Guided Composition:** Use a profile grid (Name, Origin, City, Activity) to write three sentences for each person:
  - `Herr Wagner kommt aus Deutschland. Er wohnt in München. Er arbeitet heute.`
  - `Du kommst aus England. Du wohnst in Berlin. Du lernst heute.` [Unit 12]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Verb-Form Substitution.
- **Execution:** Adapt the regular verb endings to match the subject pronoun, keeping V2 syntax secure [Unit 12]:
  - *Ich wohne in Berlin.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Er \_\_\_\_\_\_ in Berlin.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Er ****wohnt**** in Berlin.*
  - *Sie lernen heute.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich \_\_\_\_\_\_ heute.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich ****lerne**** heute.*
  - *Du arbeitest heute.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Wir \_\_\_\_\_\_ heute.* (Receptive) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Wir ****arbeiten**** heute.* [Unit 12]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–2

- Retrieve countries of origin (*Kanada, Spanien, England*) [Day 1 core, Unit 3] and time adverbs (*heute, morgen*) [Day 12 core] to build schedule profiles.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Team Introduction Mingle:** The learner roleplays participating in an introductory round-robin at a new vocational school (*Berufsschule*) [Unit 12]. They must introduce themselves, state their city of residence, and introduce a classmate to the group using correct regular present-tense conjugation:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich wohne in Frankfurt. Das ist Alex. Er kommt aus England. Er wohnt in Frankfurt. Er lernt heute." [Unit 12]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If a classmate speaks too quickly during their introduction, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 3 core] to slow down the speed of processing.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Supervisor):* "Frau Müller, arbeiten Sie heute?" [Unit 12]
- *Learner:* "Nein, ich arbeite heute nicht. Ich lerne heute." [Unit 12]
- *Native (Supervisor):* "Alles klar. Danke!" [Unit 12]
- *Learner:* "Tschüss!" [Day 2 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Spaced Retrieval Booster:** Use a digital language app to complete a 10-item present-tense verb conjugation quiz. Focus strictly on spelling agreement and dental stems (*arbeiten*) [Day 31 core].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can conjugate regular verbs (*kommen, wohnen, lernen, arbeiten*) accurately across all singular pronouns [Unit 12].
  - Can formulate complete V2 schedule descriptions using regular verbs [Unit 12].

---

### DAY 16 TIME BUDGET (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, adding *der Chef*) [Day 16 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Regular conjugation endings, dental stem spelling rules, scheduling adverbs) [Unit 12].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: regular verb suffix conjugation table drills; 45 mins: auditory suffix identification; 45 mins: practicing the dental stem insertion in *arbeitest*).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Narrating your own daily activities aloud: *Ich lerne heute. Mein Chef arbeitet heute.*) [Day 16 core, Unit 12].

### DAY 17: Noun Gender and Definite Articles (Unit 13)

#### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to identify noun gender using the three definite articles (*der, die, das*) and apply semantic and phonetic classification rules (*natural gender, -el, -in*) to map articles to core vocabulary.

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the three definite articles (*der, die, das*) alongside core workplace nouns *der Chef, die Kollegin, der Schlüssel, das Geld* [Unit 13].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit explanation of grammatical gender [Unit 13]. They learn that German nouns must never be memorized as naked words, but always as a **single phonetic block with their definite article** (*das-Geld*) [Unit 13].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner scans a printed inventory checklist and highlights the masculine (*der*), feminine (*die*), and neuter (*das*) articles in different colors [Unit 13].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves workspace nouns from previous weeks (*Drucker, Handy, Bahnhof*).
- **CONTROLLED USE:** A quick card-sorting drill where nouns are sorted under columns labeled *der*, *die*, or *das*[Unit 13].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Point and Declare. Partners look at a workspace diagram and locate resources: *Was ist das? — Das ist der Computer. Und was ist das? — Das ist das Handy.* [Day 6 core, Unit 13].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Filing an equipment report at a school workshop office, identifying missing items by their correct grammatical gender.
- **REVISIT:** Quick oral pronunciation rehearsal of the terminal devoicing in *Geld* [gɛlt] before the end of the day [Unit 13].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **das Brot** (The bread / Neuter Noun) [Day 17 core]
- **der Kaffee** (The coffee / Masculine Noun) [Day 17 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation

- **Focus:** Terminal consonant devoicing (Auslautverhärtung) in **"das Geld"** and the vocalic **"er"** in *der* [Unit 13].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* [t] (The terminal "d" in *Geld* is completely devoiced, sounding like a sharp, voiceless "t").
  2. *Word:* *Geld* [gɛlt] [Unit 13].
  3. *Phrase:* *das Geld* [das gɛlt] [Unit 13].
  4. *Sentence:* *Das Geld ist hier.* [Unit 13].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Der Chef ist da. Das Geld ist hier.* (Maintain clean, crisp terminal devoicings on *Geld* and *Chef* [Unit 13]).

#### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **The Grammatical Gender System:** In German, every noun has an inherent gender: masculine (*der*), feminine (*die*), or neuter (*das*) [Unit 13].
- **The Three A1 Gender Cheats:**
  1. *Natural Gender:* Biological males are masculine (*der Chef, der Kollege*); biological females are feminine (*die Chefin, die Kollegin*) [Unit 13].
  2. *Phonetic Ending -el:* Nouns ending in **-el** are highly likely to be masculine (*der Schlüssel, der Drucker*) [Unit 13].
  3. *Phonetic Suffix -in:* Female professions ending in **-in** are always feminine (*die Kollegin, die Chefin*) [Unit 13].
- Nouns must be learned as an integrated phonetic unit: **Article + Noun** [Unit 13].

#### 6. Listening

- **Bottom-Up Connected Auditory Decoding:** Listen to a native speaker pronouncing *"Wo ist der Schlüssel?"* and *"Wo ist das Geld?"* Notice how the article merges with the verb: /vɔɪstde:ɐ̯ˈʃlʏsəl/ [Unit 13].
- **Top-Down Gist Extraction:** Listen to an office assistant taking inventory. Circle whether the mentioned resources are masculine (*der*), feminine (*die*), or neuter (*das*) on a visual spreadsheet [Unit 13].

#### 7. Speaking

- **Visual Substitution Drills:** The teacher flashes a card showing a key icon \\(\rightarrow\\) Learner says: *"der Schlüssel"*. Money icon \\(\rightarrow\\) Learner says: *"das Geld"*. Female coworker icon \\(\rightarrow\\) Learner says: *"die Kollegin"* [Unit 13].
- **Point and State:** Point to items around your desk and state their location: *Der Drucker ist dort. Das Handy ist hier. Die Kollegin ist da.* [Unit 13].

#### 8. Reading

- Read a simple office checklist:
  - *Status: Der Schlüssel ist hier. Das Geld ist dort. Die Kollegin arbeitet heute. Der Chef ist im Büro.* [Unit 13]
- Categorize the nouns from the checklist into three gender columns.

#### 9. Writing

- **Controlled Noun Composition:** Write four simple sentences describing a workplace scene using definite articles:
  1. `Der Schlüssel ist hier.`
  2. `Die Kollegin ist da.`
  3. `Das Geld ist im Büro.`
  4. `Der Chef arbeitet heute.` [Unit 13]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Noun/Definite Article Substitution.
- **Execution:** Swap the subject nouns and choose the correct definite article (*der, die, das*) based on grammatical gender rules:
  - *Der Drucker ist da.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *\_\_\_\_\_\_ Schlüssel ist da.* \\(\rightarrow\\) ***Der**** Schlüssel ist da.*
  - *Die Kollegin ist krank.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *\_\_\_\_\_\_ Chef ist krank.* \\(\rightarrow\\) ***Der**** Chef ist krank.*
  - *Das Handy ist hier.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *\_\_\_\_\_\_ Geld ist hier.* \\(\rightarrow\\) ***Das**** Geld ist hier.* [Unit 13]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–2

- Retrieve the workspace nouns *Drucker* [Day 26 core], *Schlüssel* [Unit 11], and *Handy* [Day 6 core] to practice article-noun pairing.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Equipment Inventory:** The learner roleplays as a newly hired apprentice checking in at their vocational workspace [Unit 13]. They are given an inventory sheet and must read each item to their supervisor, stating its correct gender and presence:
  - *Learner:* "Herr Schmidt, der Drucker ist hier. Das Geld ist im Büro. Aber der Schlüssel ist nicht da. Haben Sie den Schlüssel?" [Note: *den Schlüssel* is treated as an unanalyzed chunk here; the focus remains on the nominative inventory baseline] [Unit 13].

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the supervisor expresses concern over the missing key, the learner immediately deploys: **"Es tut mir leid. Ich verstehe nicht."** [Day 25 core] to prompt a simplified explanation.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Supervisor):* "Wo ist die Kollegin?" [Unit 13]
- *Learner:* "Die Kollegin ist heute krank." [Unit 13]
- *Native (Supervisor):* "Oh, das tut mir leid." [Unit 13]
- *Learner:* "Danke. Tschüss!" [Day 2 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Virtual Cafe:** Look at a photo of a typical German café counter. Identify five items (such as *das Brot*, *der Kaffee*) and silently pair them with their correct definite article in German [Day 17 core].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- The learner can pair definite articles with high-frequency core nouns, achieving correct gender mapping for at least 8 out of 10 targeted items in a controlled sorting task [Unit 13].

---

### DAY 17 TIME BUDGET (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, adding *das Brot*, *der Kaffee*) [Day 17 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Definite article introduction, gender Hacks [-el, -in, natural gender], article integration) [Unit 13].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: card-sorting definite article drills; 45 mins: practicing terminal devoicing [gɛlt]; 45 mins: point-and-declare spatial routines).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Labeling items around your workspace or kitchen with sticky notes showing their definite articles: *der Schlüssel*, *das Brot*).

### DAY 18: Indefinite Articles in the Nominative (Unit 14)

#### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to introduce novel items using indefinite articles (*ein, eine, ein*) in the Nominative case, pairing them with the demonstrative frame *Das ist ein/eine...* based on noun gender.

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the indefinite articles (*ein, eine, ein*) alongside the demonstrative frame *Das ist ein/eine...* [Unit 14].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit rule connecting indefinite articles to definite articles (*der \\(\rightarrow\\) ein*, *die \\(\rightarrow\\) eine*, *das \\(\rightarrow\\) ein*) [Unit 14]. They observe that masculine and neuter share the exact same indefinite form (*ein*) in the Nominative, while feminine always ends in **-e** (*eine*) [Unit 14].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to native speakers pronouncing *"Das ist ein Drucker"* vs. *"Das ist eine E-Mail"*and identifies how the final schwa vowel in *eine* changes the rhythmic cadence of the sentence [Unit 14].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves definite article associations from Day 17 to determine indefinite article choice.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** A card-matching drill connecting indefinite articles with noun cards (*Drucker* \\(\rightarrow\\) *ein*, *E-Mail* \\(\rightarrow\\) *eine*).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Describing the Workspace. Partners look at a photo of a cluttered desk and describe what they see: *Das ist ein Computer. Das ist ein Schlüssel. Und das ist eine E-Mail.* [Unit 14].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Roleplaying receiving packages at a front desk, declaring what they are using indefinite articles [Unit 14].
- **REVISIT:** Rehearsing the glottal stop (*Knacklaut*) before *ein* to avoid slurred speech transitions [Unit 14].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **die Verspätung** (The delay / Feminine Noun) [Day 18 core]
- **schicken** (To send / Verb) [Day 18 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation

- **Focus:** The diphthong **"ei" (/aɪ/)** in *ein/eine* and the glottal stop (*Knacklaut*) [Unit 14].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /aɪ/ (Open your mouth wide on "a" and glide quickly to "i", keeping the ending of *eine* as a soft, unstressed schwa [ˈaɪnə]).
  2. *Word:* *ein* [aɪn] $\rightarrow$ *eine* [ˈaɪnə] [Unit 14].
  3. *Phrase:* *Das ist ein* [das ɪst ˈ[pause]aɪn] (A small, clean catch in the throat before *ein*).
  4. *Sentence:* *Das ist ein Drucker.* [Unit 14].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Was ist das? — Das ist eine E-Mail.* [Unit 14].

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **Indefinite Articles in the Nominative:** Indefinite articles ("a" or "an") correspond directly to the grammatical gender of the noun [Unit 14]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|l} \text{Gender} & \text{Indefinite Article} & \text{Example} \ \hline \text{Masculine (der)} & \mathbf{ein} & \text{Das ist }\mathbf{ein}\text{ Drucker.} \ \text{Feminine (die)} & \mathbf{eine} & \text{Das ist }\mathbf{eine}\text{ E-Mail.} \ \text{Neuter (das)} & \mathbf{ein} & \text{Das ist }\mathbf{ein}\text{ Handy.} \end{array}$$
- **Nominative Alignment:** At this stage, clauses using *Das ist...* are strictly demonstrative (subject copula), so no Accusative case shifts are applied yet [Unit 14].

#### 6. Listening

- **Bottom-Up Connected Rhythm Parsing:** Listen to native speakers uttering *"Das ist ein Drucker"* vs. *"Das ist eine E-Mail"* [Unit 14]. Notice the rhythmic shift and the clear glottal catch before *ein* and *eine* [Unit 14].
- **Top-Down Delivery Manifest:** Listen to a delivery driver announcing the parcels they are bringing into the office. Check off the correct items on a package manifest list (e.g., a phone, a printer, a card) [Unit 14].

#### 7. Speaking

- **Visual Substitution Drills:** The teacher displays a picture of a phone (*Handy*). Learner says: *"Das ist ein Handy."*Picture of a ticket (*Fahrkarte*). Learner says: *"Das ist eine Fahrkarte."* [Unit 14].
- **Desk Inventory Tour:** Point to objects on your study desk and introduce them to your partner: *Das ist ein Computer. Das ist ein Schlüssel. Und das ist eine E-Mail.* [Unit 14].

#### 8. Reading

- Read a simple dialogue from an un-simplified graded reader:
  - *A: Was ist das? Ist das ein Ticket?*
  - *B: Nein, das ist eine Fahrkarte für den Bus.* [Unit 14]
  - *A: Ah, okay. Und das hier ist ein Handy.* [Unit 14]
- Highlight all indefinite articles and match them to the corresponding noun genders.

#### 9. Writing

- **Controlled Frame Composition:** Write four simple sentences introducing the items on an office desk using the *Das ist ein/eine...* frame [Unit 14]:
  1. `Das ist ein Computer.`
  2. `Das ist eine E-Mail.`
  3. `Das ist ein Schlüssel.`
  4. `Das ist ein Handy.`

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Noun/Article Substitution.
- **Execution:** Convert Definite Article statements into Indefinite Article demonstrative frames based on gender alignment [Unit 14]:
  - *Der Drucker ist hier.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das ist \_\_\_\_\_\_.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das ist ****ein Drucker****.*
  - *Die E-Mail ist da.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das ist \_\_\_\_\_\_.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das ist ****eine E-Mail****.*
  - *Das Handy ist dort.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das ist \_\_\_\_\_\_.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das ist ****ein Handy****.* [Unit 14]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–2

- Retrieve the nouns *Handy* [Day 6 core], *Fahrkarte* [Day 11 core], and *Brot* [Day 17 core] to populate the indefinite article frames.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Parcel Reception:** The learner roleplays receiving a package delivery at their vocational school front desk [Unit 14]. They must greet the courier formally, inspect the delivered items, declare what they are using indefinite articles, and confirm:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Danke. Das ist ein Drucker, das ist ein Computer, und das ist eine E-Mail-Bestätigung." [Unit 14]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the courier mumbles or asks an unexpected delivery question, the learner uses the Week 1 echoic name-verification technique with rising intonation (*"John?"*) or asks for repetition.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Courier:* "Was ist das?" [Unit 14]
- *Learner:* "Das ist ein Schlüssel für das Büro." [Unit 14]
- *Courier:* "Alles klar. Bitte hier unterschreiben." [Unit 14]
- *Learner:* "Vielen Dank! Tschüss!" [Days 1, 2 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Input Flood Technique:** Prompt an AI language tool: *"Give me 10 simple German sentences using 'Das ist ein...' or 'Das ist eine...' describing common office items. Highlight where the glottal stop occurs."* Read them aloud to automate the articulatory catch in the throat [Unit 14].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can accurately select and deploy the correct indefinite article (*ein / eine*) based on noun gender within the demonstrative frame [Unit 14].
  - Can pronounce *ein* and *eine* with correct diphthongs and a clear glottal stop [Unit 14].

---

### DAY 18 TIME BUDGET (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, adding *die Verspätung*, *schicken*) [Day 18 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Indefinite articles in Nominative, demonstrative clause structure, glottal stop mechanics) [Unit 14].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing the "ei" diphthong and glottal stop; 60 mins: visual picture-to-indefinite-phrase matching; 45 mins: delivery roleplay simulations).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Walking around your room, pointing to objects and naming them aloud: *Das ist ein Tisch. Das ist eine Kaffeemaschine.*) [Unit 14].

### DAY 19: Common High-Frequency Irregular Verbs (Unit 15)

#### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to conjugate and use high-frequency irregular German verbs (*haben, sprechen*) in the singular present tense (*ich, du, er/sie/es*) and formal *Sie*, identifying the vocalic and consonantal stem mutations (*hast/hat*, *sprichst/spricht*) in V2 clauses.

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the irregular singular present-tense conjugations of *haben* (to have) and *sprechen* (to speak) [Unit 15].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit grammar explanation of stem mutation [Unit 15]. They observe that the vocalic and consonantal shifts occur strictly in the **2nd person singular (*****du*****)** and **3rd person singular (*****er/sie/es*****)**, while the 1st person (*ich*) and formal *Sie* remain completely regular [Unit 15].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to native speech and distinguishes between the regular *ich spreche* and the mutated *du sprichst*, identifying the consonant cluster and terminal sibilants [Unit 15].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves language names and office nouns from previous days.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Root-and-suffix matching drills for irregular stems (*hab-* vs. *ha-*, *sprech-* vs. *sprich-*).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Language Exchange. Partners mingle and ask: *Sprechen Sie Englisch, Herr Schmidt? — Ja, ich spreche Englisch und ein bisschen Deutsch. Sprichst du auch Spanisch, Alex? — Nein, er spricht Spanisch, ich spreche Englisch.* [Unit 15].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Declaring your language profile and tools to a training coordinator in German.
- **REVISIT:** Focused pronunciation of the palatal fricative or "ich-laut" [ç] in *sprichst* before concluding.

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **das Geld** (The money / Neuter Noun) [Day 19 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation

- **Focus:** The initial consonant cluster **"sp" (/ʃp/)** and the dorsal fricative **"ch" [ç]** in *sprichst / spricht* [Unit 15].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ʃp/ (Pronounce "sp-" at the start of a German word as a soft shushing "schp" sound with rounded lips).
  2. *Word:* *spricht* [ʃpʁɪçt] ( Tongue forward against lower teeth, smile, blow air out) [Unit 15].
  3. *Phrase:* *er spricht* [e:ɐ̯ ʃpʁɪçt] [Unit 15].
  4. *Sentence:* *Er spricht Englisch.* [Unit 15].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Sprichst du Deutsch? — Ja, ich spreche ein bisschen Deutsch.* [Unit 15].

#### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **The Singular Irregular Paradigm:** High-frequency irregular German verbs mutate their stem, but only in the **2nd person singular (*****du*****)** and **3rd person singular (*****er/sie/es*****)** [Unit 15]:
  - **haben (to have):** stem *hab-* loses the **-b-** $\rightarrow$ *du hast*, *er/sie/es hat* [Unit 15].
  - **sprechen (to speak):** stem vowel **-e-** mutates to **-i-** $\rightarrow$ *du sprichst*, *er/sie/es spricht* [Unit 15]. $$\begin{array}{l|c|l||c|l} \text{Pronoun} & \text{haben Stem} & \text{haben conjugated} & \text{sprechen Stem} & \text{sprechen conjugated} \ \hline \text{ich} & \text{hab-} & \text{ich hab}\mathbf{e} & \text{sprech-} & \text{ich sprech}\mathbf{e} \ \text{du} & \text{ha-} & \text{du ha}\mathbf{st}\text{ (irregular)} & \text{sprich-} & \text{du sprich}\mathbf{st}\text{ (irregular)} \ \text{er/sie/es} & \text{ha-} & \text{er ha}\mathbf{t}\text{ (irregular)} & \text{sprich-} & \text{er sprich}\mathbf{t}\text{ (irregular)} \ \text{Sie} & \text{hab-} & \text{Sie hab}\mathbf{en} & \text{sprech-} & \text{Sie sprech}\mathbf{en} \end{array}$$

#### 6. Listening

- **Bottom-Up Phonemic Parsing:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *"Sprichst du Deutsch?"* Decode how the terminal sibilants [-çst] and the pronoun *du* merge into a single acoustic block: /ʃpʁɪçstʊ/ [Unit 15].
- **Top-Down Profile Matching:** Listen to three short job interviews. Identify which languages each candidate claims to speak (*ich spreche*) and what tools they claim to possess (*ich habe*) [Unit 15].

#### 7. Speaking

- **Oral Substitution Drills:** The teacher prompts: *"ich / haben"*. Learner: *"Ich habe"*. Teacher: *"er / haben"*. Learner: *"Er hat"*. Teacher: *"du / sprechen"*. Learner: *"Du sprichst"* [Unit 15].
- **Class Language Survey:** Ask classmates about their languages: *Sprechen Sie Englisch, Herr Schmidt? — Ja. — Und sprichst du Spanisch, Alex? — Nein, er spricht Spanisch, ich spreche Englisch.* [Unit 15].

#### 8. Reading

- Read a profile list of international apprentices at a vocational school [Unit 15]:
  - *Alex spricht Englisch. Er hat ein Handy. Sarah spricht Spanisch und Deutsch. Sie hat einen Schlüssel für das Büro.* [Note: *einen Schlüssel* is receptive here].
- Match the students to their language profiles and tools.

#### 9. Writing

- **Guided Profile Composition:** Write three simple sentences describing your team's linguistic capabilities and resources:
  1. `Ich spreche Englisch und ein bisschen Deutsch.`
  2. `Mein Kollege spricht Spanisch.`
  3. `Der Chef hat einen Schlüssel für das Büro.` [Unit 15]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Verb-Form Substitution (Irregular).
- **Execution:** Substitute subjects and apply the correct irregular stem mutations for *haben* and *sprechen*, keeping V2 syntax secure [Unit 15]:
  - *Ich spreche Deutsch.* $\rightarrow$ *Du \_\_\_\_\_\_ Englisch.* $\rightarrow$ *Du ****sprichst**** Englisch.*
  - *Ich habe das Geld.* $\rightarrow$ *Er \_\_\_\_\_\_ das Handy.* $\rightarrow$ *Er ****hat**** das Handy.*
  - *Sie sprechen Englisch.* $\rightarrow$ *Sie (she) \_\_\_\_\_\_ Spanisch.* $\rightarrow$ *Sie ****spricht**** Spanisch.* [Unit 15]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–2

- Retrieve language names (*Deutsch, Englisch, Spanisch*) [Day 3 core] and office nouns (*Handy, Schlüssel*) [Day 6 core, Unit 11] to build descriptions.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Language Survey:** The learner roleplays conducting a language survey among their new vocational classmates [Unit 15]. They must ask at least two classmates about their language abilities formally and informally, and record the results:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag, Herr Schmidt. Sprechen Sie Englisch? — Ja. — Und sprichst du Spanisch, Alex? — Ja, ich spreche Spanisch." [Unit 15]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If a classmate answers with rapid, incomprehensible German, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Noch einmal, bitte!"** [Day 3 core] to slow down the process and manage their speaking anxiety.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Supervisor:* "Sprechen Sie Deutsch?" [Unit 15]
- *Learner:* "Ja, ich spreche ein bisschen Deutsch." [Unit 15]
- *Supervisor:* "Sehr gut! Haben Sie Fragen?" [Unit 15]
- *Learner:* "Nein. Danke! Tschüss!" [Days 1, 2 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Shadowing Practice:** Spend 15 minutes shadowing a short dialogue of native speakers introducing themselves. Focus entirely on matching the shushing /ʃp/ in *sprechen* and the vocalic pitch on mutated stems [Unit 15, 327].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can accurately conjugate *haben* and *sprechen* across all singular pronouns and formal *Sie*, executing correct stem mutations [Unit 15].
  - Can pronounce the initial "sp-" as a shushing /ʃp/ and "ch" as a smiling /ç/ cleanly [Unit 15].

---

### DAY 19 TIME BUDGET (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, adding *das Geld*) [Day 19 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Irregular present-tense singular stem mutations, *haben* and *sprechen*conjugations, palatal "ch" acoustics) [Unit 15].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing the /ʃp/ and /ç/ sibilants; 60 mins: verbal present-tense substitution drills; 45 mins: transcribing native dialogue profiles).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing speaking profiles in front of a mirror: *Ich spreche Englisch, aber mein Chef spricht Deutsch.*) [Day 16, 19 core, Unit 15].
