### DAY 20: Possessives and Personal Workspace Descriptions

#### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to identify and declare ownership of workspace tools using singular possessive determiners (*mein/meine*, *Ihr/Ihre*, *dein/deine*) in the Nominative case, constructing cohesive, multi-sentence personal team descriptions.

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the singular possessive determiners *mein/meine* (my), *dein/deine* (your, informal), and *Ihr/Ihre* (your, formal) in the Nominative case [Unit 20].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit rule explaining how possessive determiners decline exactly like indefinite articles (*ein / eine*) [Unit 20]. They observe that masculine and neuter share the ending-free form (*mein, dein, Ihr*), while feminine always adds the suffix **-e** (*meine, deine, Ihre*) [Unit 20].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to dialogues of colleagues sorting out mixed-up belongings and matches each colleague to their items based on the possessive determiners heard [Unit 20].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves regular and irregular conjugations from previous days.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical matching drills connecting possessive determiners with nouns based on gender and formal/informal cues [Unit 20].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Finding Belongings. Partner A says: *Ich suche mein Handy. Ist das dein Handy dort?*$\rightarrow$ Partner B: *Nein, das ist nicht mein Handy. Das ist mein Buch.* $\rightarrow$ Partner A: *Ah, mein Handy ist hier. Danke!* [Day 6 core, Unit 20].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Confirming personal and contact details at a vocational school registration counter.
- **REVISIT:** Rehearsing the phonetic distinction between formal *Ihr / Ihre* [i:ɐ̯] and informal *dein / deine* [daɪnə] [Unit 20].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Bezahlen, bitte!** (The bill, please! / Interaction Formula) [Day 20 core]
- **Stimmt so!** (Keep the change! / Interaction Formula) [Day 20 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation

- **Focus:** Vowel length in **"Ihr / Ihre"** (/i:/) and the diphthong **"ein"** (/aɪ/) in *mein/dein* [Unit 20].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /i:ɐ̯/ (Tense lips, tongue high and forward, ending in a soft schwa-breath).
  2. *Word:* *Ihr* [i:ɐ̯] $\rightarrow$ *Ihre* [ˈi:ʁə] [Unit 20].
  3. *Phrase:* *Ist das Ihr Schlüssel?* [ɪst das i:ɐ̯ ˈʃlʏsəl] [Unit 20].
  4. *Sentence:* *Nein, das ist nicht mein Schlüssel.* [Unit 20].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Guten Tag. Ist das Ihre Fahrkarte? — Ja, das ist meine Fahrkarte.* [Unit 20].

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **Singular Possessive Determiners (Nominative):** Possessive determiners show ownership and agree grammatically with the noun they modify, declining exactly like indefinite articles (*ein / eine*) [Unit 20]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|l} \text{Owner} & \text{Masculine (der)} & \text{Feminine (die)} & \text{Neuter (das)} \ \hline \text{my (ich)} & \text{mein Schlüssel} & \text{meine Arbeit} & \text{mein Handy} \ \text{your (du)} & \text{dein Schlüssel} & \text{deine Arbeit} & \text{dein Handy} \ \text{your (Sie - formal)} & \text{Ihr Schlüssel} & \text{Ihre Arbeit} & \text{Ihr Handy} \end{array}$$
- **Grammatical Alignment:** Masculine and Neuter forms share the suffix-free root in the Nominative case, while Feminine always ends in **-e** [Unit 20].

#### 6. Listening

- **Bottom-Up Connected Transcription:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *"Ist das Ihr Schlüssel?"* and *"Das ist meine E-Mail"* [Unit 20]. Note how the formal *Ihr* is delivered with a light, unstressed vocalic breath.
- **Top-Down Workspace Sorting:** Listen to three colleagues identifying their personal tools. Circle the correct owner on a visual worksheet layout [Unit 20].

#### 7. Speaking

- **Possessive Prompt Drills:** The teacher flashes a card showing a phone and a formal pointer $\rightarrow$ Learner says: *"Ihr Handy"*. Key and self-pointing $\rightarrow$ Learner says: *"mein Schlüssel"*. Class and informal pointer $\rightarrow$ Learner says: *"deine Klasse"* [Unit 20].
- **Desk Check:** Describe your teammate's items: *Das ist meine Kollegin. Sie kommt aus Spanien. Das ist ihr Computer und das ist ihr Handy. Ihr Drucker ist dort.* [Unit 20].

#### 8. Reading

- Read an office coordination email:
  - *Guten Tag. Das ist meine Kollegin Sarah. Sie wohnt in Frankfurt. Das ist mein Chef Herr Schmidt. Er hat mein Handy. Ist das Ihre E-Mail, Herr Schmidt?* [Unit 20]
- Identify all possessive determiners and match them to their respective noun genders.

#### 9. Writing

- **Guided Description Writing:** Write a short, four-sentence profile introducing your team members, their origins, and their tools using singular possessives:
  1. `Das ist meine Kollegin Sarah. Sie kommt aus Spanien.`
  2. `Das ist mein Chef Herr Schmidt. Er hat meinen Schlüssel.` (Receptive shift)
  3. `Das ist mein Handy.`
  4. `Ist das Ihre E-Mail, Herr Schmidt?` [Unit 20]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Personal Descriptions & Possessives.
- **Execution:** Build multi-sentence descriptions by combining greetings, names, origins, and workspace tools with correct possessive determiners [Unit 20]:
  - *This is my colleague Sarah. She lives in Berlin.* $\rightarrow$ *Das ist meine Kollegin Sarah. Sie wohnt in Berlin.*
  - *This is my boss Mr. Schmidt. He is working today.* $\rightarrow$ *Das ist mein Chef Herr Schmidt. Er arbeitet heute.*
  - *This is my phone. It is here.* $\rightarrow$ *Das ist mein Handy. Es ist hier.*

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–2

- Retrieve the nouns *Chef* [Day 16 core], *Kollegin* [Unit 13], *Arbeit* [Day 15 core], and *Handy* [Day 6 core] to build possessive clauses.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Registration Desk Verification:** The learner roleplays verifying their personal coordinates at a German municipal desk (*Bürgeramt*) [Unit 20]. They approach the clerk, introduce themselves formally, present their documents, and confirm:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Das ist meine Adresse. Und das ist meine E-Mail. Haben Sie meinen Namen im Computer?" [Unit 20]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the clerk looks at the screen in confusion, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Noch einmal, bitte."**[Day 3 core] to prompt verification, maintaining complete control in German.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Clerk:* "Guten Tag. Ist das Ihre E-Mail?" [Unit 20]
- *Learner:* "Ja, das ist meine E-Mail. Und das ist mein Name." [Unit 20]
- *Clerk:* "Alles klar. Danke. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]
- *Learner:* "Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Interactive Desk Audit:** Look at an image of a German office space online. Practice pointing to different objects and describing who owns them using German possessive determiners (*mein / Ihr / dein*) [Unit 20].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can correctly choose and deploy singular possessive determiners (*mein/meine*, *Ihr/Ihre*, *dein/deine*) in the Nominative case based on noun gender [Unit 20].
  - Can build a cohesive 3-sentence personal description of a coworker using correct possessives and regular conjugations [Unit 20].

---

### DAY 20 TIME BUDGET (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, adding *Bezahlen, bitte!*, *Stimmt so!*) [Day 20 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Possessive determiners, Nominative declension rules, personal description templates) [Unit 20].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: picture-to-possessive-phrase generation; 45 mins: possessive matching grammar games; 45 mins: mock registration desk roleplays).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing your own personal team presentation aloud: *Das ist mein Kollege Alex. Er wohnt in Frankfurt...*).

### DAY 21: Conversational Mingle, Spaced Synthesis, and Formal Checkout

#### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to synthesize all Week 3 grammatical, lexical, and phonetic coordinates to execute a multi-stage, interactive personal communication check and a simulated cafe checkout transaction entirely in German.

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor reviews all Week 3 structural baselines, focusing on regular and irregular present-tense singular conjugations, definite and indefinite articles, and possessive descriptions [Unit 40].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the comprehensive dialogue synthesis map showing how personal greetings, language profiles, workspace status, and retail payment transactions flow together in a natural conversational cycle [Unit 40].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner scans a complete written conversation transcript and highlights any conjugation suffixes, gender mismatches, or register errors.
- **RETRIEVE:** Systematic spaced recall of all Core and main curriculum vocabulary from Weeks 1–3.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Restructuring scrambled statements to align subject pronouns and mutated verb stems.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work executing the complete Week 3 synthesis mingle from start to finish.
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching the formal checkout examiner to complete the Week 3 Milestone Task.
- **REVISIT:** Final pronunciation audit of consonant clusters [ʃp], palatal fricatives [ç], and terminal devoicings [gɛlt] before the test.

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **krank** (Sick / Adjective) [Day 21 core]
- **der Arzt** (The doctor / Masculine Noun) [Day 21 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation

- **Focus:** Spoken rhythm, sibilant transitions, and liaison across all Week 3 targets [Unit 40].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ç/ $\rightarrow$ /ʃp/ $\rightarrow$ /ts/ $\rightarrow$ /z/ (Consolidating all target sounds).
  2. *Word:* *Arzt* [a:ɐ̯tst] $\rightarrow$ *krank* [kʁaŋk] $\rightarrow$ *Chef* [ʃɛf] (Sharp voiceless consonant releases).
  3. *Phrase:* *ich spreche* $\rightarrow$ *ist geöffnet* $\rightarrow$ *mein Schlüssel* (Smooth boundary linking).
  4. *Sentence:* *Das ist mein Kollege. Er spricht Englisch.* [Unit 15, 20].
  5. *Connected Speech:* Deliver the entire 5-sentence personal introduction and cafe payment block with natural, fluid rhythm and sentence stress.

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **The V2 Syntactic law:** Regardless of changes in the subject pronoun (*ich, du, er, Sie*) or verb regularity (*wohne, bin, spricht, hat*), the finite, conjugated verb remains locked securely in **Position 2** of every main clause [Unit 1]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|l} \text{Position 1} & \text{Position 2 (Verb)} & \text{Position 3} & \text{Position 4} \ \hline \text{Ich} & \text{wohne} & \text{in Berlin.} & \ \text{Das} & \text{ist} & \text{meine Kollegin} & \text{Sarah.} \ \text{Er} & \text{hat} & \text{das Geld} & \text{im Büro.} \ \text{Morgen} & \text{lerne} & \text{ich} & \text{heute.} \end{array}$$

#### 6. Listening

- **Bottom-Up Dictation Transcription:** Listen to a multi-stage native dialogue recorded at a business vocational school. Fill in the missing letters and articles on your worksheet, focusing on transcribing pronoun endings, mutated verb vowels, and noun articles.
- **Register Validation:** Audit three audio clips and confirm if the speakers maintain consistent formal register (*Sie/sind/haben/sprechen*) without mixing registers.

#### 7. Speaking

- **The Mirror Presentation:** Practice delivering a continuous 5-sentence personal team introduction aloud with proper mouth shapes and vocal cords vibrating immediately on initial voiced sibilants.
- **Rapid Question Response:** Answer immediate verbal prompts from the instructor:
  - *"Wo wohnt er?"* $\rightarrow$ *Er wohnt in Berlin.* [Unit 12]
  - *"Sprichst du Englisch?"* $\rightarrow$ *Ja, ich spreche Englisch.* [Unit 15]

#### 8. Reading

- The student reads an un-simplified graded extensive text containing 98% familiar vocabulary:
  - *Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich wohne in Berlin. Das ist meine Kollegin Sarah. Sie kommt aus Spanien. Sie lerne heute nicht, sie arbeitet. Das ist mein Chef Herr Schmidt. Er hat mein Handy und meine E-Mail-Bestätigung. Er spricht Deutsch und Englisch. Bezahlen, bitte! Stimmt so!*
- The student verifies that all verbs occupy Position 2 [Unit 1].

#### 9. Writing

- **Controlled Essay Composition:** The learner writes their own team profile and work status report (5 sentences) in their notebook, ensuring correct capitalization, spelling of suffixes, and perfect verb-second syntax.

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** The Mini-Dialogue Creation (Week 3 Synthesis) [Unit 40].
- **Execution:** Construct a complete, coherent conversation between a student and a registrar, combining greetings, personal details, language profiles, and tools:
  - *Student:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John."
  - *Clerk:* "Guten Tag. Wohnen Sie in Frankfurt?"
  - *Student:* "Ja, ich wohne in Frankfurt. Und das ist meine Kollegin Sarah."
  - *Clerk:* "Sprechen Sie Deutsch, Sarah?"
  - *Student:* "Sie spricht ein bisschen Deutsch, aber sie spricht Spanisch."

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–2

- Retrieve spelling, origin, numbers, station status (*geöffnet/geschlossen*), and cash checkout formulas to enrich the final interaction.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Team Introduction and Cafe Payment (Milestone Task):** The learner roleplays participating in a school social and coffee transaction [Unit 40]. They approach their coordinator, introduce themselves and their Spanish classmate formally, state their residence, declare their language profile, order coffee, pay with Euro, and execute tipping:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag, Herr Schmidt. Ich bin John. Ich wohne in Frankfurt. Das ist meine Kollegin Sarah. Sie kommt aus Spanien. Sie spricht Spanisch. ... Bezahlen, bitte! Das macht acht Euro. ... Hier sind zehn Euro. Stimmt so! Danke. Bis morgen!" [Days 1, 2, 7, 8, 14, 15, 17, 20 core, Unit 12, 20]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the waiter calls out an incorrect total, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Noch einmal, bitte."** [Day 3 core] to slow down the process and manage the transaction successfully in German.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction

- *Native (Supervisor):* "Guten Tag. Sind Sie Herr John?" [Unit 11]
- *Learner:* "Ja, ich bin John. Und das ist meine Kollegin Sarah." [Unit 11, 20]
- *Native (Supervisor):* "Sprechen Sie Deutsch, Sarah?" [Unit 15]
- *Learner:* "Sie spricht ein bisschen Deutsch. Ich spreche Englisch." [Unit 15]
- *Native (Supervisor):* "Ausgezeichnet! Willkommen!" [Unit 11]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Extensive Reading Sprint:** Spend 15 minutes reading a short graded story about a vocational class introduction in Hamburg. Focus on enjoying the narrative flow, noting how pronouns, regular conjugations, and definite/indefinite articles are naturally embedded.

#### 16. Mastery Check

- The learner successfully completes the Day 21 Milestone Task under un-speeded roleplay conditions, demonstrating V2 syntax stability, correct regular/irregular conjugations, and clear phonetic boundaries [Unit 40].

### DAY 21 STUDY TIME ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 20 minutes (Reviewing all Days 15–21 items) [Day 21 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 40 minutes (Verb-Second syntactic review, dialogue mapping, cash payment steps) [Unit 40].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (Mirror presentation, question-answer conversion drills, spelling and phone number dictations, mock testing).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Completing a final self-assessment checklist and reviewing incorrect pronunciation entries).

# WEEK 3 REVIEW

### 1. What should now be recognized (Receptive Mastery)

- The difference between formal *Sie* and informal *du* subject-verb suffixes (*-en* vs. *-st*) [Unit 11, 12].
- Identifying spoken word boundaries of merged pronouns and mutated verb stems (e.g. *sprichst du* /ʃpʁɪçstʊ/) in connected speech [Unit 15].
- The plural pronouns *wir* and *ihr* and their verb endings in native input [Unit 11, 12, 17].
- The three definite articles (*der, die, das*) and indefinite articles (*ein, eine, ein*) in Nominative texts [Unit 13, 14].

### 2. What should now be actively produced (Active Mastery)

- Present-tense singular conjugations of regular verbs (*wohnen, kommen, lernen, arbeiten*) with perfect suffix agreement in V2 declarative sentences [Unit 12].
- Conjugation and usage of singular irregular verbs *sein* (*bin, bist, ist*), *haben* (*habe, hast, hat*), and *sprechen*(*spreche, sprichst, spricht*) [Unit 11, 15].
- Mapping definite and indefinite articles accurately to core nouns (*der Chef, die Kollegin, der Schlüssel, das Geld, das Brot, der Kaffee*) based on natural gender and phonetic ending rules [Unit 13, 14].
- Formulating demonstrative statements using *Das ist ein/eine...* in the Nominative case [Unit 14].
- Stating ownership using singular possessive determiners (*mein/meine*, *Ihr/Ihre*, *dein/deine*) [Unit 20].
- Formulating complete personal profiles (name, origin, residence, language profile, work/study status, and tools) [Unit 12, 20].

### 3. What should remain mainly receptive

- Exhaustive gender suffix tables beyond the three high-utility rules [Unit 13].
- Active morphological calculation of the Accusative direct object case (to be explicitly introduced in Week 4) [Unit 16].
- Plural conjugations beyond the receptive *wir* and *ihr* forms [Unit 11, 12].

### 4. Pronunciation Gains

- Intelligible production of the initial consonant cluster **"sp"** as a soft shushing "schp" (/ʃp/) sound [Unit 15].
- Intelligible production of the palatal fricative **"ch"** as a smiling hissing palatal "ich-laut" (/ç/) in *nicht* and *sprichst*, avoiding English "sh" or throat-clearing defaults [Unit 11, 15].
- Applying terminal consonant devoicing consistently (e.g., pronouncing the "d" in *das Geld* as a sharp unvoiced "t" [gɛlt]) [Unit 13].

### 5. Sentence-Building Gains

- Progressed from simple noun labeling to **flexible pronoun and verb-form substitutions** [Unit 12].
- Successfully formulated Nominative indefinite article structures (*Das ist ein Drucker*) and possessive workspace descriptions (*Das ist meine Kollegin*) [Unit 14, 20].
- Consolidated syntax to deliver coherent, multi-sentence personal team profiles under communicative conditions [Unit 40].

### 6. Practical Abilities Gained

- Can participate in an introductory mingle round-robin, introducing themselves and their teammate's residency, origin, and work status with correct conjugations [Unit 12].
- Can conduct equipment inventory audits, declaring possession of workstation tools and managing registration files [Unit 13, 14].
- Can coordinate language surveys and order cafe items, paying cash and executing tipping etiquette (*Bezahlen, bitte!*, *Stimmt so!*) [Day 20 core, Unit 15].

### 7. Weak Areas to Diagnose (Diagnostic Guide)

- **Mutation Omissions:** Check if the learner is dropping vowel changes in *sprechen* (e.g. producing *du sprechst*instead of *du sprichst*) or consonant drops in *haben* [Unit 15].
- **Register Discrepancies:** Ensure the learner is not pairing formal titles with informal pronouns (e.g. *du bist Herr Müller* instead of *Sie sind Herr Müller* or *du bist Alex*) [Unit 11].
- **Glottal Stop Omissions:** Ensure the learner maintains a clean glottal catch (*Knacklaut*) before vowel-initial indefinite articles (*ist [pause] ein*) to prevent slurred transitions [Unit 14].

---

# WEEK 3 MASTERY CHECK

This formal evaluation uses **qualitative, functional competence criteria** to determine readiness for Week 4, completely bypassing arbitrary speed, percentage, or trial-count gates [Newton & Nation, 2020].

```
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                 WEEK 3 MASTERY CHECKLIST                                        |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  1. PHONOLOGICAL INTELLIGIBILITY (Receptive & Productive)                                       |
|     -> Can produce distinct shushing [ʃp] (spricht) and terminal devoiced [gɛlt] (Geld) sounds.   |
|                                                                                                 |
|  2. CANONICAL V2 CONJUGATION AGREEMENT                                                          |
|     -> Maintains accurate suffix and stem vowel mappings across regular and irregular copulas.   |
|                                                                                                 |
|  3. DEMONSTRATIVE AND GENDER STABILITY                                                          |
|     -> Maps der/die/das and ein/eine correctly based on phonetic and semantic guidelines.        |
|                                                                                                 |
|  4. SOCIAL AND TRANSACTIONAL AUTONOMY (TBLT Task)                                               |
|     -> Formulate introductions, verify coordinates, describe resources, order, and pay cash.     |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

```

### 1. Pronunciation Check

- **Target Sound Isolation:** The learner reads aloud the following three sentences, which contain the critical phonetic contrasts of Week 3 [Vaughan-Rees, 2002]:
  1. *Mein Chef spricht Eng-lisch und ein biss-chen Deutsch.* (Evaluates initial /ʃp/ in *spricht*, palatal /ç/ in *bisschen*, and regular conjugation).
  2. *Das ist mei-ne Kol-le-gin Sarah. Sie wohnt in Frank-furt.* (Evaluates feminine possessive *meine*, the female profession suffix *-in*, and regular 3rd person singular agreement).
  3. *Wir ha-ben das Geld für das Brot.* (Evaluates plural copula agreement, definite neuter *das*, and terminal devoiced /t/ in *Geld*).
- **Standard of Success:** Intelligible pronunciation. The learner must demonstrate clear articulatory effort to produce German-specific sibilants and avoid English "w" slurs or flat vowels.

### 2. Functional Communication Check (The School Welcome and Coffee Run)

- **The Scenario:** The learner roleplays arriving at school and visiting the breakroom café. The examiner acts as the school coordinator and the waiter [Unit 40].
- **The Task:** The learner must complete a 6-step interactive cycle under natural, un-speeded conditions:
  1. Greet the coordinator formally, confirm their own identity, and introduce their Spanish coworker formally using possessives (*Das ist meine Kollegin...*) [Unit 11, 20].
  2. State city of residence and describe work schedule and activities using correct conjugated suffixes (*Sie arbeitet heute nicht*) [Unit 12].
  3. Declare teammate's language profile, executing correct irregular stem vowel mutations (*Sie spricht Spanisch...*) [Unit 15].
  4. Verify presence or absence of necessary tools using correct definite articles (*Der Schlüssel ist hier, das Geld ist dort*) [Unit 13].
  5. Order café items (*Brot*, *Kaffee*), decode the price total up to 20 Euro, pay cash, and execute tipping etiquette (*Bezahlen, bitte!*, *Stimmt so!*) [Day 20 core, Unit 14].
  6. Politely bid goodbye using *Bis morgen!* or *Auf Wiedersehen* [Day 5, 14 core].
- **Standard of Success:** Conversational success. Minor grammatical slips (such as irregular verb endings) are recast implicitly [Long, 2015]. The learner meets the criteria if meaning is successfully negotiated, the verb remains consistently in Position 2, articles are mapped correctly based on phonetic/semantic cues, and any rapid input is managed using German repair formulas (*Wie bitte?*) without reverting to English.

---

*The complete Week 3 (Days 15–21) detailed study plan is finalized, audited, and officially locked under the designated design parameters. Stop. Do not proceed to Week 4.*


---

# WEEK 4 — DAYS 22–28


# WEEK 4 STUDY PLAN: ACCUSATIVE CASE, NEGATION, MODAL REQUESTS, SEPARABLE VERBS, AND V2 FLEXIBILITY

Week 4 marks a significant transition in the adult second language acquisition (SLA) process. Having established a stable first-person and formal identity baseline in Weeks 1–3, the learner’s brain is now cognitively prepared to shift from rigid Subject-Verb-Object (S-V-O) word order templates to flexible, canonical German Verb-Second (V2) syntax and case-marking operations.

Through **Transfer-Appropriate Processing (TAP)** and **High-Variability Phonetic Training (HVPT)**, this 7-day study plan systematically proceduralises and automates the **masculine accusative shift**, contrastive negation (**nicht vs. kein**), **modal request frames**, and **separable verb syntax**. By using **interleaved retrieval practice** and **Task-Based Language Teaching (TBLT)**, the learner proceduralises explicit declarative grammar rules into spontaneous communicative skills.

---

## DAY-BY-DAY EXECUTABLE SPRINT (DAYS 22–28)

---

## DAY 22: The Accusative Case Direct Object Baseline & The Masculine Suffix Shift (*der $\rightarrow$ den*)

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to identify direct objects in transitive clauses, actively apply the masculine accusative shift (*der $\rightarrow$ den*, *ein $\rightarrow$ einen*) using high-frequency verbs (*haben*, *suchen*, *brauchen*), and use the core scheduling formulas *keine Zeit* and *den Termin verschieben* [Day 22 core].

### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The teacher models transitive clauses where direct objects receive accusative case markings, highlighting that the grammatical shift is strictly limited to masculine nouns [Unit 16].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit declarative layout showing that feminine (*die/eine*) and neuter (*das/ein*) direct objects remain structurally stable in the accusative case [Unit 16].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short office inventories and checks a box indicating whether the direct object mentioned is masculine (*einen Schlüssel*) or neuter (*ein Handy*) [Unit 16, 20].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves learned workspace nouns (*Schlüssel*, *Handy*, *Drucker*) from previous weeks [Day 6, 26 core, Unit 11].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical matching drills where learners combine transitive verbs with correctly inflected direct objects based on gender prompts [Unit 16].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner drill where students describe missing office equipment using *brauchen* and *suchen*[Unit 16].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching an administrative reception counter to request a replacement key [Unit 16].
- **REVISIT:** Spaced spelling audit of the accusative suffix **-en** before concluding the study day.

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **keine Zeit** (No time / Interaction Formula) [Day 22 core]
- **den Termin verschieben** (To reschedule/postpone the appointment / Sentence Frame) [Day 22 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The terminal nasal suffix **-en** (/ən/) in *den*, *einen*, and *verschieben*, avoiding swallowed or silent endings [Unit 16, 21].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ən/ (Release air cleanly through the nose while keeping the tongue flat against the alveolar ridge).
  2. *Word:* *einen* [ˈaɪnən] [Unit 16].
  3. *Phrase:* *einen Termin* [ˈaɪnən tɛʁˈmi\:n] [Day 22 core].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich muss den Termin verschieben.* [Day 22 core, Unit 21].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Entschuldigung, ich habe heute keine Zeit. Ich muss den Termin verschieben.* (Slide smoothly over word boundaries with a soft, unstressed schwa-nasal release [Day 22 core]).

### 5. Minimum Explanation/Theory

- **The Nominative vs. Accusative Direct Object Rule:**
  - **MUST MASTER:** Direct objects in German receive **Accusative case** [Unit 16]. **Only masculine nouns change in the Accusative!** Feminine, neuter, and plural nouns remain identical to their Nominative forms [Unit 16]:
    - **Masculine (*****der \\(\rightarrow\\) den*****, *****ein \\(\rightarrow\\) einen*****):** *Ich suche ****den**** Schlüssel. Ich brauche ****einen**** Drucker.* [Unit 16].
    - **Feminine (*****die \\(\rightarrow\\) die*****, *****eine \\(\rightarrow\\) eine*****):** *Ich brauche ****eine**** Fahrkarte.* [Unit 16].
    - **Neuter (*****das \\(\rightarrow\\) das*****, *****ein \\(\rightarrow\\) ein*****):** *Ich habe ****ein**** Handy.* [Unit 16].
  - **IMPORTANT:** Mapping high-frequency transitive verbs (*haben, brauchen, suchen*) to their direct objects [Unit 16, 17].
  - **DEFER:** Dative case prepositions, plural accusative case declensions, and adjective endings [Unit 16].

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Case Contrast:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *"Ich suche mein Handy"* vs. *"Ich suche meinen Schlüssel"* [Unit 16, 20]. Identify how the masculine accusative ending is held slightly longer to produce the terminal nasal resonance [Unit 16, 20].
- **Top-Down Supply Order:** Listen to a voicemail message from a colleague requesting office tools. Check whether they need *einen Drucker* (masculine) or *ein Handy* (neuter) on a digital supply grid.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Suffix Drills:** The teacher prompts: *"ich / suchen / Schlüssel"* \\(\rightarrow\\) Learner says: *Ich suche den Schlüssel.* [Unit 16]. Prompt: *"ich / brauchen / Drucker"* \\(\rightarrow\\) Learner says: *Ich brauche einen Drucker.*[Unit 16].
- **Appointment Desk:** Rehearse the formal core apology: *Ich habe keine Zeit. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben, bitte.* [Day 22 core, Unit 21].

### 8. Reading Practice

- Scan a short printed office email:
  - *Guten Tag Herr Schmidt. Ich suche den Drucker. Der Drucker ist in Raum 101. Aber ich habe keinen Schlüssel. Haben Sie den Schlüssel? Danke.* [Unit 16, 18]
- Highlight all direct objects and identify their grammatical gender (masculine, feminine, or neuter).

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Suffix Writing:** Complete the sentences using correct accusative masculine endings (*den* or *einen*):
  1. `Ich brauche _____ (the) Schlüssel für das Büro.` $\rightarrow$ *den*
  2. `Suchen Sie _____ (a) Drucker?` $\rightarrow$ *einen* [Unit 16]

### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Words to Word Groups (Accusative Objects).
- **Execution:** Combine transitive verbs with masculine nouns to form correct accusative word groups, establishing the direct object baseline:
  - *haben / der Euro* \\(\rightarrow\\) *einen Euro haben* [Day 10 core, Unit 17]
  - *suchen / der Chef* \\(\rightarrow\\) *den Chef suchen* [Day 16 core, Unit 16]
  - *brauchen / der Termin* \\(\rightarrow\\) *einen Termin brauchen* [Day 22 core, Unit 16]

### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–3

- Retrieve the singular pronouns (*ich*, *Sie*) and conjugation endings from Week 3 (*ich habe*, *Sie haben*) [Unit 11, 15, 17] to form the main clauses for today's sentence-building drills.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Bürgeramt Appointment Shuffle (TBLT):** The learner roleplays calling a local registration office (*Bürgeramt*) [Unit 3]. They must greet the clerk formally, declare that they have no time today, request to reschedule their appointment, and confirm that they have their registration email [Day 22 core, Unit 11]:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich habe heute keine Zeit. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben, bitte. Ich habe meine E-Mail-Bestätigung hier. Danke!" [Days 1, 22 core, Unit 11]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the clerk speaks too rapidly, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 3 core] to prompt an immediate decrease in speech velocity.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Clerk - Formal):* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie einen Termin?" [Unit 21]
- *Learner:* "Ja. Ich habe heute keine Zeit. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben." [Day 22 core]
- *Native (Clerk - Formal):* "Kein Problem. Wann geht es?" [Day 12, 13 core]
- *Learner:* "Morgen passt gut. Danke!" [Day 12 core, Unit 5]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **Input Flood:** Prompt an AI assistant: *"Give me 10 simple, natural German sentences using the transitive verbs 'suchen' and 'brauchen' with masculine accusative objects like 'den Schlüssel' or 'einen Drucker'. Keep them practical for everyday administrative life."* Read them aloud to build phonetic automaticity.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can apply the masculine accusative shift (*der \\(\rightarrow\\) den*, *ein \\(\rightarrow\\) einen*) in controlled speech and writing [Unit 16].
  - Can state "Ich habe keine Zeit" and request "Ich möchte den Termin verschieben" with correct V2 syntax [Day 22 core, Unit 21].

---

### DAY 22 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *keine Zeit*, *den Termin verschieben*) [Day 22 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Accusative case, direct object boundaries, masculine shift mechanics) [Unit 16].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: mouth-mirror training for terminal nasal endings [-ən]; 60 mins: card-sorting subject vs. direct object slots; 45 mins: shadowing native accusative requests).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Walking through your workspace, pointing to objects and stating your needs aloud: *Ich brauche den Schlüssel. Ich suche einen Drucker.*) [2, Unit 16].

---

## DAY 23: Noun Negation via the Negative Determiner *kein* (*kein / keine / keinen*)

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to negate nouns (stating "no/none" or "not a") using the negative determiners *kein*, *keine*, and *keinen* with correct accusative case suffixes, and express formal empathy using *Gute Besserung!* [Day 21, 23 core, Unit 18].

### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents the negative determiners *kein* (neuter/masculine nominative), *keine*(feminine), and *keinen* (masculine accusative) [Unit 18].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit rule explaining that *kein* declines exactly like the indefinite article *ein* (simply prefixing a **k-**) [Unit 18]. Only masculine direct objects receive the **-en** suffix (*keinen*) [Unit 18].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to workspace dialogues and checks off which resources are declared out of stock or missing (*kein Geld*, *keinen Schlüssel*, *keine E-Mail*) [Unit 18, 19].
- **RETRIEVE:** The learner retrieves indefinite articles (*ein, eine, ein*) from Week 3 to anchor the declension [Unit 14].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Gap-fill exercises matching noun genders with the correct negative determiner (*kein*, *keine*, *keinen*).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner inventory roleplay. Students check their tool grids and state which items they do not possess: *Ich habe keinen Drucker. Ich habe kein Geld.* [Unit 18].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Stating the absence of necessary registration documents or funds at a local agency desk.
- **REVISIT:** Quick pronunciation workout on the sharp aspirated initial [kʰ] sound of *kein* [Unit 18].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Gute Besserung!** (Get well soon! / Interaction Formula) [Day 21, 23 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The sharp aspirated initial voiceless velar stop **"K" (/kʰ/)** and the diphthong **"ei" (/aɪ/)** in *kein* [Unit 18].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /kʰ/ (Produce a sharp, explosive puff of air from the back of the mouth, similar to the initial "c" in English "cat" but with more distinct breath force).
  2. *Word:* *keinen* [ˈkʰaɪnən] [Unit 18].
  3. *Phrase:* *keinen Schlüssel* [ˈkʰaɪnən ˈʃlʏsəl] [Unit 18].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich habe keinen Schlüssel.* [Unit 18].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Gute Besserung! Ich habe heute keinen Termin.* (Keep the initial /kʰ/ crisp and aspirated to ensure clear semantic distinction [Day 23 core, Unit 18]).

### 5. Minimum Explanation/Theory

- **Direct Object Noun Negation:**
  - **MUST MASTER:** To negate a noun in German (declaring the absence of an item or stating "I have no..."), use the negative determiner **kein** [Unit 18]. It declines exactly like *ein* and follows the accusative masculine shift rule [Unit 18]:
    - **Masculine (*****kein \\(\rightarrow\\) keinen*****):** *Ich habe ****keinen**** Schlüssel. Ich brauche ****keinen**** Drucker.*[Unit 18].
    - **Feminine (*****keine \\(\rightarrow\\) keine*****):** *Ich habe ****keine**** E-Mail.* [Unit 18].
    - **Neuter (*****kein \\(\rightarrow\\) kein*****):** *Ich habe ****kein**** Geld.* [Unit 18].
  - **IMPORTANT:** Using *kein* strictly for negating nouns (not verbs or adjectives) [Unit 18].
  - **DEFER:** Plural dative declensions of *kein* [Unit 18].

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Negation Sorting:** Listen to five short recordings of employees listing missing items [Unit 18]. Transcribe the negative determiners heard and verify if they match masculine, feminine, or neuter nouns.
- **Empathy Detection:** Listen to three dialogues in a medical center. Circle the exact moment the phrase *Gute Besserung!* is spoken [Day 21, 23 core].

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Transformation Drills:** Convert positive statements into negative ones:
  - *Ich habe einen Schlüssel.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich habe keinen Schlüssel.* [Unit 18].
  - *Ich habe ein Handy.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich habe kein Handy.* [Unit 18].
- **Empathy Practice:** Deliver *Gute Besserung!* with natural, warm falling intonation to a sick colleague [Day 21, 23 core].

### 8. Reading Practice

- Read an office text-message log:
  - *Hallo John. Ich bin heute krank. Ich gehe zum Arzt. Ich arbeite heute nicht. — Oh, Gute Besserung! Ich habe heute keinen Termin im Büro. Bis morgen!* [Day 14, 21, 23 core, Unit 12, 19]
- Underline all noun negations and circle the formal empathy formula [Day 21, 23 core, Unit 18].

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Guided Negation Writing:** Write three simple sentences in your workbook stating what supplies your home office lacks:
  1. `Ich habe keinen Drucker.` [Unit 18]
  2. `Ich habe kein Geld.` [Unit 18]
  3. `Ich habe keine E-Mail.` [Unit 18]

### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Simple Sentences (Direct Object Negation).
- **Execution:** Construct simple V2 negative statements by combining nouns and transitive verbs with *kein/keine/keinen*, reusing older vocabulary:
  - *ich / haben / das Geld* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich habe kein Geld.* [Day 19 core, Unit 18]
  - *Sie / brauchen / der Schlüssel* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Sie brauchen keinen Schlüssel.* [Unit 18]
  - *wir / haben / die Fahrkarte* \\(\rightarrow\\) (Receptive) *Wir haben keine Fahrkarte.* [Day 11 core, Unit 18]

### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–3

- Retrieve the irregular present-tense singular forms of *haben* (*hast/hat*) [Unit 15, 17] and definite articles from Week 3 [Unit 13] to correctly identify noun genders before applying the *kein* prefix.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Medical Center Check-In (TBLT):** The learner roleplays visiting a doctor's clinic (*Arztpraxis*) because their training colleague is sick [Day 21 core, Unit 11]. They must formally introduce themselves, state that their colleague is sick, declare that they have no appointment today, and express wishes for recovery:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Mein Kollege ist krank. Ich habe heute keinen Termin. Hier ist das Dokument. Gute Besserung für die Kollegin!" [Days 1, 21, 23 core, Unit 11, 18]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the receptionist (the teacher) shakes their head in confusion, the learner politely points to their calendar app and says: **"Noch einmal, bitte."** [Day 1 core] to repeat the statement clearly.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Receptionist - Formal):* "Guten Tag. Ist Ihre Kollegin krank?" [Day 21 core]
- *Learner:* "Ja, sie ist krank. Sie geht zum Arzt." [Day 21 core]
- *Native (Receptionist - Formal):* "Oh, das tut mir leid. Haben Sie einen Termin?" [Unit 21]
- *Learner:* "Nein, ich habe keinen Termin heute." [Unit 18]
- *Native (Receptionist - Formal):* "Kein Problem. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **Targeted Vocabulary Drilling:** Spend 15 minutes reviewing digital flashcard stacks containing core A1 nouns. Practice transforming each noun immediately from its positive form (*ein Drucker*) to its negative accusative form (*keinen Drucker*) to build procedural speed.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can decline *kein, keine, keinen* accurately based on noun gender in direct object positions [Unit 18].
  - Can use "Gute Besserung!" naturally in response to a health status report [Day 21, 23 core].

---

### DAY 23 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *Gute Besserung!*) [Day 21, 23 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Noun negation mechanics, article-prefix mapping, gender re-verification) [Unit 18].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: positive-to-negative oral sentence conversions; 45 mins: transcription of diagnostic inventory lists; 45 mins: practicing aspirated velar stop voiceless releases [kʰ]).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing medical registration dialogues aloud while performing tasks).

---

## DAY 24: Negation Contrasts — Noun Negation (*kein*) vs. Verb/Adjective Negation (*nicht*)

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to distinguish between noun negation (*kein*) and verb/adjective negation (*nicht*), apply correct syntactic placement of *nicht* in a V2 clause, and use the stable feminine possessive frame *Das ist meine...* [Day 24 core, Unit 19].

### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The teacher models the structural contrast between negating a noun with *kein* and negating a verb, adjective, or clause with *nicht* [Unit 18, 19].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit syntactic guide showing that *nicht* sits at the end of simple clauses when negating verbs, but stands directly before adjectives and prepositional modifiers [Unit 19].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to native speech samples and identifies whether the negation is targeting a noun (*kein*) or an action/modifier (*nicht*) [Unit 18, 19].
- **RETRIEVE:** Retrieval of the negative determiner *kein* from Day 23 [Unit 18].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical selection drills choosing between *nicht* and *kein* for a series of scrambled statements.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partners describe a dysfunctional workplace where items are missing and equipment is broken: *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich habe keinen Schlüssel.* [Day 26 core, Unit 18, 19].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Navigating a transit ticketing office and explaining that a ticket or receipt was not printed.
- **REVISIT:** Quick acoustic verification of the smiling "ich-laut" (/ç/) in *nicht* [Unit 19].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **die Fahrkarte** (The transit ticket / Feminine Noun) [Day 24 core]
- **die Adresse** (The address / Feminine Noun) [Day 24 core]
- **Das ist meine...** (This is my... [Feminine] / Possessive Frame) [Day 24 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The voiceless palatal fricative **"ich-laut" (/ç/)** and final alveolar stop **"t"** in *nicht* [Unit 19].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ç/ (Open mouth slightly in a smile, push the tongue forward against the lower teeth, and blow air out to make a soft "hissing cat" sound, adding a sharp, clean /t/ release at the end: [nɪçt]).
  2. *Word:* *nicht* [nɪçt] [Unit 19].
  3. *Phrase:* *funktioniert nicht* [fʊŋktsioˈni:ɐ̯t nɪçt] [Unit 19].
  4. *Sentence:* *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.* [Day 26 core, Unit 19].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Das ist meine Fahrkarte. Sie funktioniert nicht.* (Keep the "ich-laut" soft and distinct from English "sh" or "ch" [Day 24 core, Unit 19]).

### 5. Minimum Explanation/Theory

- **Noun Negation (*****kein*****) vs. Verb/Adjective Negation (*****nicht*****):**
  - **MUST MASTER:** Use **kein** to negate nouns (stating "no" or "none") [Unit 18]. Use **nicht** to negate verbs, adjectives, or entire clauses [Unit 19]:
    - *Ich habe ****keinen**** Drucker.* (Negates the noun *Drucker* \\(\rightarrow\\) "I have no printer") [Unit 18].
    - *Ich arbeite heute ****nicht****.* (Negates the verb *arbeite* \\(\rightarrow\\) "nicht" sits at the end of the clause) [Unit 19].
    - *Das Büro ist ****nicht**** geöffnet.* (Negates the adjective *geöffnet* \\(\rightarrow\\) "nicht" sits directly before the adjective) [Day 11 core, Unit 19].
  - **IMPORTANT:** Capitalize nouns (*Fahrkarte*, *Adresse*) but keep possessives (*meine*) lowercase [Day 24 core, Unit 11].
  - **DEFER:** Complex negative coordination using *sondern* [Unit 19].

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Negation Identification:** Listen to five spoken sentences [Unit 19]. Identify whether the speaker uses *nicht* or *kein* by writing the correct word on your worksheet.
- **Boundary Parsing:** Listen to *"Das ist meine Fahrkarte"* [Day 24 core]. Identify the exact phonetic boundaries and the glottal stop separating *ist* and *meine* in connected speech.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Sentence Transformations (Positive to Negative Modifier):**
  - *Das Büro ist geöffnet.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das Büro ist nicht geöffnet.* [Day 11 core, Unit 19].
  - *Der Drucker funktioniert.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.* [Day 26 core, Unit 19].
- **Asserting Ownership:** Point to your transit ticket and say: *Das ist meine Fahrkarte. Sie ist hier.* [Day 24 core, Unit 11].

### 8. Reading Practice

- Read a transit passenger alert notice:
  - *Achtung: Der Fahrkartenautomat im Bahnhof funktioniert heute nicht. Bitte kaufen Sie Ihre Fahrkarte im Büro.* [Day 8, 24 core, Unit 19]
- Draw a circle around the negative adverb *nicht* and label the grammatical categories of the surrounding words.

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Negation Contrast Writing:** Write four sentences in your notebook, contrasting *nicht* and *kein*:
  1. `Ich habe heute keine Zeit.` [Day 22 core, Unit 18]
  2. `Das Büro ist nicht geöffnet.` [Day 11 core, Unit 19]
  3. `Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.` [Day 26 core, Unit 19]
  4. `Das ist meine Fahrkarte.` [Day 24 core]

### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Sentence Transformations (Negation Contrasts).
- **Execution:** Transform positive sentences into negative ones, choosing correctly between *nicht* and *kein* based on the target of negation:
  - *Ich lerne heute.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich lerne heute ****nicht****.* (Negates verb) [Unit 19]
  - *Ich habe ein Handy.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich habe ****kein**** Handy.* (Negates noun) [Unit 18]
  - *Der Bahnhof ist geöffnet.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Der Bahnhof ist ****nicht**** geöffnet.* (Negates adjective) [Day 8, 11 core, Unit 19]

### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–3

- Retrieve the regular verb *arbeiten* (*ich arbeite*, *Sie arbeiten*) [Unit 12] and spatial coordinates (*hier/dort*) [Day 6 core] to construct complete, contrastive negative sentences.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Transit Counter Query (TBLT):** The learner roleplays standing at a train station ticketing window [Day 8 core]. They must greet the agent formally, present their address and ticket, explain that the ticket machine is not working, and state that they do not have a ticket:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich fahre mit dem Zug. Das ist meine Adresse. Aber der Drucker dort funktioniert nicht. Ich habe keine Fahrkarte. Können Sie mir helfen?" [Days 8, 24, 26 core, Unit 6, 18, 19]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the agent asks for a different document, the learner immediately deploys: **"Es tut mir leid. Ich verstehe nicht."**[Day 25 core] to request a simplified explanation.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Agent - Formal):* "Guten Tag. Ist Ihre Fahrkarte hier?" [Day 24 core]
- *Learner:* "Nein, der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich habe keine Fahrkarte." [Day 24, 26 core, Unit 18, 19]
- *Native (Agent - Formal):* "Ah, kein Problem. Ich schreibe das Ticket auf." [Day 9, 13 core]
- *Learner:* "Vielen Dank! Tschüss!" [Day 2 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Spaced Retrieval Booster:** Review your personal German grammar journal. Draw a simple, two-column flowchart contrasting *nicht* (for actions and modifiers) vs. *kein* (for nouns), and write one personally relevant example for each.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can correctly choose between *nicht* and *kein* to negate verbs, adjectives, and nouns [Unit 18, 19].
  - Can use the possessive frame "Das ist meine..." with proper feminine noun agreement [Day 24 core].

---

### DAY 24 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing *die Fahrkarte*, *die Adresse*, *Das ist meine...*) [Day 24 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Negation contrast theory, clause-final *nicht* syntax, adjective negation) [Unit 18, 19].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: grammar-reconstruction games matching *nicht* vs. *kein*; 45 mins: reading and transcribing transit notices; 45 mins: practicing the palatal fricative /ç/ in *nicht*).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Walking through your home, finding broken or missing items, and narrating: *Die Kaffeemaschine funktioniert nicht. Ich habe kein Brot.*) [2, Day 17 core, Unit 18, 19].

## DAY 25: Possessive Determiners in Nominative & Accusative Masculine Shift

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to declare possession of workspace tools using singular possessive determiners (*mein*, *dein*, *Ihr*), apply correct accusative endings to masculine possessive direct objects (*meinen*, *deinen*, *Ihren*), and deploy the repair chunk *Es tut mir leid, Ich verstehe nicht* [Day 25 core, Unit 20].

### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor presents singular possessive determiners (*mein*, *dein*, *Ihr*) and their accusative masculine inflections (*meinen*, *deinen*, *Ihren*) [Unit 20].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit explanation showing that possessives decline exactly like *ein* and *kein* [Unit 20]. They observe that masculine possessives in direct object positions must take the ending **-en**(*meinen*), while feminine (*meine*) and neuter (*mein*) forms remain identical to the nominative [Unit 20].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short office interactions and identifies whether a speaker is referring to their own item (*mein*) or the formal partner's item (*Ihr*) in the accusative case [Unit 20].
- **RETRIEVE:** Retrieval of nominative possessive determiners from Week 3 [Unit 20].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical gap-fills inserting correct possessive suffixes based on gender and case prompts (*Ich suche \_\_\_\_\_ [my] Schlüssel* $\rightarrow$ *meinen*) [Unit 20].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work sorting mixed-up office belongings: *Ist das Ihr Drucker? — Nein, das ist mein Drucker. Suchen Sie Ihren Schlüssel?* [Unit 20].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching a guesthouse registration counter to verify contact details.
- **REVISIT:** Focused pronunciation of the tense, long vowel /i:/ in formal *Ihr* and *Ihre* [Unit 20].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Es tut mir leid, Ich verstehe nicht** (I am sorry, I don't understand / Communication Repair Formula) [Day 25 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The long vowel **"Ihr" (/i:ɐ̯/)** and the diphthong **"mein" (/aɪ/)** in connected possessive structures [Unit 20].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /i:ɐ̯/ (Tense your lips, place your tongue high and forward, and let it flow into a soft, relaxed vocalic breath).
  2. *Word:* *Ihren* [ˈi:ʁən] [Unit 20].
  3. *Phrase:* *Ihren Schlüssel* [ˈi:ʁən ˈʃlʏsəl] [Unit 20].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich suche Ihren Schlüssel.* [Unit 20].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Es tut mir leid, ich suche meinen Drucker dort.* (Ensure clear, smooth boundary transitions without swallowing the terminal nasal endings [Day 25 core, Unit 20]).

### 5. Minimum Explanation/Theory

- **Possessive Determiner Case Declensions:**
  - **IMPORTANT:** Possessive determiners decline exactly like *ein* and *kein* [Unit 20]. The masculine direct object accusative shift applies to possessives, appending the suffix **-en** to masculine nouns while keeping feminine and neuter forms structurally stable [Unit 20]: $$\begin{array}{l|c|c|c} \text{Owner (Case)} & \text{Masculine (der Schlüssel)} & \text{Feminine (die Fahrkarte)} & \text{Neuter (das Handy)} \ \hline \text{my (ich - Acc.)} & \text{Ich suche \textbf{meinen} Schlüssel.} & \text{Ich brauche \textbf{meine} Fahrkarte.} & \text{Ich habe \textbf{mein} Handy.} \ \text{your (du - Acc.)} & \text{Ich suche \textbf{deinen} Schlüssel.} & \text{Ich brauche \textbf{deine} Fahrkarte.} & \text{Ich habe \textbf{dein} Handy.} \ \text{your (Sie - Acc.)} & \text{Ich suche \textbf{Ihren} Schlüssel.} & \text{Ich brauche \textbf{Ihre} Fahrkarte.} & \text{Ich habe \textbf{Ihr} Handy.} \end{array}$$
  - **DEFER:** Plural possessive endings and dative possessive declensions [Unit 20].

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Acoustic Discrimination:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *"Ich brauche Ihren Schlüssel"* vs. *"Ich brauche Ihr Handy"* [Unit 16, 20]. Identify how the final syllable [-en] in *Ihren* changes the rhythmic cadence of the sentence [Unit 20].
- **Dialogue Mapping:** Listen to a receptionist sorting office keys. Match the spoken names to their corresponding possessive references on a ledger worksheet.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Substitution Drills:** The teacher prompts: *"ich / suchen / mein Schlüssel"* $\rightarrow$ Learner says: *Ich suche meinen Schlüssel.* [Unit 20]. Prompt: *"Sie / haben / Ihr Handy"* $\rightarrow$ Learner says: *Sie haben Ihr Handy.* [Unit 20].
- **Repair Drill:** Rehearse delivering the formal core repair formula: *Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht.* with a natural, polite falling intonation [Day 25 core].

### 8. Reading Practice

- Read an un-simplified graded dialog:
  - *A: Guten Tag. Suchen Sie Ihren Schlüssel, Herr Schmidt?*
  - *B: Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. ... Ah, mein Schlüssel! Ja, ich suche meinen Schlüssel. Danke!* [Day 25 core, Unit 20]
- Highlight all possessive determiners and map their case endings based on noun gender [Unit 20].

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Guided Possessive Composition:** Write four sentences describing your workspace setup, using Nominative and Accusative possessives:
  1. `Das ist mein Drucker.` [Unit 20]
  2. `Ich suche meinen Schlüssel.` [Unit 20]
  3. `Haben Sie mein Handy, Herr Schmidt?` [Unit 20]
  4. `Ich brauche Ihre E-Mail-Adresse.` [Day 24 core, Unit 20]

### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Personal Sentences (Possessive Accusatives).
- **Execution:** Formulate personal ownership and search statements about your own surroundings, utilizing the correct masculine accusative possessive endings:
  - *I am looking for my key.* $\rightarrow$ *Ich suche ****meinen**** Schlüssel.* [Unit 20]
  - *Do you have your ticket (formal)?* $\rightarrow$ *Haben Sie ****Ihre**** Fahrkarte?* [Day 24 core, Unit 20]
  - *He is looking for his printer.* $\rightarrow$ *Er sucht ****seinen**** Drucker.* [Unit 16, 20]

### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–3

- Retrieve the definite article genders (*der Schlüssel*, *das Handy*, *die Fahrkarte*) from Weeks 1–3 [Day 6, 8, 24 core, Unit 13] to correctly identify the target endings for today's possessive drills.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Lost Belongings Counter (TBLT):** The learner roleplays visiting a municipal lost-and-found desk (*Fundbüro*) in Germany [Unit 1]. They must greet the clerk formally, state that they have lost their phone and key, use possessive accusatives, and handle unexpected questions using the Day 25 repair formula:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich suche meinen Schlüssel und mein Handy. Haben Sie meinen Schlüssel hier? — [Clerk mumbles] — Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Noch einmal, bitte. Danke!" [Days 1, 25 core, Unit 11, 20]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the clerk uses unfamiliar vocabulary, the learner immediately deploys: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 3, 25 core] to manage the input velocity and keep the interaction in German.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Clerk - Formal):* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie Ihre Adresse?" [Day 24 core]
- *Learner:* "Nein, ich suche meinen Schlüssel." [Unit 20]
- *Native (Clerk - Formal):* "Ah, der Schlüssel für das Büro?" [Unit 11]
- *Learner:* "Ja! Das ist mein Schlüssel. Danke." [Unit 20]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Virtual Tour:** Look at an online workspace photo or room diagram. Practice pointing to different objects and naming their hypothetical owners using Nominative and Accusative possessives (*mein/meinen*, *dein/deinen*, *Ihr/Ihren*) [Unit 20].

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can accurately apply the masculine accusative possessive endings (*meinen*, *deinen*, *Ihren*) in active speech and writing [Unit 20].
  - Can deploy the core repair formula "Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht" to resolve communication breakdowns [Day 25 core].

---

### DAY 25 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *Es tut mir leid, Ich verstehe nicht*) [Day 25 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Possessive article paradigms, masculine shift rules, possessive-direct object integration) [Unit 20].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: possessive matching and gap-fill card drills; 45 mins: lost-and-found roleplay simulations; 45 mins: practicing long /i:/ transitions in *Ihren*).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing your own ownership and потеря log aloud: *Das ist mein Handy, aber ich suche meinen Schlüssel.*) [Unit 20].
