## DAY 26: Polite Request split-brackets — Modal Verbs (*können, müssen, möchten*)

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to declare obligations and desires using the modal verb split-brackets *können*, *müssen*, and *möchten*, consistently send the uninflected infinitive main verb to the absolute end of the clause, and use the core office items *der Drucker* and *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht* [Day 26 core, Unit 21].

### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The teacher models split-bracket modal requests (*Können Sie...*, *Ich möchte...*, *Ich muss...*), emphasizing how they "clamp" around the sentence elements [Unit 21].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit syntactic guide showing that the conjugated modal sits in Position 2, while the uninflected infinitive main verb is launched to the absolute end of the clause [Unit 21].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short office requests and identifies the final infinitive verb that completes the sentence bracket [Unit 21].
- **RETRIEVE:** Retrieval of the unanalyzed request frame *Können Sie mir helfen?* from Week 2 [Day 9 core].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Scrambled word-ordering exercises to construct grammatically correct modal sentences [Unit 21].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work requesting assistance with office equipment failures: *Können Sie mir bitte helfen? Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.* [Day 26 core, Unit 21].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching a school coordinator to postpone an appointment or request equipment repairs.
- **REVISIT:** Pronunciation check of the rounded Umlaut "ö" (/ø:/) in *möchten* before finishing [Unit 21].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **der Drucker** (The printer / Masculine Noun) [Day 26 core]
- **Der Drucker funktioniert nicht** (The printer is not working / Sentence Frame) [Day 26 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The rounded vowel **"ö" (/ø:/)** in *möchten* and the terminal **"-en"** in infinitive verbs [Unit 21].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ø:/ (Round your lips tightly as if to say "o", but try to pronounce the English vowel "e"—similar to the vowel in the English word "hurt" but tense and rounded).
  2. *Word:* *möchten* [ˈmœçtən] [Unit 21].
  3. *Phrase:* *einen Termin verschieben* [ˈaɪnən tɛʁˈmi\:n fɛʁˈʃi\:bən] [Day 22 core].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich möchte den Termin verschieben.* [Day 22 core, Unit 21].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Können Sie mir helfen? Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.* (Maintain solid, rounded lip posture for *können* and *möchten* [Day 9, 26 core, Unit 21]).

### 5. Minimum Explanation/Theory

- **The Split-Bracket Modal Syntax:**
  - **IMPORTANT / LEARN AS A CHUNK:** In German main clauses using modal verbs (*können, müssen, möchten*), the sentence operates as a split-bracket construction [Unit 21]. The conjugated modal verb occupies **Position 2** (V2 anchor), while the uninflected main verb in the infinitive (ending in **-en**) is sent to the **absolute end of the sentence** [Unit 21]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|c} \text{Subject (Pos 1)} & \text{Modal (Pos 2)} & \text{Modifier (Pos 3)} & \text{Infinitive (Absolute End)} \ \hline \text{Ich} & \text{\textbf{möchte}} & \text{den Termin} & \text{\textbf{verschieben}.} \ \text{Können} & \text{\textbf{Sie}} & \text{mir bitte} & \text{\textbf{helfen?}} \ \text{Ich} & \text{\textbf{muss}} & \text{heute} & \text{\textbf{arbeiten}.} \end{array}$$
  - **MUST MASTER:** The uninflected infinitive is permanently placed at the absolute end of the clause [Unit 21].
  - **DEFER:** Complete conjugation tables for *können* and *müssen* across all plural pronouns [Unit 21].

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Bracket Parsing:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing modal sentences [Unit 21]. Tap your pencil once when the modal is heard, and twice when the final infinitive verb is pronounced at the end of the sentence.
- **Error Detection:** Listen to three non-native sentences. Identify whether the speaker incorrectly placed the main verb before the modifier (V2 violation) by marking a checklist.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Modal Transformations (V2 to Modal Bracket):**
  - *Ich verschiebe den Termin.* $\rightarrow$ *Ich möchte den Termin verschieben.* [Day 22 core, Unit 21].
  - *Ich arbeite heute.* $\rightarrow$ *Ich muss heute arbeiten.* [Unit 12, 21].
- **Troubleshooting Reporting:** Point to a broken printer icon and state: *Können Sie mir helfen? Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.* [Day 9, 26 core].

### 8. Reading Practice

- Read an office coordination ticket:
  - *Status: Der Drucker funktioniert heute nicht. Ich muss meine E-Mail-Bestätigung schicken, aber ich kann nicht drucken. Können Sie helfen?* [Day 15, 18, 26 core, Unit 21]
- Underline the modal auxiliary in Position 2 and draw a bracket arrow leading to the infinitive verb at the absolute end [Unit 21].

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Bracket Construction:** Write down the scrambled sentences in your workbook using correct modal split-bracket syntax:
  1. `ich / heute / müssen / arbeiten` $\rightarrow$ *Ich muss heute arbeiten.* [Unit 21]
  2. `Sie / können / helfen / mir` $\rightarrow$ *Können Sie mir helfen?* [Day 9 core, Unit 21]
  3. `ich / möchten / bezahlen / jetzt` $\rightarrow$ *Ich möchte jetzt bezahlen.* [Day 20 core, Unit 21]

### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Modal Verb Transformations (Polite Requests).
- **Execution:** Transform simple V2 statements into polite modal requests, sending the main verb infinitive to the absolute end:
  - *Ich trinke Kaffee.* $\rightarrow$ *Ich möchte Kaffee trinken.* [Day 17 core, Unit 21]
  - *Ich fahre mit dem Zug.* $\rightarrow$ *Ich muss mit dem Zug fahren.* [Unit 6, 21]
  - *Ich buchstabiere meinen Namen.* $\rightarrow$ *Können Sie Ihren Namen buchstabieren?* [Unit 3, 21]

### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–3

- Retrieve the singular modal conjugation forms *muss* and *möchte* [Unit 21] and transit chunks (*mit dem Zug*) from Week 2 [Unit 6] to populate the modal frames.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Office Troubleshooter (TBLT):** The learner roleplays experiencing an office equipment breakdown [Day 26 core]. They must approach their supervisor, formally report that their phone or printer is not working, explain their task obligation using *müssen*, and request assistance:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag, Herr Schmidt. Können Sie mir helfen? Der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich muss meine E-Mail schicken. Ich habe keine Zeit. Danke!" [Days 9, 15, 22, 26 core, Unit 21]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the supervisor is busy and speaks rapidly, the learner deploys: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Noch einmal, bitte."** [Day 1, 25 core] to prompt a slow, clear repetition.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Supervisor - Formal):* "Guten Tag. Funktioniert Ihr Drucker nicht?" [Day 26 core]
- *Learner:* "Nein, der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich muss arbeiten." [Day 26 core, Unit 21]
- *Native (Supervisor - Formal):* "Ah, das ist ein Problem. Ich helfe Ihnen." [Day 13 core]
- *Learner:* "Vielen Dank! Tschüss!" [Day 2 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Virtual Office Interaction:** Spend 15 minutes navigating a simplified German office simulation or watching a short workplace vlog online. Focus on spotting how many times the split-brackets *Können Sie...* and *ich muss...* are used to coordinate daily work tasks.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can construct polite requests and obligation statements using modal split-bracket structures with infinitives locked at the absolute end [Unit 21].
  - Can state "Der Drucker funktioniert nicht" and request help appropriately [Day 26 core].

---

### DAY 26 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *der Drucker*, *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht*) [Day 26 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Modal aux-verb split-bracket theory, infinitive positioning rules, workplace coordinate reviews) [Unit 21].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: modal syntax block-shuffling; 45 mins: transcribing spoken workplace requests; 45 mins: practicing rounded Umlaut lip postures [/ø:/]).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Formulating helpful modal requests aloud during daily routines: *Ich möchte einen Kaffee trinken. Ich muss arbeiten.*) [Unit 21].

---

## DAY 27: Separable Verbs (*anrufen, anfangen*) & Temporal V2 Inversion Flexibility

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to conjugate and split present-tense separable verbs (*anrufen, anfangen*), place temporal adverbs (*heute, morgen, jetzt*) in Position 1 while executing Subject-Verb-Subject V2 inversion, and use the core items *schicken*and *die Nachricht* [Day 27 core, Unit 22, 23].

### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The teacher presents separable verbs (*anrufen, anfangen*) and temporal adverbs (*heute, morgen, jetzt*), demonstrating how starting a sentence with a temporal adverb triggers Subject-Verb-Subject inversion to preserve V2 order [Unit 22, 23].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit syntax guide explaining that (1) the separable base verb splits, sending the prefix *an-* to the end of the clause, and (2) starting with a temporal adverb forces the subject pronoun to jump to Position 3 to keep the conjugated verb in Position 2 [Unit 22, 23].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to native spoken statements and identifies whether a speaker says *Ich rufe an* or *Heute fange ich... an*, transcribing the final prefixes [Unit 22, 23].
- **RETRIEVE:** Retrieval of the temporal adverbs *heute* and *morgen* from previous weeks [Day 12 core].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical restructuring drills converting S-V-O sentences into inverted temporal-first statements.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work coordinating daily work routines: *Heute fange ich die Arbeit an. Jetzt rufe ich den Chef an.* [Day 15, 16, 27 core, Unit 22, 23].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Texting a supervisor to confirm your study schedule and calling a doctor's office.
- **REVISIT:** Practicing the glottal stop (*Knacklaut*) before the prefix *an* at the end of the sentence [Unit 22].

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **schicken** (To send / Verb) [Day 27 core]
- **die Nachricht** (The message / Feminine Noun) [Day 27 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The glottal stop **"an"** (/an/) and initial consonant clusters **"sch"** (/ʃ/) in *schicken* and *die Nachricht* [Day 27 core, Unit 22].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ʃ/ $\rightarrow$ /an/ (Produce a loud "sh" with rounded, projected lips, and a clean, abrupt throat-stop right before pronouncing *an*).
  2. *Word:* *schicken* [ˈʃɪkən] $\rightarrow$ *an* [an] [Day 27 core, Unit 22].
  3. *Phrase:* *ich schicke* [ɪç ˈʃɪkə] $\rightarrow$ *rufe... an* [ˈʁu\:fə... an] [Day 27 core, Unit 22].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich schicke heute die Nachricht.* [Day 27 core, Unit 23].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Morgen fange ich die Arbeit an. Jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht.* (Keep the final prefix *an*distinctly stressed and preceded by a brief glottal catch [Day 15, 27 core, Unit 22, 23]).

### 5. Minimum Explanation/Theory

- **Separable Verbs & Temporal-Fronting V2 Syntax:**
  - **MUST MASTER (V2 Inversion):** German is strictly a **Verb-Second (V2)** language. If a sentence begins with a temporal adverb (*heute, morgen, jetzt*), the conjugated verb **must remain in Position 2**, forcing the subject pronoun to invert to **Position 3** [Unit 23]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|c|c} \text{Pos 1: Time Adverb} & \text{Pos 2: Verb (Locked)} & \text{Pos 3: Subject} & \text{Pos 4: Object} & \text{Pos 5: Prefix (End)} \ \hline \text{Heute} & \text{\textbf{fange}} & \text{ich} & \text{die Arbeit} & \text{\textbf{an}.} \ \text{Morgen} & \text{\textbf{rufe}} & \text{ich} & \text{den Chef} & \text{\textbf{an}.} \ \text{Jetzt} & \text{\textbf{schicke}} & \text{ich} & \text{die Nachricht.} & \end{array}$$
  - **IMPORTANT:** Separable base verbs (*ruf-*, *fang-*) are conjugated regularly, while the prefix *an* is detached and placed at the absolute end of the clause [Unit 22].
  - **DEFER:** Plural separable verb agreements and other complex prefixes like *ab-* or *aus-* [Unit 22].

### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Prefix Tracking:** Listen to three native speakers describing their daily schedules. Write down the exact word that sits at the absolute end of each sentence to track the split prefix *an* [Unit 22, 23].
- **Word Boundary Detection:** Listen to a speaker saying *"Jetzt schicke ich..."* [Day 27 core]. Identify how the final consonant of *jetzt* links with the starting consonant of *schicke* in connected speech.

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Inversion Conversions (S-V-O to Inverted V2):**
  - *Ich rufe morgen den Arzt an.* $\rightarrow$ *Morgen rufe ich den Arzt an.* [Day 21 core, Unit 22, 23].
  - *Ich bin jetzt fertig.* $\rightarrow$ *Jetzt bin ich fertig.* [Day 29 core, Unit 23].
- **Status Update reporting:** Practice saying: *Jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht.* with clear, natural connected-speech rhythm [Day 27 core, Unit 23].

### 8. Reading Practice

- Read an office team chat message:
  - *Alex: Guten Tag. Ich bin krank. Morgen gehe ich zum Arzt. Jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht an den Chef.*
  - *Chef: Gute Besserung, Alex! Heute fange ich die Arbeit ohne Alex an.* [Day 15, 16, 21, 23, 27 core, Unit 12, 22]
- Identify all split separable structures and circle the inverted subject-verb patterns.

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Temporal-Fronting Composition:** Write four sentences in your workbook starting with temporal adverbs, maintaining strict V2 inversion and separable splits:
  1. `Heute fange ich die Arbeit an.` [Day 15 core, Unit 22, 23]
  2. `Morgen rufe ich den Chef an.` [Day 16 core, Unit 22, 23]
  3. `Jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht.` [Day 27 core, Unit 23]
  4. `Heute habe ich keine Zeit.` [Day 22 core, Unit 23]

### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Temporal-Fronted Inversions (V2 Flexibility).
- **Execution:** Rearrange scrambled word groups to start with temporal adverbs, executing Subject-Verb inversion to keep the verb in Position 2:
  - *jetzt / ich / den Arzt / anrufen* $\rightarrow$ *Jetzt rufe ich den Arzt an.* [Day 21 core, Unit 22, 23]
  - *heute / wir / mit dem Bus / fahren* $\rightarrow$ (Receptive) *Heute fahren wir mit dem Bus.* [Unit 6, 23]
  - *morgen / die Nachricht / ich / schicken* $\rightarrow$ *Morgen schicke ich die Nachricht.* [Day 27 core, Unit 23]

### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–3

- Retrieve the nouns *Arzt* [Day 21 core], *Chef* [Day 16 core], and *Arbeit* [Day 15 core] to populate the direct object slots in today's separable verb structures.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Sick Leave Update (TBLT):** The learner roleplays calling their supervisor to report an illness and update their shift status [Day 21 core, Unit 22]. They must greet them, explain they are sick, state that they are calling the doctor, and confirm that they are sending their registration message now:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag, Herr Schmidt. Ich bin John. Ich bin krank. Heute rufe ich den Arzt an. Jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht. Danke. Tschüss!" [Days 21, 25, 27 core, Unit 22, 23]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the supervisor asks a follow-up scheduling question, the learner uses their automated repair chunk: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht."** [Day 25 core] to manage the breakdown.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Supervisor - Formal):* "Guten Tag, Herr John. Sind Sie krank?" [Day 21 core]
- *Learner:* "Ja, ich bin krank. Heute rufe ich den Arzt an." [Day 21 core, Unit 23]
- *Native (Supervisor - Formal):* "Schade. Schicken Sie die Nachricht?" [Day 27 core]
- *Learner:* "Ja, jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht. Tschüss!" [Day 2, 27 core, Unit 23]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Spacing Repetition Trainer:** Use your digital vocabulary tutor app for 15 minutes. Practice reconstructing scrambled German V2 clauses containing *heute*, *morgen*, and *jetzt* to solidify the neurological routing of V2 inversion.

### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can correctly conjugate and split separable verbs (*anrufen, anfangen*) in active speech and writing [Unit 22].
  - Can apply Subject-Verb inversion when starting a sentence with *heute, morgen,* or *jetzt*, keeping the verb locked in Position 2 [Unit 23].

---

### DAY 27 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *schicken*, *die Nachricht*) [Day 27 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Separable verb mechanics, V2 inversion rules, temporal adverb syntax) [Unit 22, 23].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: physical block-swapping of inverted V2 clauses; 45 mins: transcribing voicemail schedules; 45 mins: practicing the glottal stop before final prefixes).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing your daily schedule update aloud in front of a mirror: *Heute fange ich die Arbeit an. Jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht.*) [2, Day 15, 27 core, Unit 23].

---

## DAY 28: Comprehensive Synthesis, Task-Based Graduation, and Milestone TBLT Tasks

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to synthesize all Week 4 grammatical, lexical, and phonetic coordinates to execute a multi-stage, interactive appointment rescheduling task and a separate workplace status report entirely in German [Unit 40].

### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The instructor reviews all Week 4 structural targets, focusing on Accusative case endings, negation contrasts (*nicht* vs. *kein*), modal brackets, separable splits, and temporal-fronting V2 inversion [Unit 40].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the comprehensive synthesis flowchart showing how greetings, apologies, lost equipment reports, scheduling, and office status logs link together into cohesive, realistic daily transaction loops.
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner scans a complete written workplace exchange and highlights any V2 violations, case inflection errors, or register inconsistencies.
- **RETRIEVE:** Systematic spaced recall of all Core and main curriculum vocabulary from Weeks 1–4.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Reordering scrambled dialogues containing scheduling, purchases, and commuting updates.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work executing the complete Week 4 checkout and administrative simulation from start to finish.
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching the formal checkout examiner to complete the Week 4 Milestone Tasks.
- **REVISIT:** Final pronunciation audit of terminal devoicings, rounded Umlauts, and sibilant clusters before the test.

### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Ich bin fertig** (I am finished / I am ready / Interaction Formula) [Day 28 core]
- **Ich mache eine Pause** (I am taking a break / Interaction Formula) [Day 28 core]

### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Consolidating **terminal devoicing**, **Umlauts**, **palatal fricatives**, and **connected speech liaison** across all target Week 4 items [Unit 40].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ç/ $\rightarrow$ /ø:/ $\rightarrow$ /ts/ $\rightarrow$ /ʃ/ $\rightarrow$ /kʰ/ (Reviewing rounded vowels, sibilants, aspirated velars, and smiling hushing sounds).
  2. *Word:* *Arzt* [a:ɐ̯tst] $\rightarrow$ *nicht* [nɪçt] $\rightarrow$ *Drucker* [ˈdʁʊkɐ] (Crisp voiceless consonant releases).
  3. *Phrase:* *Können Sie helfen* $\rightarrow$ *Heute fange ich* (Practice sliding word boundaries smoothly without choppy gaps).
  4. *Sentence:* *Heute fange ich die Arbeit an. Jetzt mache ich eine Pause.* [Day 15, 28 core, Unit 23].
  5. *Connected Speech:* Deliver the entire administrative presentation and scheduling request as a fluid, connected breathing unit with natural intonation.

### 5. Minimum Theory/Explanation

- **The Syntactic Hierarchy of the German Clause:**
  - **MUST MASTER:** In every German main clause, the conjugated, finite verb remains locked in **Position 2**[Unit 1]. Temporal adverbs starting a sentence trigger Subject-Verb inversion [Unit 23]. Modal verbs create a split-bracket, sending the uninflected infinitive main verb to the **absolute end** [Unit 21]. Separable verbs split, launching the prefix to the **absolute end** [Unit 22]. $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|c|l} \text{Position 1} & \text{Position 2 (Verb: Locked)} & \text{Position 3 (Subject)} & \text{Modifier/Object} & \text{End Bracket (Prefix/Infinitive)} \ \hline \text{Heute} & \text{\textbf{muss}} & \text{ich} & \text{meinen Schlüssel} & \text{\textbf{suchen}.} \ \text{Jetzt} & \text{\textbf{rufe}} & \text{ich} & \text{den Chef} & \text{\textbf{an}.} \ \text{Morgen} & \text{\textbf{schicke}} & \text{ich} & \text{die Nachricht.} & \end{array}$$

### 6. Listening Practice

- **C-Test Transcription:** Listen to a multi-stage native dialogue recorded at a municipal office. Fill in the missing letters and case suffixes on your worksheet, focusing on transcribing masculine accusative endings, modal requests, and temporal V2 inversions.
- **Register Validation:** Audit three audio clips and confirm if the speakers maintain consistent formal register (*Sie/sind/haben/können*).

### 7. Speaking Practice

- **The Roleplay rehearsal:** Practice reciting the complete, multi-stage lost belongings, scheduling, and shift update dialogues with natural sentence stress.
- **Rapid Retrieval Round:** Immediately answer prompt cards showed by the instructor:
  - *"Today - call Chef"* $\rightarrow$ *Heute rufe ich den Chef an.* [Day 16 core, Unit 23]
  - *"Now - take break"* $\rightarrow$ *Jetzt mache ich eine Pause.* [Day 28 core, Unit 23]

### 8. Reading Practice

- The student reads an un-simplified graded extensive text containing 98% familiar vocabulary:
  - *Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich wohne in Berlin. Heute fahre ich mit dem Zug zur Arbeit. Das ist mein Büro. Aber der Drucker funktioniert heute nicht. Ich habe keinen Schlüssel für das Büro. Ich habe keine Zeit. Jetzt rufe ich den Chef an. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben. Jetzt bin ich fertig. Ich mache eine Pause.*
- The student identifies and verifies all V2 positions in the text [Unit 1].

### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Essay Composition:** The learner writes their own lost belongings and schedule update log (5 sentences) in their notebook, ensuring correct capitalization, spelling of suffixes, and perfect verb-second syntax.

### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** The Mini-Dialogue Creation (Week 4 Synthesis).
- **Execution:** Construct a complete, coherent conversation between a student and a registration officer, combining all learned Week 4 coordinates:
  - *Student:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John."
  - *Agent:* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie Ihren Schlüssel?"
  - *Student:* "Nein, ich suche meinen Drucker. Aber der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich muss meine Nachricht schicken."
  - *Agent:* "Kein Problem. Ich helfe Ihnen."
  - *Student:* "Danke! Jetzt bin ich fertig. Ich mache eine Pause."

### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–3

- The learner systematically retrieves greetings, name spelling, country of origin, and repair formulas from previous weeks to populate the final checkpoint interactions.

### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Double-Milestone Graduation (TBLT Tasks):** The learner roleplays navigating two distinct, realistic daily tasks [Unit 40]:
  - **Task A (The Appointment and Key Query):** The learner approaches the office counter, apologizes, declares they have no time today, requests to reschedule their appointment using modal verbs, and verifies they are looking for their key:
    - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich habe heute keine Zeit. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben, bitte. Ich suche meinen Schlüssel. Haben Sie meinen Schlüssel?" [Days 1, 22 core, Unit 20, 21]
  - **Task B (The Broken Printer and Break Status):** The learner reports that the workstation printer is broken, confirms they must send their registration email, and announces they are taking a break:
    - *Learner:* "Entschuldigung! Der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich muss meine Nachricht schicken. Jetzt bin ich fertig. Ich mache eine Pause. Danke!" [Days 4, 26, 27, 28 core, Unit 19, 21, 23]

### 13. Communication Repair

- If the agent speaks too rapidly, the learner immediately deploys: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 3, 25 core] to manage the input velocity without breaking character.

### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Clerk - Formal):* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie Ihren Termin?" [Day 22 core]
- *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben." [Day 22 core, Unit 21]
- *Native (Clerk - Formal):* "Morgen ist frei. Passt das?" [Day 12 core]
- *Learner:* "Ja, morgen passt gut. Jetzt bin ich fertig. Ich mache eine Pause. Tschüss!" [Days 2, 12, 28 core]

### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Extensive Reading Sprint:** Spend 15 minutes reading a short graded story about a commuter's administrative hurdles in Munich. Focus on enjoying the narrative flow, noting how the structural boundaries of accusative, negation, and modal request frames are naturally embedded in the text.

### 16. Mastery Check

- The learner successfully completes the Day 28 Milestone Tasks under un-speeded roleplay conditions, demonstrating V2 syntax stability, correct accusative endings, and spontaneous repair deployment [Unit 40].

---

### DAY 28 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 20 minutes (Reviewing all Days 22–28 items) [Day 28 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 40 minutes (Verb-Second syntactic synthesis, separable splits, temporal V2 inversion) [Unit 40].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (Mirror presentation, question-answer conversion drills, spelling and phone number dictations, mock testing).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Completing a final self-assessment checklist and reviewing incorrect pronunciation entries).

# WEEK 4 REVIEW

### 1. What should now be recognized (Receptive Mastery)

- The semantic distinction between **direct objects (Accusative)** and **subjects (Nominative)** in complex written German texts [Unit 16].
- The structural split-brackets of modal verbs (*können*, *müssen*, *möchten*) and separable verbs (*anrufen*, *anfangen*) in native spoken input [Unit 21, 22].
- Acoustic cues indicating the masculine accusative shift (*den*, *einen*, *keinen*, *meinen*, *Ihren*) in connected native speech [Unit 16, 18, 20].
- The structural function of the negative adverb *nicht* as opposed to the negative determiner *kein/keine/keinen* [Unit 18, 19].

### 2. What should now be actively produced (Active Mastery)

- Applying the masculine accusative suffix **-en** to masculine direct objects (*den*, *einen*, *keinen*, *meinen*, *Ihren*, *deinen*) after transitive verbs (*haben*, *brauchen*, *suchen*) [Unit 16, 18, 20].
- Negating nouns using *kein/keine/keinen* with correct case and gender inflections [Unit 18].
- Negating verbs, adjectives, and entire clauses using *nicht* in the correct syntactic position [Unit 19].
- Formulating polite requests and obligation statements using modal split-brackets (*Können Sie...*, *Ich möchte...*, *Ich muss...*) with infinitives locked at the absolute end of the clause [Unit 21].
- Conjugating and splitting separable verbs (*anrufen*, *anfangen*) in the present tense [Unit 22].
- Executing temporal-fronting V2 inversion when starting sentences with *heute*, *morgen*, or *jetzt* (*Heute fange ich die Arbeit an*) [Unit 23].
- Deploying survival apologies and repair formulas (*Es tut mir leid, Ich verstehe nicht*) [Day 25 core].

### 3. What should remain mainly receptive

- Dative case case-marking systems (except for frozen, unanalyzed chunks like *mit dem Zug*) [Unit 6].
- Plural case inflections and plural possessive determiner declensions [Unit 11, 20].
- Complex separable prefixes (such as *ab-*, *aus-*, *mit-*) in spontaneous, un-scaffolded speech [Unit 22].

### 4. Pronunciation Gains

- Intelligible production of the palatal fricative **"ich-laut"** (/ç/) in *nicht*, avoiding English "sh" or hard coughing defaults [Unit 19].
- Clean, aspirated voiceless velar stop **"K"** (/kʰ/) at the beginning of *kein*, *keine*, and *keinen* [Unit 18].
- Correct, un-swallowed articulation of the terminal suffix **-en** (/ən/) in *einen*, *keinen*, *meinen*, and *Ihren* [Unit 16, 20].
- Solid rounded lip posture for the Umlaut **"ö"** (/ø:/) in *möchten* [Unit 21].
- A distinct glottal catch (*Knacklaut*) before vowel-initial suffixes and prefixes like *an* [Unit 22].

### 5. Sentence-Building Gains

- Progressed from Nominative possessives to **Accusative masculine substitutions** and **contrastive negations** [Unit 16, 18, 19].
- Developed the syntactic flexibility to construct **split-bracket modal request frames** and **separable verb structures** [Unit 21, 22].
- Successfully automated **temporal-fronted V2 inversions** to vary sentence starters while keeping the verb locked securely in Position 2 [Unit 23].

### 6. Practical Abilities Gained

- Can navigate municipal office and school lost-and-found transactions, declaring and locating lost keys and phones [Unit 20].
- Can report technical failures of workstation equipment (*Der Drucker funktioniert nicht*) and request supervisor assistance formally [Day 26 core, Unit 21].
- Can handle complex medical and administrative appointment rescheduling loops, stating lack of time and proposing alternative slots [Day 22 core, Unit 21].
- Can manage transit updates, declaring ticket machine malfunctions and reporting lack of tickets formally [Day 24 core, Unit 19].

### 7. Weak Areas to Diagnose (Diagnostic Guide)

- **Masculine Suffix Omissions:** Correct the learner if they drop the *-en* ending on masculine direct objects (e.g., saying *Ich suche mein Schlüssel* instead of *Ich suche meinen Schlüssel*) [Unit 20].
- **Negation Conflation:** Correct the learner if they use *nicht* to negate nouns (e.g., saying *Ich habe nicht Schlüssel*instead of *Ich habe keinen Schlüssel*) [Unit 18, 19].
- **V2 Inversion Violations:** Ensure that when a sentence begins with a temporal adverb, the learner does not default to S-V-O (e.g., saying *Heute ich fange die Arbeit an* instead of *Heute fange ich die Arbeit an*) [Unit 23].
- **Infinitive Displacement:** Check that the learner consistently sends the uninflected infinitive to the absolute end of modal clauses, rather than grouping it with the auxiliary (e.g., *Ich möchte verschieben den Termin* instead of *Ich möchte den Termin verschieben*) [Unit 21].

---

# WEEK 4 MASTERY CHECK

This formal evaluation uses **qualitative, functional competence criteria** to determine readiness for the next phase, completely bypassing arbitrary speed, percentage, or trial-count gates [Newton & Nation, 2020].

```
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                 WEEK 4 MASTERY CHECKLIST                                        |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  1. PHONOLOGICAL INTELLIGIBILITY (Receptive & Productive)                                       |
|     -> Produces distinct palatal /ç/ (nicht), aspirated /kʰ/ (keinen), and rounded /ø:/ (möchten). |
|                                                                                                 |
|  2. CANONICAL V2 STABILITY WITH TEMPORAL FRONTING                                               |
|     -> Keeps the conjugated verb locked in Position 2 when beginning with heute, morgen, or jetzt.  |
|                                                                                                 |
|  3. ACCUSATIVE MASCULINE SUFFIX AGREEMENT                                                       |
|     -> Accurately appends the -en suffix to masculine direct objects after transitive verbs.    |
|                                                                                                 |
|  4. BRACKET SYNTAX RECOVERY                                                                     |
|     -> Sends infinitives (modals) and prefixes (separable verbs) to the absolute end of clauses. |
|                                                                                                 |
|  5. PORTABLE REPAIR ALIGNMENT                                                                   |
|     -> Spontaneously deploys "Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht" to slow down native input.   |
|                                                                                                 |
|  6. DOUBLE-STAGE TBLT INDEPENDENCE (Milestone Tasks)                                            |
|     -> Completes Task A (Appointment/Key Query) and Task B (Broken Printer/Break Status) cycles. |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

```

### 1. Pronunciation Check

- **Target Sound Isolation:** The learner reads aloud the following three sentences, which contain the critical phonetic contrasts of Week 4 [Vaughan-Rees, 2002]:
  1. *Ich möch-te heu-te mei-nen Ter-min ver-schie-ben.* (Evaluates rounded Umlaut /ø/ in *möchte*, palatal fricative /ç/ in *ich/möchte*, temporal V2 inversion, and the masculine accusative possessive shift with terminal nasal /ən/ in *meinen*).
  2. *Der Dru-cker funk-tio-niert nicht, ich ha-be kei-nen Schlüs-sel.* (Evaluates initial /ʃ/ in *Schlüssel*, palatal sibilant /ç/ in *nicht*, and direct object masculine noun negation in *keinen*).
  3. *Mor-gen ru-fe ich den Chef an. Jetzt bin ich fer-tig.* (Evaluates separable split prefix *an*, glottal stop *Knacklaut* before *an*, sharp terminal sibilant cluster /ts/ in *jetzt*, and Subject-Verb inversion).
- **Standard of Success:** Intelligible pronunciation. The learner must show a clear articulatory effort to produce German-specific sibilants, aspirated velar stops, and rounded vowels, avoiding English "w" slurs or flat vowels.

### 2. Functional Communication Check (The Municipal Desk & Shift Handover)

- **The Scenario:** The learner roleplays navigating an administrative checkpoint and updating their work supervisor in Germany. The examiner acts as the municipal clerk and the training supervisor [Unit 40].
- **The Tasks:** The learner must complete two separate, highly realistic communicative cycles under natural, un-speeded conditions:
  - **Task A (The Appointment and Key Query):**
    1. Initiate the conversation with a formal greeting (*Guten Tag*) [Day 1 core].
    2. Apologize and state that they have no time today (*Ich habe heute keine Zeit*) [Day 22 core].
    3. Formulate a polite request to postpone their appointment using modal verbs (*Ich möchte den Termin verschieben*) [Day 22 core, Unit 21].
    4. Declare that they are looking for their lost key using correct masculine accusative inflections (*Ich suche meinen Schlüssel*) [Unit 20].
    5. Handle administrative follow-up questions or mumbles using the Day 25 repair formula (*Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht*) [Day 25 core].
  - **Task B (The Broken Printer and Break Status):**
    1. Report that the office workstation printer is broken (*Der Drucker funktioniert nicht*) [Day 26 core].
    2. Explain their task obligation using obligation modals (*Ich muss meine Nachricht schicken*) [Day 27, 28 core, Unit 21].
    3. Announce their task completion and break status using V2 temporal-fronting inversion (*Jetzt bin ich fertig. Ich mache eine Pause.*) [Day 28 core, Unit 23].
    4. Bids farewell formally (*Auf Wiedersehen* or *Bis morgen!*) [Day 5, 14 core].
- **Standard of Success:** Conversational success. Minor grammatical slips (such as irregular verb endings or minor suffix omissions) are recast implicitly [Long, 2015]. The learner meets the criteria if meaning is successfully negotiated, the verb remains consistently in Position 2, modal and separable bracket structures lock verbs and prefixes at the absolute end, and any rapid native input is managed using German repair formulas (*Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht*) without reverting to English.

*The complete Week 4 (Days 22–28) detailed study plan is finalized, audited, and officially locked under the designated design parameters. Stop. Do not proceed to Week 5.*

# WEEK 5 — DAYS 29–35


# WEEK 5 STUDY PLAN: DATIVE FOUNDATIONS, LOCATION, DIRECTIONS, TRANSPORT, AND EVERYDAY SPATIAL COMMUNICATION

In Week 5, we transition the adult learner's cognitive focus to the **Dative Case Singular Baseline** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Building on the stable Nominative and Accusative categories established in Weeks 1–4, the learner's brain is now structurally prepared to process the indirect object system and spatial prepositions [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

Through **Transfer-Appropriate Processing (TAP)** and **High-Variability Phonetic Training (HVPT)**, the 7-day study plan below proceduralizes high-frequency dative prepositions (*mit, zu, bei, seit*), dative contractions (*im, am, zum, zur, beim, vom*), and the spatial boundary contrast (**Wo?** vs. **Wohin?**) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, 33, 90].

---

## DAILY EXECUTABLE SPRINT (DAYS 29–35)

---

### DAY 29: Locational Dative Contractions (*im, am*) and the Shift Handover

#### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to declare stationary locations using the copula *sein* with dative contractions **im** (*in dem*) and **am**(*an dem*) to answer the question **Wo?** (Where?) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], and report task completion using the Core frames *Ich bin fertig* and *Ich mache eine Pause* [Day 29 core].

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The locative contractions **im** (*in dem*) and **am** (*an dem*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit rule: stationary location (**Wo?**) triggers the **Dative case** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Masculine (*der*) and Neuter (*das*) nouns merge into the dative article **dem**, which contracts with prepositions: *in dem* \\(\rightarrow\\) **im**, *an dem* \\(\rightarrow\\) **am** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short office coordinates and circles whether a person or object is *im Büro* or *am Bahnhof* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RETRIEVE:** Spaced recall of office nouns (*das Büro*, *der Bahnhof*, *das Handy*) [Day 6, 8, 15 core].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Matching noun cards with the correct contraction (*Büro* \\(\rightarrow\\) *im Büro*, *Tisch* \\(\rightarrow\\) *am Tisch*).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** *Ich bin fertig* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin am Tisch fertig* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Jetzt bin ich am Tisch fertig* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partners point to an office map and ask *Wo ist Herr Schmidt?* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Er ist im Büro.*or *Er ist am Drucker.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 11, 26].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Texting a study group to state you are currently at the station or office.
- **REVISIT:** Practice the glottal stop (*Knacklaut*) before *im* (*ist [pause] im Büro*) [Unit 14].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **Ich bin fertig** (I am finished / Sentence Frame) [Day 29 core]
- **Ich mache eine Pause** (I am taking a break / Sentence Frame) [Day 29 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The terminal "ich-laut" (/ç/) in *fertig* [ˈfɛɐ̯tɪç] and the long open diphthong /aʊ/ in *Pause* [ˈpaʊzə] [Day 29 core].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ç/ $\rightarrow$ /aʊ/ (Hissing smile sound, followed by the rounded glide in "house").
  2. *Word:* *fertig* [ˈfɛɐ̯tɪç] $\rightarrow$ *Pause* [ˈpaʊzə] [Day 29 core].
  3. *Phrase:* *bin fertig* [bɪn ˈfɛɐ̯tɪç] $\rightarrow$ *mache eine Pause* [ˈmaxə ˈaɪnə ˈpaʊzə] [Day 29 core].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich bin fertig, ich mache eine Pause.* [Day 29 core].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Jetzt bin ich im Büro fertig. Ich mache eine Pause.* (Ensure terminal /ç/ remains soft and unvoiced [Day 29 core]).

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **The Stationary Location Rule (Wo?):**
  - **MUST MASTER:** Answering **Wo?** (Where?) with stationary prepositions *in* and *an* requires the **Dative Case** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Masculine (*der*) and Neuter (*das*) articles change to **dem** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. These systematically contract to prevent slurring [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - *in* + *dem* \\(\rightarrow\\) **im** (*im Büro*, *im Bahnhof*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
    - *an* + *dem* \\(\rightarrow\\) **am** (*am Tisch*, *am Drucker*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - **DEFER:** Plural dative, feminine dative *der* (introduced Day 31), and movement accusative *Wohin?* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Connected Transcription:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *im Büro* vs. *am Bahnhof*. Identify the subtle nasal differences between the bilabial /m/ releases: /ɪm ˈby:ʁo:/ vs. /am ˈba\:nho\:f/ [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Top-Down Gist:** Listen to three workplace voicemails. Fill in a spreadsheet marking who is currently finished working (*fertig*) and who is taking a break (*Pause*) [Day 29 core].

#### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Suffix Drills:** Convert noun prompts into dative locative blocks:
  - *"Drucker"* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin am Drucker.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 26 core].
  - *"Bahnhof"* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin im Bahnhof.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 8 core].
- **The Shift Handover:** Declare task completion: *Ich bin jetzt fertig. Ich mache eine Pause.* [Day 29 core, Unit 23].

#### 8. Reading Practice

- Scan a simple office whiteboard shift schedule:
  - *Herr Schmidt ist im Büro. Herr Wagner ist am Bahnhof. Sarah ist fertig, sie macht eine Pause.* [Day 29 core, Unit 11]
- Underline the locative contractions and circle the task-completion frames.

#### 9. Writing Practice

- **Guided Composition:** Write a 3-sentence digital message to your colleague:
  - `Guten Tag. Ich bin jetzt im Büro.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 11]
  - `Ich bin fertig.` [Day 29 core]
  - `Ich mache eine Pause.` [Day 29 core]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Noun to Dative Locative.
- **Execution:** Combine nouns with stationary contractions to answer the question *Wo bist du?*:
  - *das Büro* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin im Büro.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *der Bahnhof* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin am Bahnhof.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 8 core]
  - *der Drucker* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin am Drucker fertig.* [Day 26, 29 core]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–4

- Retrieve the temporal V2 inversion adverbs (*jetzt, heute, morgen*) from Week 4 to frame the schedule updates: *Jetzt bin ich am Bahnhof fertig.* [Day 8, 29 core, Unit 23].

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Shift Handover (TBLT):** The learner roleplays completing a training shift at their vocational workplace school [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. They approach the instructor, report that they are finished at the printer, and state they are taking a break:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag, Herr Schmidt. Ich bin jetzt am Drucker fertig. Ich mache eine Pause. Danke!" [Days 1, 26, 29 core, Unit 11]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the instructor speaks too rapidly, the learner immediately deploys the Week 4 repair: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht."** [Day 25 core] to prompt a simplified, slower check.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Supervisor):* "Guten Tag. Sind Sie im Büro fertig?" [Day 29 core, Unit 11]
- *Learner:* "Ja, ich bin fertig. Jetzt mache ich eine Pause." [Day 29 core, Unit 23]
- *Native (Supervisor):* "Alles klar. Kein Problem!" [Day 13 core]
- *Learner:* "Danke!" [Day 1 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **Input Flood:** Prompt an AI tutor: *"Give me 10 simple sentences using 'im' and 'am' to state locations of common office items in German. Keep them short and natural."* Read them aloud to build rhythmic fluency.

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can answer the question *Wo bist du?* using *im* and *am* dative contractions [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Can declare shift status using *Ich bin fertig* and *Ich mache eine Pause* [Day 29 core].

---

### DAY 29 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Focusing on *Ich bin fertig*, *Ich mache eine Pause*) [Day 29 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Dative locative case rules, contraction mechanics, Wo? syntax) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing the terminal "ich-laut" /ç/ in *fertig*; 60 mins: physical sentence block-swapping of locative statements; 45 mins: transcribing native office updates).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Self-narration: walking around your living room and declaring where you are: *Ich bin im Zimmer. Jetzt mache ich eine Pause.*) [3, Day 29 core].

## DAY 30: The Dative Preposition *mit* (Transit and Accompaniment) and The Colleague

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to express transit methods and accompaniment using the dative-governing preposition **mit** paired with correct singular masculine and feminine dative articles (*mit dem*, *mit der*), and identify group statements receptively using *wir* and *ihr* forms [Day 30 core, Unit 17].

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The dative preposition **mit** (by/with) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], the noun *der Kollege / die Kollegin* [Day 30 core], and receptive-only plural pronouns *wir* and *ihr* [Day 30 core, Unit 17].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit rule: *mit* always governs the dative case [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. The masculine/neuter article becomes **dem** (*mit dem Kollegen*, *mit dem Bus*), and the feminine article becomes **der** (*mit der Kollegin*, *mit der U-Bahn*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 6, 13].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to office dialogues containing plural pronouns (*wir, ihr*) to develop top-down comprehension of group statements without requiring active grammatical assembly [Day 30 core, Unit 17].
- **RETRIEVE:** Spaced recall of transit nouns (*Bus, Zug, U-Bahn*) from Week 2 [Unit 6].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical gap-fills matching *mit* with dative articles *dem* or *der* based on noun genders.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** *Ich fahre* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich fahre mit dem Kollegen* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Morgen fahre ich mit dem Kollegen mit dem Zug.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 6, 23].
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work coordinating travel: *Wie fährst du? — Ich fahre mit der Kollegin mit dem Bus.*[Day 30 core, Unit 6].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Telling a school registrar that you are traveling with your classmate today.
- **REVISIT:** Quick vocal training on the unstressed vocalic "er" in *der* [de:ɐ̯] vs. *dem* [de\:m] [Unit 13].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- **der Kollege / die Kollegin** (The colleague [m.] / The colleague [f.] / Nouns) [Day 30 core]
- **Wir sehen uns morgen!** (See you tomorrow! / Interaction Formula) [Day 30 core]

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The vocalic **"er"** in *der* [de:ɐ̯] and the silent hushing vowel transition in *Kollegin* [Day 30 core, Unit 13].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /e:ɐ̯/ (Relaxed, breathy schwa-vowel, avoiding any hard English "err" sound).
  2. *Word:* *der* [de:ɐ̯] $\rightarrow$ *die Kollegin* [di: kɔˈle\:gɪn] [Day 30 core, Unit 13].
  3. *Phrase:* *mit der Kollegin* [mɪt de:ɐ̯ kɔˈle\:gɪn] [Day 30 core].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich fahre mit der Kollegin.* [Day 30 core].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Wir sehen uns morgen! Ich fahre mit der Kollegin.* (Maintain soft, unstressed ending on *Kollegin* [Day 30 core]).

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **The Dative Preposition *****mit*****:**
  - **MUST MASTER:** The preposition **mit** (with/by) always governs the **Dative Case** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Masculine/Neuter (*der/das*) change to **dem** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - *mit dem Kollegen* (with the male colleague) [Day 30 core]
    - *mit dem Bus* (by bus) [Unit 6]
  - Feminine (*die*) changes to **der** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - *mit der Kollegin* (with the female colleague) [Day 30 core]
    - *mit der U-Bahn* (by subway) [Unit 6]
  - **RECEPTIVE:** The plural pronouns *wir* (we) and *ihr* (you all) are understood receptively; the active focus remains on singular *ich, du, er, sie* and formal *Sie* [Day 30 core, Unit 11].

#### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Article Identification:** Listen to spoken sentences. Write down if the speaker says *mit dem* or *mit der*[a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Top-Down Group Tracking:** Listen to a group chat voice note. Check whether the group (*wir*) is currently travelling by train or bus [Day 30 core, Unit 6, 17].

#### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Substitution Drills:** Point to colleague photo cards and prompt transit methods:
  - *"Sarah / Bus"* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich fahre mit der Kollegin mit dem Bus.* [Day 30 core, Unit 6].
  - *"Alex / Zug"* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich fahre mit dem Kollegen mit dem Zug.* [Day 30 core, Unit 6].
- **The Formal Farewell:** Recite with your classmate: *Wir sehen uns morgen!* [Day 30 core].

#### 8. Reading Practice

- Read an office commuter update:
  - *Guten Tag. Heute fahre ich mit der Kollegin Sarah mit der U-Bahn. Die U-Bahn ist heute zu spät. Wir sehen uns morgen!* [Day 13, 30 core, Unit 6, 23]
- Highlight the dative prepositional phrases (*mit der...*, *mit dem...*).

#### 9. Writing Practice

- **Guided Paragraph Composition:** Write a short status email to your training supervisor:
  - `Guten Tag. Ich fahre heute mit dem Kollegen Alex mit dem Bus.` [Day 30 core, Unit 6]
  - `Der Bus ist nicht zu spät.` [Day 13 core, Unit 19]
  - `Wir sehen uns morgen!` [Day 30 core]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Transit and Companion Substitution.
- **Execution:** Adapt the dative articles following *mit* depending on the noun's grammatical gender:
  - *mit / der Kollege* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich fahre ****mit dem Kollegen****.* [Day 30 core]
  - *mit / die Kollegin* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich fahre ****mit der Kollegin****.* [Day 30 core]
  - *mit / das Auto* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich fahre ****mit dem Auto****.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 6]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–4

- Retrieve the transit nouns (*Bus, Zug, U-Bahn*) from Week 2 [Unit 6] and the present-tense verb *fahren* [Unit 12] to populate the dative preposition frames.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Commute Coordination (TBLT):** The learner roleplays coordinating their commute with their vocational classmate [Day 30 core, Unit 12]. They must greet them, propose travelling together tomorrow using the correct dative articles, and close with the core parting formula:
  - *Learner:* "Hallo Alex. Morgen fahre ich mit dem Zug. Fährst du auch mit dem Zug? — Ja. — Super, wir fahren zusammen. Wir sehen uns morgen!" [Days 2, 30 core, Unit 6, 12, 23]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the partner's answer is unclear, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Noch einmal, bitte."** [Days 1, 3 core] to prompt a slow, clear repetition.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Classmate):* "Hallo. Fährst du heute mit der U-Bahn?" [Unit 6]
- *Learner:* "Nein, heute fahre ich mit der Kollegin mit dem Bus." [Day 30 core, Unit 6, 23]
- *Native (Classmate):* "Ah, alles klar. Wir sehen uns morgen!" [Day 30 core]
- *Learner:* "Ja, wir sehen uns morgen! Tschüss!" [Days 2, 30 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Shadowing Practice:** Spend 15 minutes shadowing a native conversation about work commutes. Focus entirely on matching the unrolled vocalic "er" in *der* [de:ɐ̯] and the rhythm of dative contractions [23, Unit 13].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can correctly deploy *mit dem* and *mit der* based on noun genders to state transit and accompaniment [Day 30 core, Unit 6].
  - Can use "Wir sehen uns morgen!" naturally to close a social conversation [Day 30 core].

---

### DAY 30 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, adding *der Kollege/die Kollegin*, *Wir sehen uns morgen!*) [Day 30 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Dative preposition *mit* syntax, masculine/feminine article shifts, receptive plural pronoun exposure) [Day 30 core, Unit 11].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing the vocalic "er" /e:ɐ̯/; 60 mins: picture-prompted dative preposition drills; 45 mins: transcribing commuter plans).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing your daily travel update aloud in front of a mirror: *Ich fahre mit dem Bus mit dem Kollegen.*) [3, Day 30 core].

## DAY 31: Destination Movement (*zu / zum / zur*) vs. Stationary Location (*im*) at the Station

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to distinguish between stationary location (**Wo?**) and directed movement (**Wohin?**), and actively construct movement targets using the dative-governing preposition **zu** and its contractions **zum** (*zu dem*) and **zur** (*zu der*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The movement preposition **zu** (to) and its high-frequency contractions **zum** (*zu dem*) and **zur** (*zu der*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit rule: stationary location is asked with **Wo?** and uses *in* (*im Büro*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Directed movement to a local landmark is asked with **Wohin?** (Where to?) and uses *zu* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Since *zu* governs the dative, masculine/neuter become **zum** and feminine becomes **zur** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short spoken updates and marks whether the speaker is *stationary* inside a landmark or *moving* toward it [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RETRIEVE:** Spaced recall of landmarks (*der Bahnhof*, *das Büro*) [Day 8 core, Unit 11] and the verb *gehen* [Unit 12].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Gap-fill exercises matching landmarks with *zum* or *zur* based on gender.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** *Ich gehe* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich gehe zum Bahnhof* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Heute gehe ich zum Bahnhof.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 8 core, Unit 23].
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work asking and answering travel destinations: *Wohin gehst du? — Ich gehe zur Arbeit. Und du? — Ich gehe zum Bahnhof.* [Day 8, 15 core, Unit 12].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Confirming your travel destination to a transit counter agent in German.
- **REVISIT:** Practice pronouncing the alveolar affricate "Z" (/ts/) in *zu*, *zum*, *zur* [Unit 4].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- Consolidating and randomizing previous items: **der Bahnhof** [Day 8 core], **Ich wohne in...** [Day 8 core], **Es tut mir leid** [Day 25 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The German alveolar affricate **"Z" (/ts/)** and long tense vowel **"u" (/u:/)** in *zu*, *zum*, *zur* [Unit 4].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ts/ (Voiceless "ts" sound, like at the end of "cats" but placed at the start of the word, followed by rounded lips for the tense /u:/).
  2. *Word:* *zu* [tsu:] $\rightarrow$ *zum* [tsu\:m] $\rightarrow$ *zur* [tsu:ɐ̯] [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  3. *Phrase:* *zum Bahnhof* [tsu\:m ˈba\:nho\:f] $\rightarrow$ *zur Arbeit* [tsu:ɐ̯ ˈaʁbaɪt] [Day 8, 15 core].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich gehe zum Bahnhof.* [Day 8 core].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Guten Tag. Ich wohne in Berlin. Heute gehe ich zum Bahnhof.* (Ensure the initial /ts/ is sharp and voiceless [Day 8 core, Unit 4]).

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **Location (Wo?) vs. Movement (Wohin?):**
  - **MUST MASTER:** Stationary location (**Wo?**) uses **im** (*im Büro*, *im Bahnhof*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Directed movement toward a public landmark (**Wohin?**) uses the dative-governing preposition **zu** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Masculine/Neuter (*der/das*) change to **dem**, contracting to **zum** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - *zu* + *dem* \\(\rightarrow\\) **zum** (*zum Bahnhof*, *zum Büro*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Feminine (*die*) changes to **der**, contracting to **zur** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - *zu* + *der* \\(\rightarrow\\) **zur** (*zur Arbeit*, *zur Post*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - **DEFER:** Movement *into* a building using accusative prepositions (*in das / ins*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Contrast Parsing:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *im Bahnhof* vs. *zum Bahnhof*. Identify the semantic boundaries between stationary location and directed movement based on the nasal contractions: /ɪm ˈba\:nho\:f/ vs. /tsu\:m ˈba\:nho\:f/ [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Top-Down Destination Matching:** Listen to three commuters. Draw lines connecting each commuter (Alex, Sarah, David) to their target landmarks on a municipal map layout [Unit 12].

#### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Suffix Transformations (Location to Movement):**
  - *Ich bin im Büro.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich gehe zum Büro.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 11, 12].
  - *Ich bin am Bahnhof.* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich gehe zum Bahnhof.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 8 core].
- **Query-Response Pairings:** Practice: *Wohin gehst du? — Ich gehe zur Arbeit.* [Day 15 core].

#### 8. Reading Practice

- Scan a simple transit station leaflet:
  - *Fahrplan heute: Zug nach Hamburg. Passagiere gehen zum Gleis 2. Info-Termin zur Arbeit ist im Büro 101.*[Day 15, 22 core, Unit 21]
- Highlight the movement contractions (*zum*, *zur*) and underline the stationary location contraction (*im*).

#### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Destination Composition:** Complete the commuter status report:
  1. `Ich bin jetzt im Bahnhof.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 8 core]
  2. `Ich gehe jetzt zum Drucker.` [Day 26 core, Unit 12]
  3. `Ich gehe morgen zur Arbeit.` [Day 15 core, Unit 23]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Wo? vs. Wohin? Sentence Construction.
- **Execution:** Build parallel location and movement sentences, choosing correctly between *im* and *zum/zur* based on semantic direction:
  - *Stationary (Wo?):* *der Bahnhof* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin ****im Bahnhof****.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *Movement (Wohin?):* *der Bahnhof* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich gehe ****zum Bahnhof****.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *Movement (Wohin?):* *die Arbeit* \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich gehe ****zur Arbeit****.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–4

- Retrieve the singular present-tense conjugation of *gehen* (*ich gehe*, *er geht*) [Unit 12] and transit nouns to frame today's destination exercises.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Ticket Office Destination Check (TBLT):** The learner roleplays asking for directions to a specific platform at the train station counter [Day 8 core]. They greet the agent formally, state their transit plan, and ask how to reach their platform:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich fahre mit dem Zug. Ich muss zum Gleis vier gehen. Ist das Gleis geöffnet? Danke." [Days 1, 11 core, Unit 6, 21]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the agent speaks too rapidly, the learner immediately deploys: **"Wie bitte? Noch einmal, bitte."** [Days 1, 3 core] to manage the speech velocity.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Agent):* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie den Zug nach Berlin?" [Day 8 core, Unit 16]
- *Learner:* "Ja. Ich gehe zum Zug. Ist der Bahnhof geöffnet?" [Day 8, 11 core]
- *Native (Agent):* "Ja, der Bahnhof ist geöffnet. Der Zug ist auf Gleis vier." [Day 8, 11 core]
- *Learner:* "Vielen Dank! Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Virtual Commute:** Watch a short 5-minute video of a German train station commute. Focus on identifying how many times commuters refer to going *zum Bahnhof* or *zur Arbeit* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can distinguish between stationary *im* (Wo?) and directional *zum/zur* (Wohin?) in active speech [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Can pronounce the German affricate /ts/ in *zu*, *zum*, and *zur* cleanly [Unit 4].

---

### DAY 31 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Consolidating *der Bahnhof*, *Ich wohne in...*, *Es tut mir leid*) [Day 8, 25 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Location vs. Movement syntax, *zu* contraction rules, Wohin? queries) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing the alveolar affricate "Z" /ts/; 60 mins: sorting Wo? (im) vs. Wohin? (zum/zur) sentence strips; 45 mins: transcribing native announcements).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing saying your destinations aloud while leaving your room: *Ich gehe zur Küche. Ich gehe zum Tisch.*) [3, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

## DAY 32: Pedestrian Directions and Imperative Navigation (*geradeaus, rechts, links*)

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to ask for and give simple pedestrian directions using the directional adverbs *geradeaus*, *rechts*, and *links*, and formulate formal navigation imperatives using V2 syntax [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** Directional adverbs **geradeaus** (straight ahead), **rechts** (right), **links** (left) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], and the formal imperative structure *Gehen Sie...* / *Biegen Sie... ab* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit syntax guide explaining that formal pedestrian instructions utilize the V2 imperative frame, placing the verb stem in Position 1 and the formal pronoun *Sie* in Position 2 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Separable direction verbs split, sending the prefix (*ab*) to the very end: *Biegen Sie links ab.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to native direction instructions over background noise and traces the correct route on a simple grid map [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RETRIEVE:** Spaced recall of landmarks (*Bahnhof, Supermarkt*) and dative contractions [Day 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Restructuring scrambled imperative words to form correct navigation commands [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** *Gehen Sie* $\rightarrow$ *Gehen Sie geradeaus* $\rightarrow$ *Gehen Sie geradeaus zum Bahnhof.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 8 core].
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work guiding a blindfolded or mapping partner to landmarks on a street layout sheet [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching a pedestrian in a German city and asking for directions politely [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **REVISIT:** Practice pronouncing the complex final consonant clusters in *rechts* and *links* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- Consolidating previous items: **Kein Problem!** [Day 13 core], **hier/dort** [Day 6 core], **geöffnet/geschlossen** [Day 11 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The final consonant clusters in **"rechts"** (/çts/) and **"links"** (/ŋks/) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /çts/ $\rightarrow$ /ŋks/ (Smile for the palatal "ich-laut", followed by a crisp "ts" release; then nasal "ng" followed by "ks").
  2. *Word:* *rechts* [ʁɛçts] $\rightarrow$ *links* [lɪŋks] [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  3. *Phrase:* *nach rechts* [nax ʁɛçts] $\rightarrow$ *nach links* [nax lɪŋks] (Treating "nach" as a receptive directional chunk).
  4. *Sentence:* *Biegen Sie rechts ab.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Gehen Sie geradeaus. Biegen Sie rechts ab. Der Bahnhof ist dort.* (Ensure clear, crisp hushing sibilant releases [Day 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]).

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **Formal Imperative Navigation Syntax:**
  - **MUST MASTER:** Formal directions use the imperative structure, where the verb stem moves to **Position 1**and the formal pronoun *Sie* occupies **Position 2** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - *Gehen Sie geradeaus.* (Go straight ahead.) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
    - *Biegen Sie rechts ab.* (Turn right - separable split where *ab* lands at the absolute end) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - **IMPORTANT:** Capitalize nouns (*Bahnhof*, *Supermarkt*) in written tasks [Unit 11].
  - **DEFER:** Informal imperatives (*geh*, *geht*) and complex spatial prepositions (*unter*, *über*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Directional Extraction:** Listen to five short native direction clips. Write down the sequence of directional adverbs heard (e.g., *geradeaus — links — rechts*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Top-Down Route Mapping:** Listen to a pedestrian guiding a lost tourist. Mark the final landmark reached on your visual city grid worksheet [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Imperative Conversions (S-V-O to Imperative):**
  - *Sie gehen geradeaus.* $\rightarrow$ *Gehen Sie geradeaus!* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - *Sie biegen links ab.* $\rightarrow$ *Biegen Sie links ab!* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Lost in Berlin:** Rehearse asking: *Entschuldigung, wo ist der Bahnhof?* [Day 8, 25 core].

#### 8. Reading Practice

- Scan a printed municipal map guide description:
  - *Weg zum Supermarkt: Gehen Sie geradeaus. Biegen Sie links ab. Der Supermarkt ist beim Bahnhof geöffnet.*[Day 8, 11 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- Circle the imperative verbs in Position 1 and underline the directional adverbs.

#### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Route Composition:** Write a simple 4-step direction log guiding a coworker from the office to the train station:
  1. `Gehen Sie geradeaus.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  2. `Biegen Sie rechts ab.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  3. `Gehen Sie zum Bahnhof.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 8 core]
  4. `Der Bahnhof ist geöffnet.` [Day 8, 11 core]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Imperative Conversions & Navigation.
- **Execution:** Convert standard statements into polite formal direction commands, splitting separable verbs correctly:
  - *Sie gehen zum Supermarkt.* $\rightarrow$ *Gehen Sie zum Supermarkt!* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *Sie biegen rechts ab.* $\rightarrow$ *Biegen Sie rechts ab!* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *Sie kommen an.* $\rightarrow$ *Kommen Sie heute an!* [Day 12, 14 core, Unit 22]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–4

- Retrieve the polite apologies (*Entschuldigung*) [Day 4 core] and spatial indicators (*hier/dort*) [Day 6 core] to frame your pedestrian requests naturally.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **Lost in Berlin (TBLT):** The learner roleplays being lost in a German city [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. They approach a pedestrian, apologize, ask for directions to the supermarket or train station, decode the spoken response, and confirm:
  - *Learner:* "Entschuldigung! Wo ist der Supermarkt? — Gehen Sie geradeaus und biegen Sie links ab. — Ah, geradeaus und links abbiegen. Vielen Dank! Tschüss!" [Days 2, 4, 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the pedestrian speaks too quickly, the learner immediately deploys: **"Langsam, bitte! Ich verstehe nicht."** [Day 3, 25 core] to manage the input velocity calmly.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Pedestrian):* "Guten Tag. Suchen Sie den Bahnhof?" [Day 8 core, Unit 16]
- *Learner:* "Ja. Ist der Bahnhof weit?" [Day 8 core]
- *Native (Pedestrian):* "Nein. Gehen Sie geradeaus. Biegen Sie rechts ab. Da ist der Bahnhof." [Day 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Learner:* "Geradeaus und rechts abbiegen. Super, danke! Tschüss!" [Days 2, 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Walk-and-Talk Practice:** While walking or commuting, spend 5 minutes narrating your movements aloud in German: *"Ich gehe geradeaus. Jetzt biege ich rechts ab."* to automate the motor-syntactic loop.

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can ask for directions politely and decode simple directional adverbs (*geradeaus, rechts, links*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Can formulate formal imperatives using V2 word order and split separable verbs correctly [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

---

### DAY 32 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing previous items, consolidating *Kein Problem!*, *hier/dort*) [Day 6, 13 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Imperative syntax, separable verb splits, directional adverb phonetics) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing the terminal consonant clusters /çts/ and /ŋks/; 60 mins: mapping-and-guiding routing roleplays; 45 mins: writing simple direction guides).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Guiding yourself around your house using German navigation commands: *Gehen Sie zum Tisch! Biegen Sie links ab!*) [3, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

## DAY 33: Dative Personal Pronouns (*mir, Ihnen*) and the Verb *helfen*

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to identify and use dative personal pronouns **mir** (me) and **Ihnen** (you, formal) paired with the dative-governing verb **helfen** (to help) in polite request and response frames [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** Dative singular personal pronouns **mir** and **Ihnen** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], and the transitive dative verb **helfen** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit explanation: the verb *helfen* is a dative-governing verb that requires its recipient object to stand in the **Dative Case** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Therefore, the personal pronouns change (*ich* $\rightarrow$ **mir**, *Sie* $\rightarrow$ **Ihnen**) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]: *Können Sie mir helfen?* / *Ich helfe Ihnen.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 9 core].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to short help-desk clips and identifies who is receiving assistance based on the pronouns *mir* or *Ihnen* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RETRIEVE:** Spaced recall of the request frame *Können Sie mir helfen?* from Week 2 [Day 9 core].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Grammatical gap-fills inserting *mir* or *Ihnen* based on conversational cues.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** *Helfen* $\rightarrow$ *Ich helfe Ihnen* $\rightarrow$ *Heute helfe ich Ihnen im Büro.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 11, 23].
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work at an office assistance desk: *Können Sie mir helfen? — Ja, ich helfe Ihnen gerne.* [Day 9 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching an information counter and requesting help formally.
- **REVISIT:** Practice the initial glottal friction /h/ in *helfen* and *hier* [Unit 17].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- Consolidating previous items: **Können Sie mir helfen?** [Day 9 core], **Schreiben Sie das bitte auf?** [Day 9 core], **Wie bitte?** [Day 3 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The glottal friction **"h" (/h/)** in *helfen* and the tense **long vowel "I" (/i:/)** in *Ihnen* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 17].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /h/ $\rightarrow$ /i\:nən/ (Warm sigh from the chest, followed by the tense, high-forward tongue for "ee").
  2. *Word:* *helfen* [ˈhɛlfən] $\rightarrow$ *Ihnen* [ˈi\:nən] [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 17].
  3. *Phrase:* *helfe Ihnen* [ˈhɛlfə ˈi\:nən] $\rightarrow$ *helfen Sie mir* [ˈhɛlfən zi: mi:ɐ̯] [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  4. *Sentence:* *Können Sie mir helfen?* [Day 9 core].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Es tut mir leid, können Sie mir helfen? — Ja, ich helfe Ihnen.* (Maintain a warm, vocalic transition [Day 25 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]).

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **The Dative-Governing Verb Rule (*****helfen*****):**
  - **MUST MASTER:** The verb **helfen** systematically governs the **Dative Case** for the person being helped [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Personal pronouns decline as follows [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - *ich* $\rightarrow$ **mir** (*Können Sie mir helfen?* — "Can you help me?") [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 9 core].
    - *Sie* $\rightarrow$ **Ihnen** (*Ich helfe Ihnen.* — "I help you") [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - **DEFER:** Dative plural pronouns (*uns*, *euch*) and informal *dir* to protect early morphosyntactic retrieval [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Pronoun Parsing:** Listen to native speakers pronouncing *Ich helfe Ihnen* vs. *Helfen Sie mir*. Identify the exact pronoun endings and vowel lengths in connected speech [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Top-Down Assistance Log:** Listen to three help-desk dialogs. Write down who needs help and what office tools are missing (*keinen Schlüssel*, *keinen Drucker*) [Unit 18, 20].

#### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Suffix Drills (Pronoun Substitution):**
  - *"help me"* $\rightarrow$ *Können Sie mir helfen?* [Day 9 core].
  - *"help you (formal)"* $\rightarrow$ *Ich helfe Ihnen.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Information Desk Roleplay:** Practice asking: *Können Sie mir am Bahnhof helfen?* [Day 8, 9 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 8. Reading Practice

- Scan a printed customer assistance memo:
  - *Guten Tag. Ich bin im Büro, aber ich habe keinen Schlüssel. Können Sie mir helfen? — Ja, ich komme heute und helfe Ihnen.* [Day 9, 29 core, Unit 18]
- Highlight the dative pronouns (*mir*, *Ihnen*) and underline the verb *helfen*.

#### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Pronoun Composition:** Complete the office assistance email:
  - `Guten Tag Herr Schmidt. Mein Computer funktioniert nicht.` [Day 26 core, Unit 11]
  - `Können Sie mir heute helfen?` [Day 9 core, Unit 23]
  - `Ich helfe Ihnen morgen im Büro.` [Day 29 core, Unit 23]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Dative Pronoun Substitution.
- **Execution:** Modify pronouns correctly depending on the direction of assistance (me vs. you-formal):
  - *you help me* $\rightarrow$ *Können Sie ****mir**** helfen?* [Day 9 core]
  - *I help you (formal)* $\rightarrow$ *Ich helfe ****Ihnen****.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *he helps you (receptive)* $\rightarrow$ *Er hilft ****Ihnen****.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–4

- Retrieve the negative determiner *kein / keine / keinen* from Week 4 [Unit 18] to report missing supplies during assistance requests: *Ich habe keinen Schlüssel. Können Sie mir helfen?* [Day 9 core, Unit 18].

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Information Desk Query (TBLT):** The learner roleplays seeking help with workstation setup [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. They approach the office assistant formally, report a technical failure, request help, and accept assistance:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Können Sie mir helfen? Der Drucker funktioniert heute nicht. — Ja, kein Problem. Ich helfe Ihnen. — Vielen Dank!" [Days 1, 9, 13, 26 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the clerk speaks too rapidly, the learner immediately deploys: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 3, 25 core] to manage the interaction safely in German.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Assistant):* "Guten Tag. Kann ich Ihnen helfen?" [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Learner:* "Ja. Ich habe keinen Schlüssel für das Büro. Können Sie mir helfen?" [Day 9 core, Unit 11, 18]
- *Native (Assistant):* "Ah, der Schlüssel ist hier. Ich helfe Ihnen." [Unit 11, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Learner:* "Super, vielen Dank! Tschüss!" [Days 2, 9 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Spaced Retrieval Booster:** Review your personal vocabulary journal. Write down 5 different polite request sentences starting with *Können Sie mir...* to consolidate the dative frame.

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can use *mir* and *Ihnen* correctly with the verb *helfen* in spoken dialogs [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Can execute the complete "help-response" loop politely using formal registers [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

---

### DAY 33 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing *Können Sie mir helfen?*, *Schreiben Sie das bitte auf?*) [Day 9 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Dative personal pronouns, dative-governing verbs, formal request loops) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (60 mins: gap-fill pronoun sorting exercises; 45 mins: mock help-desk roleplays; 45 mins: practicing initial glottal friction /h/ in *helfen*).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing speaking assistance requests aloud during household chores: *Können Sie mir helfen? Ich habe keine Zeit.*) [3, Day 22 core].
