## DAY 34: Duration with *seit* and Scheduling Appointments at the Clinic

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to express time duration using the dative preposition **seit** (since/for), and negotiate and schedule appointments formally at a clinic using dative time structures (*am*, *um*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The time preposition **seit** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], dative singular indefinite articles (*einem, einer*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], and scheduling units (*die Besprechung, der Termin*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the explicit rule: *seit* always governs the dative [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. For duration, masculine/neuter singular nouns take **einem** (*seit einem Tag*), and feminine singular take **einer** (*seit einer Woche*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Time coordinates use **am** (*an dem* + day) and **um** (+ hour) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner listens to telephone schedules and logs the correct times and durations on a clinic grid [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RETRIEVE:** Spaced recall of *der Arzt* [Day 21 core], *krank* [Day 21 core], and *den Termin verschieben* [Day 22 core].
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Matching days and hours with correct prepositions (*Dienstag* $\rightarrow$ *am Dienstag*, *zehn Uhr* $\rightarrow$ *um zehn Uhr*).
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** *Ich bin krank* $\rightarrow$ *Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank* $\rightarrow$ *Heute bin ich seit zwei Tagen krank.* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 23].
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner scheduling. Negotiating medical appointments: *Passt das am Dienstag um zehn Uhr? — Ja, das passt gut.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 5].
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Calling a medical clinic, reporting illness duration, and booking a slot in German.
- **REVISIT:** Focused pronunciation of the diphthong "ei" (/aɪ/) in *seit* [zaɪt] [Unit 14].

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- Consolidating previous items: **krank** [Day 21 core], **der Arzt** [Day 21 core], **den Termin verschieben** [Day 22 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** The diphthong **"ei" (/aɪ/)** in *seit* and the final consonant cluster **"rzt"** (/ʁtst/) in *Arzt* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /zaɪt/ $\rightarrow$ /a:ɐ̯tst/ (Sharp initial sibilant voiced "z" followed by diphthong /aɪ/; then vocalic "r" followed by a crisp "tst" cluster).
  2. *Word:* *seit* [zaɪt] $\rightarrow$ *Arzt* [a:ɐ̯tst] [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  3. *Phrase:* *seit einem Tag* [zaɪt ˈaɪnəm ta\:k] $\rightarrow$ *beim Arzt* [baɪm a:ɐ̯tst] [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  4. *Sentence:* *Ich bin beim Arzt.* [Day 21 core].
  5. *Connected Speech:* *Guten Tag. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank. Ich muss zum Arzt.* (Ensure the final cluster /tst/ is completely audible [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]).

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **Duration with *****seit***** & Scheduling Prepositions:**
  - **MUST MASTER:** The preposition **seit** (since/for) always governs the **Dative Case** to indicate ongoing duration [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Singular indefinite articles change as follows [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
    - Masculine/Neuter (*ein \\(\rightarrow\\) einem*): *seit ****einem**** Tag* (for a day) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
    - Feminine (*eine \\(\rightarrow\\) einer*): *seit ****einer**** Woche* (for a week) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - **IMPORTANT:** Time prepositions: **am** (*an dem*) for days of the week (*am Dienstag*) and **um** for specific hours (*um zehn Uhr*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - **DEFER:** Plural dative *seit Tagen* and dative verb *gefallen* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 6. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Preposition Parsing:** Listen to medical bookings. Circle whether the receptionist says *am* (+ day) or *um* (+ hour) for the slots mentioned [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Top-Down Gist Extraction:** Listen to a voicemail from a doctor's clinic. Extract the patient's name, illness duration (*seit...*), and rescheduled appointment time [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Speaking Practice

- **Oral Duration Drills:** Convert time prompts into dative duration blocks:
  - *"one day"* $\rightarrow$ *Ich bin seit einem Tag krank.* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - *"one week"* $\rightarrow$ *Ich bin seit einer Woche krank.* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Interactive Scheduling:** Alternate asking: *Wann geht es? — Am Dienstag um zehn Uhr.* [Day 12 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 8. Reading Practice

- Scan a simple doctor's clinic booking portal receipt:
  - *Termin: John. Patient ist seit drei Tagen krank. Termin beim Arzt ist am Dienstag um elf Uhr.* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- Underline the *seit* duration phrase and circle the time prepositions (*am*, *um*).

#### 9. Writing Practice

- **Guided Composition:** Write a short email confirming a rescheduled clinic booking:
  - `Guten Tag. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank.` [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - `Ich möchte den Termin verschieben.` [Day 22 core]
  - `Passt das am Dienstag um zehn Uhr? Danke!` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 5]

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Time preposition & Duration Insertion.
- **Execution:** Insert correct dative time modifiers and prepositions into schedule requests based on noun gender and duration rules:
  - *seit / ein Tag* $\rightarrow$ *Ich bin seit ****einem**** Tag krank.* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *am / Dienstag* $\rightarrow$ *Der Termin ist ****am**** Dienstag.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - *um / zehn Uhr* $\rightarrow$ *Die Besprechung ist ****um**** zehn Uhr.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–4

- Retrieve the modal verb *möchten* from Week 4 [Unit 21] to formulate polite scheduling wishes: *Ich möchte am Dienstag kommen.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 21].

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Clinic Booking (TBLT):** The learner roleplays calling a doctor's office to reschedule an appointment due to illness [Day 21 core, Unit 11]. They must greet the receptionist, state they are sick and the duration using *seit*, reschedule using modal verbs, and confirm:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben, bitte. — [Receptionist proposes slot] — Dienstag um zehn Uhr? Ja, das passt gut. Danke!" [Days 1, 21, 22 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 5]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the receptionist proposes an unavailable slot, the learner immediately deploys: **"Es tut mir leid, das passt nicht. Wann geht es?"** [Days 12, 25 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to request another option.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Receptionist):* "Dienstag um elf Uhr ist frei. Mit welchem Arzt haben Sie den Termin?" [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Learner:* "Mit Herrn Dr. Wagner, bitte. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank." [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Native (Receptionist):* "Alles klar. Der Termin steht. Bis Dienstag!" [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Learner:* "Vielen Dank. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Graded Reader Review:** Spend 15 minutes scanning a highly simplified A1 German text about a doctor's visit. Highlight all dative prepositions (*mit, zu, bei, seit*) and time markers [23, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- **Can-Do Checklist:**
  - Can state time duration using *seit* with correct singular dative indefinite articles [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - Can successfully negotiate and book a clinic appointment using correct prepositions (*am*, *um*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

---

### DAY 34 TIME BUDGET ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 15 minutes (Reviewing *krank*, *der Arzt*, *den Termin verschieben*) [Day 21, 22 core].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 45 minutes (Dative duration rules, *seit* article inflections, time coordinate syntax) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (45 mins: practicing diphthong /aɪ/ in *seit* and cluster /ʁtst/ in *Arzt*; 60 mins: roleplaying doctor clinic phone calls; 45 mins: writing appointment confirmations).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Rehearsing your health and schedule updates aloud: *Ich bin seit einem Tag krank. Ich gehe zum Arzt.*) [3, Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

## DAY 35: Week 5 Comprehensive Synthesis and Real-Life Street Navigation

### 1. Day Objective

The learner will be able to integrate all Week 5 dative constructions, prepositions (*mit, zu, bei, seit*), contractions (*im, am, zum, zur*), and directional imperatives to successfully execute a multi-stage street navigation and appointment scheduling cycle [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 2. Main Curriculum

- **INTRODUCE:** The comprehensive synthesis of all Week 5 locative and directional dative mappings [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 40].
- **UNDERSTAND:** The learner reads the spatial flowchart showing how a traveler (1) greets a pedestrian and asks for directions, (2) navigates using directional imperatives, and (3) arrives at a doctor's clinic to confirm their booking [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **RECOGNIZE:** The learner scans a complete written navigation transcript and highlights any capitalization mistakes, register mismatches, or dative contraction errors.
- **RETRIEVE:** Systematic spaced recall of all Core and main curriculum vocabulary from Weeks 1–5.
- **CONTROLLED USE:** Reordering scrambled dialogue blocks containing street navigation and scheduling updates.
- **SENTENCE BUILDING:** See Section 10.
- **CONTEXTUAL USE:** Partner work executing the complete Week 5 street navigation and scheduling simulation.
- **REAL-LIFE USE:** Approaching the formal checkout examiner to complete the Week 5 Milestone Tasks.
- **REVISIT:** Final pronunciation audit of consonant clusters [ʁɛçts], Umlauts, and initial affricates [tsu:] before the test.

#### 3. Daily German Core Items

- Consolidating and randomizing all **Days 1–30 Daily German Core** formulas to achieve conversational automaticity [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Consolidating **terminal devoicing**, **Umlauts**, and **connected speech liaison** across all Week 5 targets [Unit 40].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Sound:* /ts/ $\rightarrow$ /çts/ $\rightarrow$ /ŋks/ $\rightarrow$ /aɪ/ (Reviewing all tricky consonant clusters, affricates, and diphthongs).
  2. *Word:* *Zug* [tsu\:k] $\rightarrow$ *Geld* [gɛlt] $\rightarrow$ *rechts* [ʁɛçts] (Crisp devoiced terminal releases).
  3. *Phrase:* *mit dem Bus* $\rightarrow$ *zum Bahnhof* $\rightarrow$ *am Dienstag* (Smooth connected boundaries).
  4. *Sentence:* *Gehen Sie geradeaus zum Bahnhof.* [Day 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  5. *Connected Speech:* Deliver the entire travel, navigation, and appointment presentation as a fluid connected breathing unit with natural intonation.

#### 5. Minimum Explanation

- **The V2 Syntactic law with Dative Elements:**
  - **MUST MASTER:** Regardless of how many dative prepositions (*mit*, *zu*, *bei*, *seit*) or time adverbs (*heute*, *morgen*, *jetzt*) are added, the conjugated verb remains locked in **Position 2** [Unit 1]. Temporal adverbs starting a sentence trigger Subject-Verb inversion, pushing the subject to Position 3 [Unit 23]: $$\begin{array}{c|c|c|c|l} \text{Position 1} & \text{Position 2 (Verb)} & \text{Position 3 (Subject)} & \text{Dative Modifier} & \text{Target Landmark} \ \hline \text{Heute} & \text{\textbf{gehe}} & \text{ich} & \text{mit dem Kollegen} & \text{\textbf{zum Bahnhof}.} \ \text{Jetzt} & \text{\textbf{bin}} & \text{ich} & \text{seit einem Tag} & \text{\textbf{krank}.} \ \text{Morgen} & \text{\textbf{fahre}} & \text{ich} & \text{mit dem Bus} & \text{\textbf{zur Arbeit}.} \end{array}$$

#### 6. Listening Practice

- **C-Test Transcription:** Listen to a multi-stage native dialogue recorded at a clinic counter. Fill in the missing letters and dative endings on your worksheet, focusing on transcribing dative articles, contractions, and adverbs.
- **Register Validation:** Audit three audio clips and confirm if the speakers maintain consistent formal register (*Sie/sind/haben/gehen*).

#### 7. Speaking Practice

- **The Roleplay Rehearsal:** Practice reciting the complete, multi-stage navigation and booking dialogue with natural sentence stress.
- **Rapid Retrieval Round:** Answer immediate verbal prompts from the instructor:
  - *"to the station (masculine)"* $\rightarrow$ *zum Bahnhof* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - *"to work (feminine)"* $\rightarrow$ *zur Arbeit* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 8. Reading Practice

- The student reads an un-simplified graded extensive text:
  - *Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich wohne in Berlin. Heute gehe ich zur Arbeit. Ich fahre mit der U-Bahn. Aber ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank. Jetzt muss ich zum Arzt gehen. Der Arzt ist beim Bahnhof. Gehen Sie geradeaus, dann links. Bis morgen!* [Day 8, 14, 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- The student verifies that all verbs occupy Position 2 [Unit 1].

#### 9. Writing Practice

- **Controlled Essay Composition:** Write your own daily schedule and transit log (5 sentences) in your notebook, ensuring correct capitalization, spelling of suffixes, and perfect verb-second syntax.

#### 10. Daily Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** The Mini-Dialogue Creation (Week 5 Synthesis) [Unit 40].
- **Execution:** Construct a complete, coherent conversation between a lost traveler and a local resident, combining greetings, destination queries, and directions:
  - *Traveler:* "Guten Tag. Ich suche den Bahnhof."
  - *Resident:* "Guten Tag. Gehen Sie geradeaus und biegen Sie rechts ab. Der Bahnhof ist dort."
  - *Traveler:* "Geradeaus und rechts abbiegen. Vielen Dank. Tschüss!"

#### 11. Retrieval from Weeks 1–4

- Systematically retrieve greetings, spelling, numbers, transit methods, and repair formulas from previous weeks to enrich the final interaction.

#### 12. Practical Germany Task

- **The Lost and Scheduled (Milestone Tasks):** The learner roleplays navigating two distinct, realistic daily tasks [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:
  - **Task A (Lost in Berlin):** The learner approaches a pedestrian, asks for directions to the doctor's office, decodes the directional response, and navigates:
    - *Learner:* "Entschuldigung! Wo ist der Arzt? — Gehen Sie geradeaus und biegen Sie links ab. — Geradeaus und links abbiegen. Danke!" [Days 4, 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - **Task B (The Clinic Booking):** The learner arrives at the clinic, reports their illness duration, reschedules their appointment formally, and confirms:
    - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben, bitte. Passt das am Dienstag um zehn Uhr? — Ja. — Vielen Dank. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Days 1, 5, 21, 22 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the resident or receptionist speaks too rapidly, the learner immediately deploys: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 3, 25 core] to manage the input velocity safely in German.

#### 14. Realistic Native Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Receptionist):* "Guten Tag. Haben Sie einen Termin am Dienstag?" [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Learner:* "Ja. Passt das um zehn Uhr?" [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Native (Receptionist):* "Ja, Dienstag um zehn Uhr passt gut." [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 5]
- *Learner:* "Super, vielen Dank. Bis Dienstag!" [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 15. Optional Interest-Driven German Exposure

- **The Extensive Reading Sprint:** Spend 15 minutes reading a short graded story about a traveler's commute and doctor's visit in Munich. Focus on enjoying the narrative flow, noting how dative prepositions and contractions are naturally embedded [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 16. Mastery Check

- The learner successfully completes the Day 35 Milestone Tasks under un-speeded roleplay conditions, demonstrating V2 syntax stability, correct dative singular endings, and spontaneous repair deployment [Unit 40].

---

### DAY 35 STUDY TIME ALLOCATION (Total: \~4 Hours)

- **A. Daily German Core:** 20 minutes (Consolidating all Days 1–30 Core items) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **B. Main Curriculum Lesson:** 40 minutes (Dative syntax review, Wo? vs. Wohin? contrasts, direction imperative structures) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **C. Intensive Practice:** 2.5 hours (Mirror presentation, question-answer conversion drills, spelling and phone number dictations, mock testing).
- **D. Real-Life German:** 30 minutes (Completing a final self-assessment checklist and reviewing incorrect pronunciation entries).

---

# WEEK 5 REVIEW

### 1. What should now be recognized (Receptive Mastery)

- Identifying subtle acoustic differences between *im* (stationary) and *zum* (directional) in connected speech [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Group statements containing plural pronouns *wir* and *ihr* in native oral input [Day 30 core].
- The relative spatial prepositions *unter, über, hinter, neben, zwischen* in reading/listening contexts [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 2. What should now be actively produced (Active Mastery)

- Answering **Wo?** (Location) using dative contractions **im** (*im Büro*) and **am** (*am Tisch*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Answering **Wohin?** (Movement) using dative contractions **zum** (*zum Bahnhof*) and **zur** (*zur Arbeit*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Expressing accompaniment and transit using the dative-governing preposition **mit** (*mit dem Kollegen*, *mit der Kollegin*) [Day 30 core].
- Formulating formal pedestrian direction commands using imperatives and adverbs (*Gehen Sie geradeaus*, *Biegen Sie rechts ab*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Using dative pronouns **mir** and **Ihnen** with the verb **helfen** (*Können Sie mir helfen?*, *Ich helfe Ihnen*) [Day 9 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Declaring duration using **seit** with correct dative singular indefinite articles (*seit einem Tag*, *seit einer Woche*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 3. What should remain mainly receptive

- Full dative plural inflections (*den ... -n*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Dative pronoun *dir* and dative-governing verb *gefallen* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Active grammatical assembly of dative prepositions *nach, von, aus* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 4. Pronunciation Gains

- Intelligible production of the alveolar affricate **"Z" (/ts/)** in *zu*, *zum*, and *zur* [Unit 4].
- Consolidated articulation of the final consonant clusters in **"rechts"** (/çts/) and **"links"** (/ŋks/) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Consolidated use of terminal consonant devoicing (*Zug* pronounced as [tsu\:k] and *Geld* as [gɛlt]) [Unit 13].

### 5. Sentence-Building Gains

- Progressed from V2/Accusative templates to **dative singular article substitutions** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Developed the syntactic flexibility to contrast **Wo?** (stationary) and **Wohin?** (directional) in active conversation [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Automated **formal pedestrian imperatives** with correct separable splits [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 6. Practical Navigation Abilities

- Can navigate local German streets, asking pedestrians for directions politely, and decoding navigation instructions [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Can schedule medical and municipal appointments, stating duration of illness and booking specific time/day slots formally [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 7. Weak Areas to Diagnose (Diagnostic Guide)

- **Preposition Confusion:** Correct the learner if they use *in* for directed movement toward a landmark (e.g., saying *Ich gehe im Bahnhof* instead of *Ich gehe zum Bahnhof*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Pronoun Registers:** Ensure the learner does not pair formal registers with informal dative pronouns (e.g., saying *Können Sie dir helfen?* instead of *Können Sie mir/Ihnen helfen?*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **V2 Word-Order Drift:** Ensure that starting a sentence with a dative time phrase triggers correct subject-verb inversion (e.g., saying *Seit einem Tag ich bin krank* instead of *Seit einem Tag bin ich krank*) [Unit 23].

---

# WEEK 5 MASTERY CHECK

This formal evaluation uses **qualitative, functional competence criteria** to determine readiness for the final phase, completely bypassing arbitrary speed, percentage, or trial-count gates [Newton & Nation, 2020].

```
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                 WEEK 5 MASTERY CHECKLIST                                        |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  1. PHONOLOGICAL INTELLIGIBILITY (Receptive & Productive)                                       |
|     -> Produces distinct /ts/ (zu/zum/zur) and terminal consonant clusters in rechts/links.      |
|                                                                                                 |
|  2. CANONICAL V2 STABILITY WITH SPATIAL ADVERBS                                                 |
|     -> Keeps the conjugated verb in Position 2 when beginning with temporal or locative phrases. |
|                                                                                                 |
|  3. DATIVE SINGULAR ARTICLE & CONTRACTION AGREEMENT                                             |
|     -> Correctly inflects and contracts dative masculine/neuter (dem) and feminine (der) forms.  |
|                                                                                                 |
|  4. STATIC VS. DIRECTIONAL CASE SELECTION                                                       |
|     -> Consistently distinguishes stationary Wo? (im/am) from directed movement Wohin? (zum/zur).|
|                                                                                                 |
|  5. DOUBLE-STAGE TBLT INDEPENDENCE (Milestone Tasks)                                            |
|     -> Completes Task A (Lost in Berlin) and Task B (The Clinic Booking) cycles.                |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

```

### 1. Pronunciation Check

- **Target Sound Isolation:** The learner reads aloud the following three sentences, which contain the critical phonetic contrasts of Week 5 [Vaughan-Rees, 2002]:
  1. *Ge-hen Sie ge-ra-de-aus zum Bahn-hof und bie-gen Sie rechts ab.* (Evaluates formal imperatives, directional adverb *geradeaus*, contraction *zum*, and the final cluster /çts/ in *rechts*).
  2. *Ich bin seit ei-ner Wo-che beim Arzt im Bü-ro.* (Evaluates dative duration preposition *seit*, feminine dative article *einer*, dative contraction *beim*, and stationary location contraction *im*).
  3. *Kön-nen Sie mir hel-fen? Ich su-che mei-ne Kol-le-gin.* (Evaluates dative request frames, dative pronoun *mir*, dative-governing verb *helfen*, and feminine accusative *meine*).
- **Standard of Success:** Intelligible pronunciation. The learner must demonstrate clear articulatory effort to produce German-specific sibilants, affricates, and clusters, avoiding English "w" slurs or flat vowels.

### 2. Functional Communication Check (The Lost Traveler & The Clinic Appointment)

- **The Scenario:** The learner roleplays navigating a street map and calling a medical center in Germany. The examiner acts as the pedestrian and the clinic receptionist [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 40].
- **The Tasks:** The learner must complete two separate, highly realistic communicative cycles under natural, un-speeded conditions:
  - **Task A (Lost in Berlin):**
    1. Approach a pedestrian politely using formal apologies (*Entschuldigung*) [Day 4 core].
    2. Ask for directions to the supermarket or train station (*Wo ist der Bahnhof?*) [Day 8 core].
    3. Decode directional adverbs (*geradeaus, rechts, links*) and confirm the route using formal imperatives (*Gehen Sie...*, *Biegen Sie... ab*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  - **Task B (The Clinic Booking):**
    1. Initiate contact formally, state they are sick and the duration of illness using *seit* with correct dative singular indefinite articles (*seit zwei Tagen krank*) [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
    2. Negotiate and book an appointment slot using correct dative time prepositions (*am Dienstag*, *um zehn Uhr*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
    3. Bids farewell formally (*Auf Wiedersehen* or *Bis Dienstag!*) [Day 5 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Standard of Success:** Conversational success. Minor grammatical slips are recast implicitly [Long, 2015]. The learner meets the criteria if meaning is successfully negotiated, the verb remains consistently in Position 2, dative articles and contractions are applied correctly based on noun genders, and any rapid native input is managed using German repair formulas (*Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht*) without reverting to English.

---

*The complete Week 5 (Days 29–35) detailed study plan is finalized, audited, and officially locked under the designated design parameters. Stop. Do not proceed to Week 6.*


---

# WEEK 6 — DAYS 36–42


# WEEK 6 STUDY PLAN: A1 INTEGRATION, PRACTICAL INDEPENDENCE, AND EXAM READINESS

Week 6 represents the final consolidation, integration, and graduation phase of the A1 curriculum. At this mature beginner stage, the adult learner’s brain does not require new grammatical paradigms [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Instead, the instructional priority shifts entirely to **proceduralisation, automaticity, and communicative transfer**.

By applying the principles of **Transfer-Appropriate Processing (TAP)**, the learner integrates prior structural systems (V2 word order, Accusative cases, negation contrasts, modal brackets, and dative locational coordinates) into distinct, realistic communicative situations. Concurrently, the curriculum integrates focused **Goethe-Zertifikat A1 exam preparation** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], transforming explicit declarative knowledge into robust, functional automaticity.

---

## DAY-BY-DAY EXECUTABLE SPRINT (DAYS 36–42)

---

### DAY 36: Supermarket & Shop Transactions & Goethe Speaking Teil 1

#### 1. Main Objective

The learner will be able to execute a complete, natural cash purchase at a German supermarket using proper Accusative cases and tipping etiquette, and master **Goethe A1 Speaking Teil 1** (spelling names, reciting phone numbers, and stating personal details).

#### 2. Main Language Being Consolidated

- *Ich brauche/suche einen Kaffee / eine Tüte.* (Accusative objects) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Das macht... Euro.* (Numbers 0–100) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Zusammen oder getrennt? — Getrennt, bitte.* (Grocery routines) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Hier ist das Geld. Stimmt so!* (Tipping formula) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 3. Daily German Core Connection

Spaced retrieval and automaticity training of: **der Euro** [Day 10 core], **Bezahlen, bitte!** [Day 19 core], and **Stimmt so!**[Day 20 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Consolidating vowel length contrasts: the long tense closed 'e' in *geben* and *nehmen* vs. the short open 'e' in *weg* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Hear:* Listen to the teacher contrast the long, tense, closed vowel in *geben* (lips spread wide) with the short, open vowel in *weg*.
  2. *Imitate:* Mimic the wide, smiling posture of *geben* and the quick, relaxed drop of the jaw in *weg*.
  3. *Compare:* Record your voice and compare your vowel lengths to a native speaker.
  4. *Repeat:* Recite the phrase: *Geben Sie mir den Kaffee, bitte.* [Day 17 core].
  5. *Use:* Integrate this vowel distinction naturally into the supermarket simulation dialogue.

#### 5. Listening Practice

- **Bottom-Up Decoding:** Listen to five rapid cash register totals. Transcribe the prices (e.g., *fünfzehn Euro fünfzig*) and check if they exceed or fall below 20 Euro.
- **Top-Down Gist:** Listen to a brief conversation at a bakery. Identify whether the customer is buying bread (*das Brot*) or cake, and if they pay together or separately.

#### 6. Speaking Practice

- **Goethe Speaking Teil 1 Prep:** Practice reciting your customized self-introduction profile (Name, age, country of origin, residence, languages, profession). Practice spelling your last name and reciting a telephone number using German letter names and digits [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Reading Practice

- Scan a printed German supermarket flyer and receipt. Identify the prices of target nouns (*Brot, Kaffee, Wasser*) and highlight all masculine items requiring accusative matching.

#### 8. Writing Practice

- **Goethe Writing Teil 1 Prep:** Fill out an official municipal library registration form using your personal details (Name, Vorname, Adresse, PLZ, Telefonnummer), ensuring perfect orthography and capitalization.

#### 9. Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Sentence Expansion (Shopping).
- **Progression:** Progressively expand a simple shopping statement from a single word to a complex V2 sentence:
  - *Kaffee* (Noun) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich brauche Kaffee.* (S-V-O) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich brauche einen Kaffee.*(Accusative shift) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Heute brauche ich einen Kaffee.* (Temporal fronting) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Heute brauche ich einen Kaffee und ich möchte bezahlen.* (Conjunction) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 10. Retrieval/Recycling

- Retrieve Nominative definite articles (*der, die, das*) and indefinite articles (*ein, eine, ein*) from Week 3 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to correctly apply the accusative masculine shift (*den, einen*) during checkout.

#### 11. Practical Germany Task

- **The Grocery Checkout (TBLT):** The learner roleplays navigating a supermarket checkout line in Germany. They greet the cashier, request a shopping bag politely, decode the price total, pay with cash, and use proper tipping etiquette:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich brauche eine Tüte, bitte. — [Cashier scans and states total] — Getrennt, bitte. Hier sind zwanzig Euro. Stimmt so!" [Days 1, 10, 19, 20 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 12. Native-Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Cashier):* "Guten Tag. Das macht sechzehn Euro, bitte. Zusammen oder getrennt?"
- *Learner:* "Getrennt, bitte. Hier sind zwanzig Euro. Stimmt so! Danke." [Days 1, 19, 20 core]
- *Native (Cashier):* "Danke schön. Einen schönen Tag noch! Tschüss!"
- *Learner:* "Danke, gleichfalls! Tschüss!" [Day 2 core]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the cashier states the price too rapidly, immediately deploy the repair chunk: **"Wie bitte? Schreiben Sie das bitte auf?"** [Day 3, 9 core] to visually verify the total.

#### 14. Optional Interest-Driven German

- Browse a digital German supermarket catalogue online. List 5 items you would buy for dinner, writing them down with their matching definite articles and prices to build real-world lexical networks.

#### 15. Mastery Check

- The learner can introduce themselves, spell their name, state their phone number, and complete a simulated grocery checkout with correct accusative case markers.

---

### DAY 37: Public Transport Navigation & Goethe Listening Teil 1 & 2

#### 1. Main Objective

The learner will be able to navigate a German train station, decode transit announcements regarding platform numbers and delays, and master **Goethe A1 Listening Teil 1 & 2** (identifying specific times, platforms, and transit methods).

#### 2. Main Language Being Consolidated

- *Der Zug fährt um neun Uhr ab.* (Separable verb *abfahren*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Der Bus kommt am Bahnhof an.* (Separable verb *ankommen*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Heute fahre ich mit dem Zug zur Arbeit.* (Temporal fronting and dative transit) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Entschuldigung für die Verspätung!* (Transit apology) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 3. Daily German Core Connection

Spaced retrieval and automaticity training of: **der Bahnhof** [Day 8 core], **die Fahrkarte** [Day 24 core], and **Entschuldigung für die Verspätung** [Day 18 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Word stress and acoustic boundaries in compound nouns like *Fahrkartenautomat* and *Hauptbahnhof* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Hear:* Listen to the teacher pronounce the compound nouns, highlighting the primary stress on the first component (*FAHR-karten-automat*, *HAUPT-bahnhof*).
  2. *Imitate:* Practice clapping on the primary stressed syllables while keeping the rest of the word light.
  3. *Compare:* Compare your rhythm and stressed syllables to a native recording.
  4. *Repeat:* Recite the phrase: *Ich bin am Hauptbahnhof.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  5. *Use:* Apply this word stress smoothly during the transit counter simulation.

#### 5. Listening Practice

- **Goethe Listening Teil 1 & 2 Prep:** Listen to three simulated railway station announcements (with train station background noise) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Identify (1) the destination city, (2) the platform number (*Gleis*), and (3) the delay duration (*Verspätung*), marking them on a multiple-choice sheet.

#### 6. Speaking Practice

- **Transit Delay Update:** Practice calling your supervisor or classmate to report a transit delay: *Guten Tag. Ich fahre mit dem Zug. Aber der Zug ist heute zu spät. Entschuldigung für die Verspätung!* [Days 15, 18, 30 core].

#### 7. Reading Practice

- Scan a printed DB (Deutsche Bahn) train schedule matrix. Locate the arrival times, platform listings, and connection tracks for a regional commuter route.

#### 8. Writing Practice

- **Goethe Writing Teil 2 Prep (Brief/E-Mail):** Write a short, polite SMS (approx. 30 words) to your colleague: State that you are at the station, explain that your train is delayed, and tell them you will arrive at ten o'clock:
  - `Guten Tag Herr Schmidt. Ich bin am Bahnhof. Der Zug hat heute Verspätung. Ich komme um zehn Uhr an. Bis morgen!` [Days 8, 14, 18 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 9. Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Noun Substitution (Separable Verbs).
- **Progression:** Progressively substitute sentence elements while maintaining strict separable split syntax:
  - *Der Zug fährt ab.* (S-V-Prefix) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Der Zug nach Hamburg fährt ab.* (Qualifier) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Morgen fährt der Zug nach Hamburg ab.* (Temporal shift) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Morgen fährt der Zug nach Hamburg um neun Uhr ab.* (Precise time placement in Position 4) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 10. Retrieval/Recycling

- Retrieve the dative transit chunks *mit dem Bus*, *mit der U-Bahn*, and *mit dem Zug* from Week 2 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to combine them with today’s temporal-fronting V2 structures.

#### 11. Practical Germany Task

- **The Transit Query (TBLT):** The learner roleplays standing at a train station ticketing window [Day 8 core]. They greet the agent, present their ticket, ask when the train to Frankfurt departs, and confirm the track number:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich fahre mit dem Zug. Wann fährt der Zug nach Frankfurt ab? Und wo ist Gleis vier, bitte? Danke." [Days 1, 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 12. Native-Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Agent):* "Guten Tag. Der Zug nach Frankfurt fährt um zehn Uhr ab. Er hat heute leider fünfzehn Minuten Verspätung."
- *Learner:* "Danke schön. Ich fahre mit dem Zug. Hier ist meine Fahrkarte. Ist Gleis vier geöffnet?" [Day 11, 24 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Native (Agent):* "Ja, Gleis vier ist geöffnet. Gute Reise!" [Day 11 core]
- *Learner:* "Vielen Dank! Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If you do not catch the track number or delay time, immediately deploy: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Noch einmal, bitte."** [Day 1, 25 core] to prompt a slow, clear repetition.

#### 14. Optional Interest-Driven German

- Explore the real-time departure board of a major German train station (e.g., Munich or Hamburg) online. Locate three delayed trains, identify their platform numbers (*Gleis*), and write them down.

#### 15. Mastery Check

- The learner can accurately extract departure times and track numbers from spoken transit announcements, and write a correct, cohesive SMS reporting a travel delay.

---

### DAY 38: Pedestrian Directions, Spatial Landmarks & Goethe Speaking Teil 3

#### 1. Main Objective

The learner will be able to ask for and give pedestrian directions using landmarks and formal imperatives with separable splits, and master **Goethe A1 Speaking Teil 3** (formulating polite requests and responses using visual cue cards).

#### 2. Main Language Being Consolidated

- *Gehen Sie geradeaus.* (V1 imperative pattern) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Biegen Sie links ab.* (Separable split imperative) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Wo ist der Supermarkt? — Der Supermarkt ist dort.* (Location query) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *zur Post / zum Bahnhof* (Dative movement contractions) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 3. Daily German Core Connection

Spaced retrieval and automaticity training of: **hier/dort** [Day 6 core] and **Können Sie mir helfen?** [Day 9 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Crisply pronouncing the final consonant clusters in *rechts* (/çts/) and *links* (/ŋks/) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Hear:* Listen to the teacher pronounce *rechts* and *links*, focusing on the sharp, voiceless terminal clusters.
  2. *Imitate:* Smile on the "ich-laut" in *rechts*, followed by a sharp "ts" release; keep the "ng-ks" transition tight in *links*.
  3. *Compare:* Contrast your articulation of these endings with the teacher's model.
  4. *Repeat:* Recite the phrase: *Biegen Sie rechts ab. Biegen Sie links ab.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  5. *Use:* Apply these words naturally while giving pedestrian directions on a map layout.

#### 5. Listening Practice

- **Pedestrian Navigation Decoding:** Listen to a passerby giving directions to a lost traveler over city traffic noise. Trace their movement across a grid map and identify the target landmark they reach (*Post*, *Bahnhof*, or *Supermarkt*).

#### 6. Speaking Practice

- **Goethe Speaking Teil 3 Prep:** Work with a classmate using visual cue cards featuring icons (e.g., a cup of coffee, a key, a train). Formulate a polite request for each card: *Können Sie mir helfen? Wo ist der Kaffee, bitte?* or *Ich suche meinen Schlüssel. Haben Sie meinen Schlüssel?* and respond appropriately [Day 9, 26 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Reading Practice

- Scan a simple German city neighborhood map. Write down the street names and locate three local landmarks based on their dative markings (*beim Supermarkt*, *vor dem Bahnhof*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 8. Writing Practice

- **Guided Pedestrian Composition:** Write a simple, 4-step pedestrian instruction guide for a colleague trying to find the post office from your office building:
  1. `Gehen Sie geradeaus.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  2. `Biegen Sie links ab.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  3. `Gehen Sie zur Post.` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  4. `Die Post ist dort beim Bahnhof.` [Day 6, 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 9. Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Combining Structures (Request + Direction).
- **Progression:** Combine a polite request with pedestrian direction commands into a cohesive dialogue:
  - *Wo ist die Post?* (Query) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Entschuldigung, wo ist die Post?* (Politeness) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Entschuldigung, können Sie mir helfen? Wo ist die Post?* (Helper bracket) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Gehen Sie geradeaus und biegen Sie links ab. Die Post ist dort.* (Imperatives and location response) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 10. Retrieval/Recycling

- Retrieve the dative contractions *zum* (masculine/neuter) and *zur* (feminine) from Week 5 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to ensure correct case assignment when specifying direction destinations.

#### 11. Practical Germany Task

- **Lost in Town (TBLT):** The learner roleplays being lost in a German city [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. They approach a stranger, apologize, ask for directions to a specific landmark, decode the response, and confirm:
  - *Learner:* "Entschuldigung! Können Sie mir helfen? Wo ist der Bahnhof? — [Stranger gives directions] — Ah, geradeaus gehen und links abbiegen. Vielen Dank. Tschüss!" [Days 2, 4, 8, 9 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 12. Native-Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Pedestrian):* "Guten Tag. Ja, ich helfe Ihnen. Der Bahnhof ist nicht weit. Gehen Sie geradeaus. Biegen Sie rechts ab. Der Bahnhof ist dort." [Day 8 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Learner:* "Geradeaus und rechts abbiegen. Super, danke! Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the pedestrian uses unfamiliar landmark names, immediately deploy: **"Langsam, bitte! Ich verstehe nicht."**[Day 3, 25 core] to manage the speech velocity.

#### 14. Optional Interest-Driven German

- Explore a German tourism website (e.g., *visitBerlin*). Find a short, 3-sentence description of a pedestrian route, highlight the directional adverbs, and practice reading them aloud with correct word stress.

#### 15. Mastery Check

- The learner can ask for directions politely, understand pedestrian commands using *geradeaus/links/rechts*, and formulate formal V1 imperatives correctly.

---

### DAY 39: Medical Appointments, Clinic Bookings & Goethe Reading Teil 1 & 2

#### 1. Main Objective

The learner will be able to call a doctor's clinic to reschedule an appointment due to illness, state the duration of their illness using dative structures, and master **Goethe A1 Reading Teil 1 & 2** (scanning emails and public notices).

#### 2. Main Language Being Consolidated

- *Ich bin seit einem Tag / einer Woche krank.* (Dative duration) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Ich muss zum Arzt gehen.* (Obligation modal and dative direction) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Ich möchte den Termin verschieben.* (Polite request split-bracket) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Passt das am Dienstag um zehn Uhr?* (Temporal prepositions) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 3. Daily German Core Connection

Spaced retrieval and automaticity training of: **krank** [Day 21 core], **der Arzt** [Day 21 core], and **den Termin verschieben** [Day 22 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Vowel length and consonant clusters in *Arzt* and *Termin* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Hear:* Listen to the teacher pronounce the long open vowel and the crisp terminal cluster in *Arzt* (/a\:st/) and the long tense closed vowel in *Termin* (/i:/).
  2. *Imitate:* Open your mouth wide for *Arzt*, making sure the "rzt" is fully audible; tense your tongue and push it forward for *Termin*.
  3. *Compare:* Record your voice and compare your vowels to a native speaker.
  4. *Repeat:* Recite the phrase: *Ich habe einen Termin beim Arzt.* [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  5. *Use:* Apply these words smoothly during the clinic phone call simulation.

#### 5. Listening Practice

- **Appointment Log Transcription:** Listen to a clinic receptionist proposing calendar slots over the phone. Mark the available hours and days on a blank scheduling calendar.

#### 6. Speaking Practice

- **Clinic Rescheduling Roleplay:** Call your doctor's office, introduce yourself, state that you are sick, specify how long you have been sick using *seit*, and propose rescheduling your appointment to Tuesday at ten o'clock [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Reading Practice

- **Goethe Reading Teil 1 & 2 Prep:** Read a short notice posted on a clinic door regarding holiday hours and matching multiple-choice questions [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Identify whether the clinic is open (*geöffnet*) or closed (*geschlossen*) on specific days, selecting the correct statement.

#### 8. Writing Practice

- **Guided Appointment Email:** Write a 3-sentence email to your clinic coordinator confirming a rescheduled appointment:
  - `Guten Tag. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank.` [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
  - `Ich möchte den Termin verschieben.` [Day 22 core]
  - `Passt das am Dienstag um zehn Uhr? Danke!` [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 5]

#### 9. Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Questions & Answers (Clinic Scheduling).
- **Progression:** Formulate scheduling queries and responses, integrating dative time and duration modifiers:
  - *Wann ist der Termin?* (Query) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich möchte den Termin verschieben.* (Request) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Passt das am Dienstag?* (Proposal) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank. Passt das am Dienstag um zehn Uhr?* (Complete context with *seit zwei Tagen* as a frozen chunk) [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 10. Retrieval/Recycling

- Retrieve the temporal prepositions *am* (for days) and *um* (for hours) from Week 5 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to ensure grammatically correct schedule coordination.

#### 11. Practical Germany Task

- **The Clinic Booking (TBLT):** The learner roleplays calling a doctor's office in Germany to reschedule an appointment due to illness [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. They greet the receptionist, state their name, declare their illness and duration using *seit*, reschedule using modal verbs, and confirm:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank. Ich möchte den Termin verschieben, bitte. — [Receptionist proposes slot] — Dienstag um zehn Uhr? Ja, das passt gut. Danke!" [Days 1, 21, 22 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 5]

#### 12. Native-Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Receptionist):* "Dienstag um elf Uhr ist frei. Mit welchem Arzt haben Sie den Termin?"
- *Learner:* "Mit Herrn Dr. Wagner, bitte. Ich bin seit zwei Tagen krank." [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Native (Receptionist):* "Alles klar. Der Termin steht. Bis Dienstag!"
- *Learner:* "Vielen Dank. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the receptionist proposes an unavailable time, immediately deploy: **"Es tut mir leid, das passt nicht. Wann geht es?"** [Day 12, 25 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to request another option.

#### 14. Optional Interest-Driven German

- Explore a German online pharmacy portal (*Apotheke*). Scan their landing page to locate emergency services (*Notdienst*) and write down their opening hours.

#### 15. Mastery Check

- The learner can write a short scheduling email, state an illness duration using *seit* with correct dative singular indefinite articles, and negotiate a calendar slot formally.

---

### DAY 40: Post Office & Banking Transactions & Goethe Writing Teil 1

#### 1. Main Objective

The learner will be able to execute a mail transaction at a German post office, use singular possessives accurately in both Nominative and Accusative cases, and master **Goethe A1 Writing Teil 1** (filling out registration forms from a short text).

#### 2. Main Language Being Consolidated

- *Das ist mein Brief / mein Schlüssel.* (Nominative masculine possessive) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Ich suche meinen Schlüssel.* (Accusative masculine possessive shift) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Haben Sie Ihre Fahrkarte / Ihre E-Mail?* (Formal feminine possessives) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Das ist meine Adresse.* (Stable feminine possessive frame) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 3. Daily German Core Connection

Spaced retrieval and automaticity training of: **die E-Mail** [Day 15 core], **schicken** [Day 27 core], and **die Nachricht** [Day 27 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Long tense vowels in *Brief* [bri\:f] vs. the diphthong 'ei' in *mein/kein* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Hear:* Listen to the teacher contrast the long, tense closed 'i' in *Brief* with the wide open diphthong in *mein*.
  2. *Imitate:* Tense your jaw and place your tongue high and forward for *Brief*; drop your jaw and glide your tongue for *mein*.
  3. *Compare:* Compare your vowel tension with the teacher's model.
  4. *Repeat:* Recite the phrase: *Ich schicke meinen Brief.* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Day 27 core].
  5. *Use:* Apply this pronunciation smoothly during the post office simulation.

#### 5. Listening Practice

- **Form Filling Dictation:** Listen to a post office clerk spelling out names and stating addresses and numbers. Write down the transcribed letters and numbers onto your worksheet.

#### 6. Speaking Practice

- **Postal Ownership Drill:** Point to various envelopes and items on a counter and state ownership using Nominative and Accusative possessives: *Das ist mein Brief. Ich suche meinen Schlüssel.* or *Das ist meine Nachricht.* [Day 27 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Reading Practice

- **Goethe Writing Teil 1 Prep:** Read a short paragraph about a person named Carlos (specifying age, country of origin, target language, residence, postal code, and phone number). Scan the text to locate matching details and fill out an official post office registration form.

#### 8. Writing Practice

- **Form Filling Completion:** Fill in the 5 blank fields of the post office form based on the reading text, ensuring 100% accurate spelling and correct capitalization of nouns.

#### 9. Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Short Descriptions (Possessive Accusatives).
- **Progression:** Build descriptive statements about postal transactions, applying proper cases:
  - *Brief* (Noun) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Das ist mein Brief.* (Nominative copula) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich schicke meinen Brief.*(Accusative shift) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Jetzt schicke ich meinen Brief zur Post.* (Temporal V2 inversion and dative direction) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 10. Retrieval/Recycling

- Retrieve possessive determiners (*mein/meine*, *Ihr/Ihre*) from Week 4 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to correctly apply the masculine accusative shift (*meinen, Ihren*) during direct object placement.

#### 11. Practical Germany Task

- **The Post Office Transaction (TBLT):** The learner roleplays standing at a post office counter in Germany. They greet the clerk, state that they want to send a letter, provide their address and target country, and complete the purchase:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich möchte diesen Brief schicken. Das ist meine Adresse. Was kostet das, bitte? — [Clerk states price] — Hier ist das Geld. Danke!" [Days 1, 15, 27 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 12. Native-Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Clerk):* "Guten Tag. Möchten Sie diesen Brief abschicken? Ich brauche Ihre Adresse."
- *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ja, ich möchte den Brief schicken. Das ist meine Adresse." [Day 15, 27 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Native (Clerk):* "Alles klar. Das macht zwei Euro." [Day 10 core]
- *Learner:* "Hier sind zwei Euro. Vielen Dank! Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the clerk asks for a different document or uses complex vocabulary, deploy the repair: **"Wie bitte? Noch einmal, bitte."** [Day 1, 3 core].

#### 14. Optional Interest-Driven German

- Look up a real German postal code (*PLZ - Postleitzahl*) search engine online. Enter your favorite German landmark or city (e.g., *München* or *Köln*) and write down its 5-digit postal code.

#### 15. Mastery Check

- The learner can accurately extract data from a running text to fill in an official administrative form with 100% spelling precision.

---

### DAY 41: Workplace Problem-Solving & Goethe Writing Teil 2

#### 1. Main Objective

The learner will be able to report a workplace technical failure formally using correct modal brackets and negation contrasts, and master **Goethe A1 Writing Teil 2** (drafting a short, polite email of approximately 30 words).

#### 2. Main Language Being Consolidated

- *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.* (Verb negation) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Ich habe keinen Schlüssel.* (Noun negation) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Ich muss arbeiten.* (Obligation modal split-bracket) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Können Sie mir helfen?* (Request bracket) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 3. Daily German Core Connection

Spaced retrieval and automaticity training of: **der Chef** [Day 16 core], **der Drucker** [Day 26 core], and **Ich bin fertig**[Day 28 core].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Consolidating hushing "sch" (/ʃ/) sounds in *schicken* and *Nachricht* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Hear:* Listen to the teacher pronounce the crisp, rounded lips hushing sound in *schicken* and *Nachricht*.
  2. *Imitate:* Round and project your lips as if to say "shh", blowing air out sharply without voice.
  3. *Compare:* Compare your hushing sound with the teacher's model.
  4. *Repeat:* Recite the phrase: *Jetzt schicke ich die Nachricht.* [Day 27 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
  5. *Use:* Apply this sound smoothly during the workplace complaint simulation.

#### 5. Listening Practice

- **Workplace Problem Logging:** Listen to an office assistant explaining a technical problem. Write down (1) what device is broken (*funktioniert nicht*) and (2) what document they must send (*schicken*).

#### 6. Speaking Practice

- **Troubleshooting Reporting:** Point to a broken printer icon and report the problem to your supervisor formally: *Ich muss arbeiten, aber der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Können Sie mir helfen?* [Day 9, 26 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Reading Practice

- Scan a short printed office memo about out-of-order equipment. Locate who to contact and where to find the key for the alternative printing station.

#### 8. Writing Practice

- **Goethe Writing Teil 2 Prep (Brief/E-Mail):** Write a short, polite email (approx. 30 words) to your colleague Sarah: Explain that you are in the office, state that the printer is not working, and tell her you are taking a break:
  - `Hallo Sarah. Ich bin im Büro. Aber der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich muss meine Nachricht schicken. Ich mache eine Pause. Tschüss!` [Days 15, 26, 27, 28 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 9. Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Practical Dialogues (Workplace Reporting).
- **Progression:** Combine greetings, problems, obligations, requests, and status reports into a cohesive dialog:
  - *Hallo Sarah.* (Greeting) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Der Drucker funktioniert nicht.* (Problem) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich muss meine Nachricht schicken.* (Obligation) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Können Sie mir helfen?* (Request) \\(\rightarrow\\) *Ich mache jetzt eine Pause.* (Status) [Days 26, 27, 28 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 10. Retrieval/Recycling

- Retrieve the structural positioning rules for *nicht* (verb/adjective negation) vs. *kein* (noun negation) from Week 4 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md] to ensure accurate workplace error reports.

#### 11. Practical Germany Task

- **The Workplace Complaint (TBLT):** The learner roleplays experiencing an office equipment failure [Day 26 core]. They approach their supervisor formally, report that the printer is broken and they cannot send their document, and request help:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag, Herr Schmidt. Können Sie mir helfen? Der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich muss meine Nachricht schicken. Danke!" [Days 1, 9, 26, 27 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 12. Native-Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Supervisor):* "Guten Tag. Ist der Drucker kaputt? Ich kann Ihnen helfen."
- *Learner:* "Guten Tag, Herr Schmidt. Ja, der Drucker funktioniert nicht. Ich muss arbeiten. Vielen Dank!" [Days 1, 26 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]
- *Native (Supervisor):* "Kein Problem. Der Schlüssel ist hier. Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5, 13 core]
- *Learner:* "Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the supervisor suggests a complex workaround you do not comprehend, immediately deploy: **"Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht."** [Day 25 core] to request a simpler explanation.

#### 14. Optional Interest-Driven German

- Look up a simple German office sign or notice online. Decode the break room or printer station guidelines, and note down 3 rules that use negation [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 15. Mastery Check

- The learner can draft a coherent, polite 30-word written message containing a formal request, maintaining correct V2 word order and case structures.

---

### DAY 42: Comprehensive A1 Final Mock Simulation & Graduation

#### 1. Main Objective

The learner will be able to integrate all core A1 communicative competencies, grammatical structures, and sound patterns to successfully complete a full **Goethe-Zertifikat A1 Mock Exam** under relaxed, low-stakes conditions [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 2. Main Language Being Consolidated

- Complete structural and lexical synthesis from Weeks 1–5 (personal introductions, shopping, commuting, spatial locations, clinic bookings, post office, and workplace reports) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 3. Daily German Core Connection

Systematic spaced retrieval and consolidation of all **Days 1–30 Daily German Core** formulas to achieve conversational automaticity [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 4. Pronunciation Practice

- **Focus:** Mastering sentence stress, rhythm, and clear word boundaries across a continuous, multi-turn self-introduction [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Progression:**
  1. *Hear:* Listen to a model native self-introduction, focusing on the natural rising and falling pitch.
  2. *Imitate:* Practice delivering your profile, pausing only at clause boundaries.
  3. *Compare:* Contrast your speech flow with the native model.
  4. *Repeat:* Recite your entire profile aloud.
  5. *Use:* Present your profile smoothly during Speaking Mock Teil 1.

#### 5. Listening Practice

- **Mock Listening Simulation:** Complete a full Goethe A1 Listening Mock test (short dialogue decodings, railway announcements, and multiple-choice questions), managing your focus to catch specific times, prices, and tracks.

#### 6. Speaking Practice

- **Mock Speaking Simulation:** Complete a full Goethe A1 Speaking Mock test with your classmate/teacher (Teil 1: Profile spelling/numbers; Teil 2: Question-and-answer cards; Teil 3: Polite requests/visual cards) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 7. Reading Practice

- **Mock Reading Simulation:** Complete a full Goethe A1 Reading Mock test (scanning email schedules, short ads, and answering true/false comprehension questions).

#### 8. Writing Practice

- **Mock Writing Simulation:** Complete a full Goethe A1 Writing Mock test (Teil 1: Filling out form fields based on a text; Teil 2: Drafting a polite email of approx. 30 words in response to a prompt).

#### 9. Sentence-Building Exercise

- **Task:** Multi-Turn Dialogue (A1 Capstone).
- **Progression:** Construct a complete, realistic, multi-turn dialogue with your partner simulating arriving in Germany and coordinating your move-in:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich fahre heute mit dem Bus. Wo ist Gleis vier, bitte? — [Partner responds] — Danke! Der Drucker funktioniert nicht, ich habe keinen Schlüssel. Ist der Schlüssel hier? — [Partner responds] — Ah, danke. Jetzt bin ich fertig. Ich mache eine Pause. Tschüss!" [Days 2, 8, 26, 28, 29 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 10. Retrieval/Recycling

- Systematic retrieval and integration of all core structures, case endings, and prepositions from Weeks 1–5 [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

#### 11. Practical Germany Task

- **The Move-In Day (TBLT capstone):** The learner roleplays arriving at their German apartment building. They greet the manager, ask where their key is, report that they have no time because they must catch a bus, and confirm their address:
  - *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ich bin John. Ich habe heute keine Zeit. Ich suche meinen Schlüssel. Haben Sie meinen Schlüssel? — [Manager responds] — Das ist meine Adresse. Danke! Tschüss!" [Days 1, 2, 22, 24 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]

#### 12. Native-Response/Interaction Practice

- *Native (Manager):* "Guten Tag. Sind Sie Herr John? Hier ist Ihr Schlüssel. Wie fährst du heute?"
- *Learner:* "Guten Tag. Ja, ich bin John. Vielen Dank. Heute fahre ich mit dem Bus. Tschüss!" [Days 1, 2 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md, Unit 6]
- *Native (Manager):* "Gute Fahrt! Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]
- *Learner:* "Auf Wiedersehen!" [Day 5 core]

#### 13. Communication Repair

- If the manager asks an unexpected question, immediately deploy: **"Wie bitte? Langsam, bitte!"** [Day 1, 3 core] to regain control of the interaction in German.

#### 14. Optional Interest-Driven German

- Write a short, 3-sentence entry in your German study journal about your language journey. List three milestones you are proud of, maintaining correct capitalization and V2 structures.

#### 15. Mastery Check

- The learner successfully completes all four parts of the Goethe A1 Mock Exam and the capstone roleplay, proving functional communicative readiness and independent error-recovery capabilities [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

# WEEK 6 REVIEW

### 1. Consolidated Knowledge

- **Syntactic Flexibility:** The learner can place verbs consistently in Position 2 across all declarative statements, questions, and temporal inversions (*Morgen fahre ich...*), while applying verb-first structures for formal imperatives (*Gehen Sie...*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Case Boundary Accuracy:** The learner can systematically apply the masculine accusative shift (*den, einen, keinen, meinen, Ihren*) after transitive verbs [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md], while maintaining static locative dative prepositions (*im, am*) and destination dative markers (*zum, zur*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **Contrastive Negation:** The learner can differentiate between noun negation (*kein*) and verb/adjective negation (*nicht*) with correct word-order placement [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 2. Active Abilities

- Can conduct fluent formal supermarket checkout transactions, handle cash payments, and execute German tipping etiquette [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Can request pedestrian directions and navigate using formal V1 imperatives and directional adverbs [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Can coordinate, reschedule, and book clinic appointments formally, stating illness duration using dative constructions [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Can report technical and logistical problems in a workplace, utilizing correct modal brackets and negation rules [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 3. Receptive Abilities

- Can understand and parse spoken transit platform numbers, arrival times, and delayed announcements over background noise [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Can identify group statements using plural pronouns *wir* and *ihr* in connected speech [Day 30 core].
- Can scan notices and emails for factual details (opening hours, contact details, schedules) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 4. Pronunciation Progress

- Crisply pronounces tricky German consonant clusters (/çts/ in *rechts*, /ŋks/ in *links*, /ʁtst/ in *Arzt*) [Day 21 core, a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Consistently applies terminal consonant devoicing (*Zug* pronounced with terminal 'k', *Geld* with terminal 't') [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Automates the vocalic glottal stop (*Knacklaut*) before vowel-initial words, avoiding slurred boundaries [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 5. Sentence-Building Progress

- Can expand sentences from single nouns to complex coordinated clauses with perfect V2 syntax [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- Can substitute tenses and cases, and correctly split separable verb prefixes at the absolute end of clauses [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 6. Practical Germany Abilities

- Can function independently in daily transit, shopping, directions, and health sectors in Germany, utilizing portable repair formulas to manage native speech velocity [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### 7. Remaining Weaknesses (Diagnostic Guide)

- **Preposition Case Drift:** Watch for learners reverting to accusative for static locations (e.g., *Ich bin in das Büro*instead of *im Büro*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
- **V1/V2 Inversion Errors:** Ensure the subject pronoun inverts to Position 3 when a sentence starts with a time adverb (e.g., *Heute ich gehe* instead of *Heute gehe ich*) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

---

# FINAL A1 GRADUATION CHECK

To graduate from A1, the learner must independently demonstrate the following functional capabilities under qualitative assessment conditions [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:

```
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|                                     A1 GRADUATION MILESTONES                                    |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|  1. PHONOLOGICAL INTELLIGIBILITY                                                                |
|     -> Distinctly pronounces u-umlauts, hushing consonants, and terminal devoiced stops.        |
|                                                                                                 |
|  2. COMMUNICATIVE AURAL DECODING                                                                |
|     -> Accurately extracts tracks, times, prices, and locations from transit announcements.     |
|                                                                                                 |
|  3. VERB-SECOND SYNTACTIC STABILITY                                                             |
|     -> Locks finite verbs in Position 2 across all declarative and inverted sentences.          |
|                                                                                                 |
|  4. STRUCTURAL SPLIT-BRACKET PRECISION                                                          |
|     -> Places infinitives (modals) and prefixes (separables) at the absolute end of clauses.     |
|                                                                                                 |
|  5. CASE REPRESENTATION ACCURACY                                                                |
|     -> Correctly inflects masculine accusatives (den/einen) and static/directional dative cases.|
|                                                                                                 |
|  6. STRATEGIC REPAIR AUTONOMY                                                                   |
|     -> Deploys "Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht" to slow down rapid native speech.          |
|                                                                                                 |
|  7. FORM-FILLING AND TEXT COMPOSITION                                                           |
|     -> Fills out administrative forms and writes cohesive 30-word messages with correct nouns.  |
+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

```

### 1. Pronunciation/Intelligibility

- *Evaluation:* Read aloud the Capstone Introduction: *Heute fahre ich mit dem Zug zur Arbeit. Ich möchte einen Kaffee trinken, aber ich habe heute keine Zeit. Mein Kollege ist krank.*
- *Standard:* Intelligible and clear. Correctly handles terminal devoicing, rounded vowels, and consonant clusters.

### 2. Listening

- *Evaluation:* Audit three train platform and flight delay announcements.
- *Standard:* Extracts the exact departure hours, track numbers, and delay updates without guessing.

### 3. Speaking

- *Evaluation:* Spontaneously answer W-Questions and ask visual request cards (e.g., *Können Sie mir helfen? Wo ist der Schlüssel?*).
- *Standard:* Forms grammatically correct, polite requests using modal and case inflections without long pauses.

### 4. Reading

- *Evaluation:* Read a 5-line business message and matching public notices.
- *Standard:* Matches times, tasks, and locations with 100% informational precision.

### 5. Writing

- *Evaluation:* Write a short, 30-word email asking a colleague to reschedule a meeting due to a broken device.
- *Standard:* Correct spelling, noun capitalization, V2 structure, and modal bracket placement.

### 6. Interaction/Repair

- *Evaluation:* Spontaneous administrative checkpoint simulation with unexpected questions from the clerk.
- *Standard:* Learner resolves the task and manages rapid or unclear input using repair formulas (*Es tut mir leid, ich verstehe nicht. Langsam, bitte!*), keeping the conversation in German.

### 7. Practical Germany Communication

- *Evaluation:* Supermarket checkout, transit booking, and pedestrian street navigation simulations.
- *Standard:* Successfully negotiates all transactions, decodes pricing and directions, and maintains polite registers.

### 8. Basic Workplace Communication

- *Evaluation:* Reporting a technical device failure to a supervisor and announcing break status.
- *Standard:* Correctly pairs *Drucker* with negation and modal rules (*Ich muss meine Nachricht schicken, aber der Drucker funktioniert nicht*).

### 9. Goethe A1 Readiness

- *Evaluation:* Completion of the Day 42 Mock Exam parts.
- *Standard:* Meets or exceeds all CEFR A1 communicative "can-do" parameters, ready to sit the official exam with high confidence.

---

# FINAL A1 STATUS

**A1 GRADUATION COMPLETE & LOCKED**

The learner has successfully completed the 6-week intensive A1 German syllabus [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### Real-World "Can-Do" Competence:

The learner can confidently enter German-speaking environments and perform essential survival, transit, shopping, direction-following, and booking tasks independently [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. They can introduce themselves, state their origin, residence, and profession, spell details, and report basic workplace problems [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]. Most importantly, they possess robust **repair autonomy**—they can confidently manage conversational breakdowns and slow down rapid native speech using strategic German repair formulas [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

### Transition to A2 (Post-Graduation Focus):

The following complex structures and features are officially handed over and postponed to the **A2 syllabus** [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md]:

1. **Dative Plural Inflections (*****den ... -n*****):** Translating the plural dative case systematically (which was kept as frozen, unanalyzed chunks like *seit zwei Tagen* in A1) [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
2. **Informal Dative Pronoun (*****dir*****):** Expanding dative pronoun mappings beyond *mir* and formal *Ihnen* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
3. **Active Assembly of Prepositions *****nach*****, *****von*****, *****aus*****:** Moving beyond receptive and chunk uses to active case-building [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
4. **Dative Verb *****gefallen*****:** Mastering reversed thematic subject-object roles [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
5. **Relative Spatial Prepositions:** Actively applying *unter, über, hinter, neben, zwischen* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
6. **Paired Positional Verb Contrasts:** Rule-based lexical calculation of *stehen/stellen* and *liegen/legen* [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].
7. **Sustained Paragraph Writing and Narrative Past Tense (*****Präteritum*****):** Moving beyond short, polite 30-word transactional messages to cohesive past narratives [a1-master-curriculum-v2.md].

*STOP. The A1 Master Syllabus is complete and locked. Do not proceed to generate A2 material.*
